Endoscopy for Modular Hecke Categories
Colton Sandvik

Abstract
. Generalizing the theory of parity sheaves on complex algebraic stacks due to Juteau-Mautner-Williamson, we develop a theory of twisted equivariant parity sheaves. We use this formalism to construct a modular incarnation of Lusztig and Yun’s monodromic Hecke category. We then give two applications: (1) a modular categorification of the monodromic Hecke algebra, and (2) a monoidal equivalence between the monodromic Hecke category of parity sheaves and the ordinary Hecke category of parity sheaves on the endoscopic group.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Motivation

Endoscopic patterns in representation theory largely date back to Lusztig’s classification of irreducible complex representations of finite reductive groups
[
Lus84
]
.
Informally, Lusztig showed one can reduce the study of all irreducible complex representations to just unipotent representations of the endoscopic group.
In recent years, related results have shown up throughout geometric representation theory.
These results have largely taken the form of an equivalence between a monodromic Hecke category and a Hecke category corresponding to the endoscopic group.

1.1.1.
Monodromic Hecke Categories

Let
G
be a connected reductive group. Let
B
be a Borel subgroup of
G
which has maximal torus
T
and unipotent radical
U
. Let
\mathbbm{k}
be a field of characteristic
p\geq 0
.
The
Hecke category
,
D_{\cons}(B\backslash G/B,\mathbbm{k})
, is defined to be the derived category of constructible sheaves on
B\backslash G/B
.
It is a monoidal category under the convolution product. The Hecke category is well-studied in geometric representation theory.
The semisimple complexes in
D_{\cons}(B\backslash G/B,\mathbb{C})
categorify the Hecke algebra and the perverse sheaves in
D_{\cons}(B\backslash G/B,\mathbb{C})
can be identified with category

\mathcal{O}
for the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
of
G
.

We will study twisted equivariant versions of
D_{\cons}(B\backslash G/B,\mathbbm{k})
where we twist the left and right equivariance constraints by rank 1 multiplicative local systems.
The set of all such local systems is denoted by
\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
.
For
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
, one can consider
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\coloneq D_{\cons}(T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}U\backslash G/U/_{\mathcal{L}}T,\mathbbm{k})
, the
(T\times T,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{-1})
-equivariant derived category of constructible sheaves on
U\backslash G/U
with coefficients in
\mathbbm{k}
.
Following Lusztig and Yun, we call this category the
monodromic Hecke category

[
LY20
]
.
The monodromic Hecke categories also admit a convolution bifunctor; although, it does not endow
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
with a monoidal structure unless
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
.
In the case where
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{T}
, we recover the non-monodromic Hecke category
{}_{\underline{\mathbbm{k}}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\underline{\mathbbm{k}}}(\mathbbm{k})=D_{\cons}(B\backslash G/B,\mathbbm{k})
.

1.1.2.
Endoscopic Equivalences

If
G
is defined over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
and
\mathbbm{k}=\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
where
p\neq\ell
, one can consider a mixed incarnation of the Hecke category
D_{\cons}^{m}(B\backslash G/B,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
.
Similarly, there is a mixed incarnation of the monodromic Hecke category
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{m}(\mathbbm{k})
.
The (mixed) monodromic Hecke category decomposes into a direct sum of full subcategories called
blocks
.
We denote
{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{m,\circ}(\mathbbm{k})
the block containing the monoidal unit. This block is called the
neutral block
.

Associated to
\mathcal{L}
, we can define a root system
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}
. This gives rise to the
endoscopic group

H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
which is a reductive group over

\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}

with maximal torus
T
and root system
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}
.
The choice of Borel
B
gives a Borel
B_{\mathcal{L}}
of
H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
The main result of
[
LY20
]
gives an equivalence of monoidal categories

(1.1.1)

D_{\cons}^{m}(B_{\mathcal{L}}\backslash H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})\cong{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{m,\circ}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}).

This equivalence is referred to as the endoscopic-monodromic equivalence.
The equivalence of
[
LY20
]
can also be extended to the non-neutral blocks using 2-categories.
In
[
Li20
]
, the equivalence of
[
LY20
]
was extended to the case of loop groups; albeit, without the extension to non-neutral blocks.

The main obstacle in defining a version of (
1.1.1
) for positive characteristic coefficients is that the categories of mixed sheaves are no longer defined.
Nonetheless, in recent years there have been a few modular incarnations of the endoscopic-monodromic equivalence obtained by replacing mixed biequivariant sheaves with
non-mixed free-monodromic sheaves
[
Gou21
,
Ete25
]
.
In this paper, we will prove another modular version of the endoscopic-monodromic equivalence, but on the biequivariant side.

Motivated by work of Achar and Riche, we will replace the mixed categories appearing in (
1.1.1
) with categories of parity sheaves
[
AR16
]
.
The category of parity sheaves
\Par(B\backslash G/B,\mathbbm{k})
on
B\backslash G/B
has a rich history of its own. It provides a categorification of the Hecke algebra giving rise to the

p
-canonical bases and
p
-Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials.
While there is no significant obstruction in defining parity sheaves contained in
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
, the original formulation of
[
JMW14
]
is not sufficiently
general to allow for them.

1.1.3.
Parity Sheaves

Following
[
JMW14
]
, we extend the theory of parity sheaves to incorporate twisted equivariance.
More precisely, let
H
be a complex algebraic group,
X
be an algebraic
H
-stack over
\mathbb{C}
, and
\mathcal{L}
a multiplicative rank one local system on
H
.
Under suitable conditions on
D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
, we define an additive category of parity sheaves
\Par(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
.
In the case of
\mathcal{L}=\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{G}
, we recover the usual theory of parity sheaves on
H\backslash X
.

The theory of twisted equivariant parity sheaves shares many similarities with the usual theory of parity sheaves. In particular, parity extensions are unique and there
are variants of the existence criterion of
[
JMW14
]
.
We expect that twisted equivariant parity sheaves can find themselves useful in other areas of geometric representation theory.
For example, one can discuss twisted equivariant parity sheaves on toric varieties and affine Grassmannians.

We will apply these constructions to the monodromic Hecke category to construct categories of parity sheaves
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\coloneq\Par(T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}U\backslash G/U/_{\mathcal{L}}T,\mathbbm{k})
.
We will show that both all parity extensions exist and that convolution preserves parity sheaves.
The categories
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
also admit a block decomposition inherited from the derived category of sheaves.
We will write
{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathbbm{k})
for the block containing the monoidal unit.

1.2.
Main Results

We can now state a preliminary version of the endoscopic-monodromic equivalence which serves as a modular analogue of
[
LY20
]
.

Theorem 1.2.1

(See Theorem
5.3.1
for more precise version)
.

There is a monoidal equivalence of categories

\Par(B_{\mathcal{L}}\backslash H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})\cong{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathbbm{k}).

In the precise version of the above theorem, we include numerous generalizations. We will briefly mention them here along with some related history.

(1)

We will replace
G
by a Kac-Moody group of either finite or affine type. In the characteristic 0 coefficient setting, the endoscopic equivalence of
[
LY20
]

had already been extended
to loop groups in Li’s thesis
[
Li20
]
.

(2)

Our equivalence will also handle the non-neutral blocks when
G
is of finite. This was already done in
[
LY20
, §10]
for characteristic 0 coefficients.

(3)

Our allowable coefficient rings will be enlarged to most noetherian domains with only minor constraints to ensure that extension-of-scalars is well-behaved.

(4)

Our equivalence also includes a version where left (resp. right) equivariance is replaced by left (resp. right) topological monodromy.

1.3.
Connections and Future Work

1.3.1.
Diagrammatic Hecke Categories

For unipotent monodromy, there is a diagrammatic incarnation of the Hecke category defined in
[
EW16
]
.
Riche and Williamson proved that diagrammatic Hecke category coincides with the geometric Hecke category of parity sheaves when the category of parity sheaves is
well-defined
[
RW18
]
.
In a subsequent paper, we will give a diagrammatic description of the monodromic Hecke categories of parity sheaves.
As in
[
EW16
]
, the diagrammatic category is defined in a much larger generality than the geometric one.
In particular, we can make sense of the monodromic Hecke category for arbitrary Coxeter groups and realizations.

A version of the endoscopic-monodromic equivalence holds for these diagrammatic categories.
This should provide a purely Coxeter-theoretic proof of the endoscopic-monodromic equivalence which avoids some technical complications encountered in §
4
.

1.3.2.
Free-Monodromic Hecke Categories

Gouttard has proven a variant of the endoscopic-monodromic equivalence for free-monodromic tilting sheaves on
U\backslash G/U
rather than biequivariant sheaves on
U\backslash G/U

[
Gou21
]
.
Similar equivalences have also been produced in more recent work of Eteve
[
Ete25
]
and Dhillon–Li–Yun–Zhu
[
DLYZ25
]
.
One can ask whether this equivalence can be lifted to mixed free-monodromic tilting sheaves. Some work is needed to define what free-monodromic mixed sheaves should mean on
U\backslash G/U
.
In particular, the parity constraints are not satisfied, so the construction of
[
AR16
]
does not work directly.
Nonetheless, Achar, Makisumi, Riche, and Williamson have defined a category of mixed free-monodromic tilting sheaves with unipotent monodromy using almost purely
categorical constructions
[
AMRW17
]
.
In a subsequent paper, we will give a variant of the monodromic-endoscopic equivalence for these mixed free-monodromic tilting sheaves.
In fact, we will show that such an equivalence follows from the biequivariant version presented in this paper using tools from higher algebra.
A benefit of working with the free-monodromic setting rather than the biequivariant setting is that we will be able to produce a degrading functor from the mixed
category to the non-mixed category.
We expect that this will allow for some explicit computations of free-monodromic character sheaves using
p
-Kazhdan-Lusztig combinatorics (c.f.,
[
Ete24
]
).

1.4.
Notations and Conventions

Throughout the entirety of the paper, our derived categories of sheaves will be triangulated.
Despite this, Appendix
A
, which provides the foundations of the sheaf theory for twisted equivariant sheaves, is written using
\infty
-categories.
This is somewhat crucial in order to make sense of twisted equivariant sheaves with respect to any multiplicative local system.
For example, in Gouttard’s thesis
[
Gou21
]
, a necessary restriction is made to work with only multiplicative local systems arising from finite central isogenies.
By constructing twisted equivariant sheaves via categorical coinvariants, we are able to side-step this issue.

By default,
\mathbbm{k}
will always denote a noetherian domain of finite global dimension.

1.4.1.
Geometry and Sheaves

The six-functor formalism of constructible sheaves on complex algebraic stacks is not well-detailed in literature, and even less so is the six-functor formalism for
twisted equivariant sheaves on stacks.
As a result, we have included Appendix
A
which develops these ideas. We will review its contents now.

Let
X
be an algebraic stack over
\mathbb{C}
of finite type. Let
H
be a complex algebraic group which acts on
X
. The set of all multiplicative locally free rank one local
systems on
H
is denoted by
\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k})
.
1
1

1

Some references such as
[
LY20
,
Gai20
]
call these rank one character sheaves on
H
. We prefer to avoid this
terminology since we will consider
H\neq T
.

Let
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k})
.
We will write
D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
for the
(H,\mathcal{L})
-equivariant bounded derived category of constructible sheaves on
X
with coefficients in
\mathbbm{k}
.
As a matter of convention, we will also write
D_{\cons}(X/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})
for
D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{-1}}X,\mathbbm{k})
. This notation will be particularly useful
when we consider equivariance with respect to multiple commuting groups.

If
\varphi:H^{\prime}\to H
is a morphism of algebraic groups and
f:X^{\prime}\to X
is a morphism of algebraic stacks such that
f
is equivariant with respect to
\varphi
, we
have a pair of adjoint functors

f^{*}:D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\rightleftarrows D_{\cons}(H^{\prime}\backslash_{\varphi^{*}\mathcal{L}}X^{\prime},\mathbbm{k}):f_{*}

There are also
!
-versions of the above functors.

If
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k})
, the tensor product of twisted equivariant sheaves gives a bifunctor

(-)\otimes^{L}(-):D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\otimes D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X,\mathbbm{k}).

Similarly, the inner Hom between sheaves gives a bifunctor

\textbf{R}\mathcal{H}om(-,-):D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})^{\textnormal{op}}\otimes D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{-1}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X,\mathbbm{k}).

The six-functors for sheaves are related in the usual sense by the Verdier duality functor

\mathbb{D}:D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})^{\textnormal{op}}\to D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{-1}}X,\mathbbm{k}).

If
\mathbbm{k}\to\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}
is a ring homomorphism, there is an extension-of-scalars functor, which we denote by

\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(-):D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(\mathcal{L})}X,\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}).

1.4.2.
Fixed Stratifications

If
\{X_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
is an
H
-equivariant stratification of
X
, we will write
D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
for the full subcategory of

D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
of sheaves constructible with respect to
\Lambda
.
For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
, there is a subcategory of “local systems”, denoted
\Loc_{\textnormal{f}}(X_{\lambda}/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of sheaves
\mathcal{K}\in D_{(X_{\lambda})}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X_{\lambda},\mathbbm{k})
such that
\For_{H}\mathcal{K}
is a local system on
X_{\lambda}
of finite type.

1.4.3.
Kac-Moody Groups

We will first review some preliminaries on Kac-Moody groups and their associated flag varieties.
Let
A
be a generalized Cartan matrix. From
A
, we can associate a Kac-Moody root datum
(S,\mathbf{X},\{\alpha_{s}\}_{s\in S},\{\alpha_{s}^{\vee}\}_{s\in S})
.
Let
W
denote the Weyl group of this root datum. The simple reflections in
W
are in bijection with
S
, and we abuse notation and use elements of
S
to denote the
simple reflections.
The root datum then gives rise to a realization
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}=(V,\{\alpha_{s}\}_{s\in S},\{\alpha_{s}^{\vee}\}_{s\in S})
of
W
as follows.
Set
V=\mathbbm{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbf{X},\mathbb{Z})
. For
s\in S
, we set
\alpha_{s}
(resp.
\alpha_{s}^{\vee}
) to be the image of the corresponding simple root (resp. simple
coroot) in
V^{*}
(resp.
V
).
All geometric realizations of a generalized Cartan matrix are automatically balanced, but are not necessarily Demazure surjective.
We can fix this as follows. Suppose
\alpha_{s}:\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbf{X},\mathbb{Z})\to\mathbb{Z}
and
\alpha_{s}^{\vee}:\mathbf{X}\to\mathbb{Z}
are surjective, we can then set
\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}=\mathbb{Z}
.
Otherwise, we set
\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}=\mathbb{Z}[\frac{1}{2}]
. It is a standard fact that
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}
satisfies Demazure surjectivity provided there exists a ring homomorphism
\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\to\mathbbm{k}
.
Note that if
\mathbb{Z}=\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}
, then this is automatically satisfied.

One can associate to
A
an integral Kac-Moody group
G_{\mathbb{Z}}
with a Borel subgroup
B_{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Let
U_{\mathbb{Z}}
denote the pro-unipotent radical of
B_{\mathbb{Z}}
and let
T_{\mathbb{Z}}
denote the canonical maximal torus whose group of characters can be canonically identified with
\mathbf{X}
.
Let
G,B,U
, and
T
denote the base change to
\mathbb{C}
of
G_{\mathbb{Z}}
,
B_{\mathbb{Z}}
,
U_{\mathbb{Z}}
, and
T_{\mathbb{Z}}
, respectively. Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}=U\backslash G
denote the enhanced flag ind-variety.
The enhanced flag variety admits a Bruhat decomposition

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}=\bigsqcup_{w\in W}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w},

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}
is the
B
-orbit of a lift of
w\in W
. Note that each Bruhat orbit is isomorphic to a product of a torus with an affine space
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}\cong T\times\mathbb{A}^{\ell(w)}.

The
B
-orbits give a stratification of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}
. The closures of the strata are given by the Bruhat order,

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\leq w}=\overline{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}}=\bigsqcup_{x\leq w}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{x}.

Let
J\subset S
of finite type. We denote by
W_{J}
the finite subgroup of
W
generated by
s\in J
and by
W^{J}
the subset of
W
consisting of elements
w
which
are minimal in the coset
wW_{J}
.
For each
w\in W
, we write
\overline{w}
for the element of
W^{J}
such that
w\in\overline{w}W_{J}
.
Since
W_{J}
is finite, it has the longest element
w_{0}^{J}\in W_{J}
. To
J
, we can also define a parabolic subgroup
P_{J,\mathbb{Z}}
of
G_{\mathbb{Z}}
with pro-unipotent radical

U^{J}_{\mathbb{Z}}
and Levi subgroup
L_{J,\mathbb{Z}}
.
Let
P_{J},U^{J},L_{J}
denote the base change to
\mathbb{C}
of
P_{J,\mathbb{Z}}
,
U^{J}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, and
L_{J,\mathbb{Z}}
, respectively.
Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{J}=U^{J}\backslash G
denote the partial enhanced flag ind-variety.
The partial flag ind-variety has a parabolic Bruhat decomposition

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{J}=\bigsqcup_{\overline{w}\in W^{J}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{J}_{\overline{w}}

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\overline{w}}^{J}\cong\mathbb{A}^{\ell(\overline{w})}\times L_{J}
.
The closures of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\overline{w}}^{J}
are denoted by
\mathcal{F}\ell_{\leq w}^{J}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\leq w}^{J}
, respectively. As in the non-parabolic case, they are a union of
Bruhat strata indexed by elements in
W^{J}
below
\overline{w}
in the restricted Bruhat order.
We will write
j_{\overline{w}}^{J}
for the embedding of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\overline{w}}^{J}
into
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{J}
.

The most prominent example of a parabolic subgroup we will encounter is the special case of
J=\{s\}
for some
s\in S
.
In this case, we will replace the superscript
J
by
s
. In particular, we will write
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{s}=\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{\{s\}}
and likewise
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{s}=\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{\{s\}}
.

1.5.
Acknowledgements

The author thanks Pramod Achar for his continuous support and careful reading of earlier drafts of this paper.
The author also thanks Ana Bălibanu, Gurbir Dhillon, Alberto San Miguel Malaney, and Simon Riche for useful comments and discussions that influenced this project.

2.
Twisted Equivariant Parity Sheaves

2.1.
Definitions and Uniqueness

In this section, we require that
\mathbbm{k}
be a noetherian complete local ring or a field.
Let
X
be an (ind)-algebraic stack of finite type.
Let
H
be a connected algebraic group acting on
X
and
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k})
.
Let
\{X_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
be an
H
-stable stratification of
X
. We write
j_{\lambda}:X_{\lambda}\hookrightarrow X
for the inclusion maps.
For each
\lambda
, denote by
\Loc_{\textnormal{ff}}(H\backslash X_{\lambda},\mathbbm{k})
the full subcategory of
D_{(X_{\lambda})}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X_{\lambda},\mathbbm{k})

consisting of sheaves
\mathcal{F}
such that
\For_{H}\mathcal{F}
is a locally free local system of finite type on
X_{\lambda}
.

In order to simplify notation, for a
\mathbbm{k}
-linear triangulated category
\mathcal{C}
, objects
c,d\in\mathcal{C}
, and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we will write

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}^{n}(c,d)\coloneq\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(c,d[n]).

Similarly, we will write

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}(c,d)=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}^{n}(c,d)

viewed as a graded
\mathbbm{k}
-module.

There is a series of conditions on
D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
which ensures that parity sheaves are sensible to define.
These conditions are called the
parity conditions
. For all
\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\in\Loc_{\textnormal{ff}}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
, we require that

(2.1.1)

\Hom_{D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})}^{n}(\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime})=\begin{cases}0&\text{for }n\text{ odd, and}\\
\text{a free }\mathbbm{k}\text{-module}&\text{for }n\text{ even}.\end{cases}

Throughout, we will assume that
D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
satisfies the parity conditions.

Definition 2.1.1
.

Let
?\in\{*,!\}
. Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
.

(1)

\mathcal{F}
is
?
-
even
(resp.
?
-
odd
) if, for all
\lambda\in\Lambda
, and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
,

H^{n}(j_{\lambda}^{?}\mathcal{F})=0
when
n
is odd, and
H^{n}(j_{\lambda}^{?}\mathcal{F})
is a local system (i.e., viewed as a non-equivariant sheaf on
X_{\lambda}
) whose
stalks are finite rank free
\mathbbm{k}
-modules when
n
is even.
Let
D_{\Lambda}^{?-\ev}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
(resp.

D_{\Lambda}^{?-\odd}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
) denote the full subcategory of
D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of
?
-even (resp.

?
-odd) objects.

(2)

\mathcal{F}
is
?
-
parity
if it is either
?
-even or
?
-odd. Let
D_{\Lambda}^{?-\pty}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
denote the full subcategory of

D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of
?
-parity objects.

(3)

\mathcal{F}
is
even
(resp.
odd
) if it is both
*
-even and
!
-even (resp. odd). Let
D_{\Lambda}^{\ev}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
(resp.

D_{\Lambda}^{\odd}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
) denote the full subcategory of

D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of even (resp. odd) objects.

(4)

\mathcal{F}
is
parity
if there is a decomposition
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{\ev}\oplus\mathcal{F}_{\odd}
where
\mathcal{F}_{\ev}
is even and
\mathcal{F}_{\odd}
is odd. Let

\Par_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
denote the full subcategory of
D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of parity objects.

The remaining results in this section are all twisted equivariant variations of results from
[
JMW14
,
MR18
]
.
We have omitted their proofs since they share nearly identical arguments.

Proposition 2.1.2

(
[
JMW14
, Proposition 2.6]
)
.

If
\mathcal{F}
is
*
-parity and
\mathcal{G}
is
!
-parity, then we have an isomorphism of
\mathbbm{k}
-modules,

\Hom_{D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})}^{n}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\cong\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\Hom_{D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X_{\lambda},\mathbbm{k})}^{n}(j_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{F},j_{\lambda}^{!}\mathcal{G}).

Moreover, both sides are free
\mathbbm{k}
-modules.

Proposition 2.1.3

(
[
JMW14
, Theorem 2.12]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be an indecomposable object in
\Par_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
. Then

(1)

the support of
\mathcal{F}
is of the form
\overline{X_{\lambda}}
, for some
\lambda\in\Lambda
;

(2)

j_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{F}\cong\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}[m]
, for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\in\Loc(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X_{\lambda},\mathbbm{k})
;

(3)

any indecomposable parity object in
\Par_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
supported on
\overline{X_{\lambda}}
and extending
\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}[m]
is
isomorphic to
\mathcal{F}
.

We conclude with a remark on the behavior of parity sheaves under extension of scalars. Let
\mathbbm{k}\to\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}
be a ring morphism of complete local rings. Consider the
extension of scalars functor

\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(-)\coloneq\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L}(-):D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(\mathcal{L})}X,\mathbbm{k}^{\prime})

Lemma 2.1.4

(
[
JMW14
, Lemma 2.36]
)
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
is
?
-even (resp. odd), then
\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(\mathcal{F})
is
?
-even (resp. odd).
In particular,
\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(-)
restricts to a functor

\Par_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\to\Par_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(\mathcal{L})}X,\mathbbm{k}^{\prime})

Lemma 2.1.5

(
[
MR18
, Lemma 2.2]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\in\Par_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
. The Hom-space
\Hom_{\Par_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})

is a free
\mathbbm{k}
-module. Moreover, extension of scalars induces an isomorphism

\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\Hom_{\Par_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Hom_{\Par_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(\mathcal{L})}X,\mathbbm{k}^{\prime})}(\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(\mathcal{E}),\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}))

2.2.
Baby Decomposition Theorem

Let
\pi:H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X\to H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}Y
be a (bounded) morphism of twisted (ind)-algebraic stacks (see
A.4.5
).
Note that
\pi
consists of the following data:

•

an underlying (bounded) morphism of stacks
\pi:X\to Y
,

•

a morphism of algebraic groups
\varphi:H\to H^{\prime}
, and

•

an isomorphism of local systems
\mathcal{L}\cong\varphi^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
.

Suppose
X
(resp.
Y
) is stratified with an
H
-stable (resp.
H^{\prime}
-stable) stratification
X=\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{X}}X_{\lambda}
(resp.
Y=\bigsqcup_{\mu\in\Lambda_{Y}}Y_{\mu}
).
We insist that these stratifications make
D_{\Lambda_{X}}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
and
D_{\Lambda_{Y}}(H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}Y,\mathbbm{k})
satisfy the parity conditions.
We will further impose that
\pi
be stratified (see
A.7.2
).
In particular,
F_{\mu}\coloneq\pi^{-1}(Y_{\mu})
is a union of strata in
X
. We will write
\Lambda_{X,\mu}
for the subset of
\Lambda_{X}
such that

F_{\mu}=\bigcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{X,\mu}}X_{\lambda}.

For any
\lambda\in\Lambda_{X,\mu}
, we can write

\pi_{\lambda,\mu}:H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X_{\lambda}\to H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}Y_{\mu}

for the restriction of
\pi
.

Definition 2.2.1
.

We say that a morphism
\pi:H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X\to H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}Y
is
even
if for all
\mu\in\Lambda_{Y}
and
\lambda\in\Lambda_{X,\mu}
,
the
*
-pushforward along the restricted morphism
\pi_{\lambda,\mu*}
takes even (resp. odd) complexes to even (resp. odd) complexes.

Remark 2.2.2
.

Our definition of even differs significantly from that given in
[
JMW14
]
. In
loc. cit.
, if
\mathcal{F}
is an
H
-equivariant parity sheaf on
X
, then
\For_{H}\mathcal{F}
is also a parity sheaf on
X
.
In particular, the parity conditions are satisfied on not just
H\backslash X
but also
X
.
A stratified morphism
\pi:X\to Y
is said to be
JMW-even
if for all
\lambda\in\Lambda_{X},\mu\in\Lambda_{Y}
, and
\mathcal{L}\in\Loc_{\textnormal{ff}}(X_{\lambda},\mathbbm{k})
, the cohomology of the fibers
F_{\lambda,\mu}
of the induced morphism
\pi_{\lambda,\mu}:X_{\lambda}\to Y_{\mu}
with coefficients in

\mathcal{L}|_{F_{\lambda,\mu}}
is torsion free and concentrated in even degrees.
Similarly, a stratified morphism
\pi:H\backslash X\to H\backslash Y
is said to be
JMW-even
if the underlying map
\pi:X\to Y
is JMW-even.
If one tries to define JMW-even directly on
\pi:H\backslash X\to H\backslash Y
without assuming parity conditions on
X
and
Y
, then in order for the
cohomology of the fiber to be well-defined, one needs that an
H
-stable base point of
Y_{\mu}
exists.
Nonetheless, if
H,H^{\prime}=1
, then the proof of
[
JMW14
, Proposition 2.34]
states JMW-even implies even.

A key ingredient in the proof of the existence of parity sheaves is given by the following proposition.

Proposition 2.2.3

(Baby Decomposition Theorem)
.

Let
\pi:H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X\to H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}Y
be a proper morphism.
Then direct image
\pi_{*}
of a
?
-even (resp.
?
-odd) object is again
?
-even (resp.
?
-odd).
As a result, the direct image
\pi_{*}
takes parity sheaves to parity sheaves.

Proof.

If the result holds for
?
-even sheaves, then it will hold for
?
-odd sheaves by shifting.
Additionally, since
\pi
is proper, there are isomorphisms.

\pi_{*}\mathbb{D}\mathcal{F}\cong\mathbb{D}\pi_{!}\mathcal{F}\cong\mathbb{D}\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}.

As a result it suffices to prove that
\pi_{*}
takes
!
-even sheaves to
!
-even sheaves.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in D(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
be
!
-even. Consider the cartesian square
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\mu}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\mu}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{Y,\mu}}

By base change, we have an isomorphism

(2.2.1)

j_{Y,\mu}^{!}\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}\cong\pi_{*}i_{\mu}^{!}\mathcal{F}

Choose a filtration

F_{\mu}=F_{r}\supset F_{r-1}\supset\ldots\supset F_{0}=\varnothing

where
F_{p}
is closed in
F_{p+1}
and
F_{p}\setminus F_{p-1}=X_{\lambda_{p}}
for some
\lambda_{p}\in\Lambda_{X,\mu}
.
We will write
i_{p}
for the inclusion
i_{p}:F_{p}\hookrightarrow F_{\mu}
, and
i_{p,\mu}
for the composition
i_{p,\mu}=i_{p}\circ i_{\mu}
.
Let
\pi_{p}:F_{p}\to F_{\mu}
denote the restriction of
\pi
to
F_{p}
.

We will prove by induction on
p
that
\pi_{p*}i_{p,\mu}^{!}\mathcal{F}
is an even object on
H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}Y_{\mu}
.
If
p=0
, then
\mathcal{F}=0
and the claim holds trivially.

Now suppose that
p>0
. Consider the commutative diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{p+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\lambda_{p+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\mu}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{p+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{p+1,\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{X,\lambda_{p+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{p,\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mu}}

Consider the open-closed distinguished triangle for the pair
(F_{p},X_{\lambda_{p+1}})
applied to
i_{p+1,\mu}^{!}\mathcal{F}
,

i_{*}j_{X,\lambda_{p+1}}^{!}\mathcal{F}\to i_{p+1,\mu}^{!}\mathcal{F}\to j_{*}i_{p,\mu}^{!}\mathcal{F}\to.

Consider the commutative diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\lambda_{p+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{p+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\lambda_{p+1},\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{p+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{p}}

After applying
\pi_{p+1*}
, we obtain a distinguished triangle

\pi_{\lambda_{p+1},\mu*}j_{X,\lambda_{p+1}}^{!}\mathcal{F}\to\pi_{p+1*}i_{p+1,\mu}^{!}\mathcal{F}\to\pi_{p*}i_{p,\mu}^{!}\mathcal{F}\to.

By definition of an even morphism,
\pi_{\lambda_{p+1},\mu*}j_{X,\lambda_{p+1}}^{!}\mathcal{F}
is even, and by induction,
\pi_{p*}i_{p,\mu}^{!}\mathcal{F}
is even.
Since the subcategory of even objects is closed under extensions, we must have that
\pi_{p+1*}i_{p+1,\mu}^{!}\mathcal{F}
is even.
Therefore, by induction
\pi_{*}i_{\mu}^{!}\mathcal{F}
is even. Since this holds for all
\mu
, by equation (
2.2.1
), we must have that
\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}
is

!
-even as desired.
∎

Since our definition for an even morphism is rather opaque, we will conclude this section with some examples.

Example 2.2.4
.

(1)

Let
X
be an (ind)-algebraic stack with an
H
-action. Let
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k})
. Let
X
be stratified by itself.
Suppose that
D_{(X)}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
is stratified and satisfies the parity conditions.
Let
F
be a simply connected algebraic variety such that
H^{n}(F;\mathbbm{k})
is 0 when
n
is odd and a free
\mathbbm{k}
-module when
n
is even.
Define
Y=X\times F
which inherits an
H
-action from
H
-acting on
X
and acting trivially on
F
.
It is clear that
D_{(Y)}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}Y,\mathbbm{k})
is also stratified and satisfies the parity conditions.
Let
\pi:H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}Y\to H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X
be the projection map. Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{(X)}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
be an even complex.
Then

H^{\bullet}(\pi_{*}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{F}))\cong H^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F})\otimes^{L}H^{\bullet}(F;\mathbbm{k})

The conditions on the cohomology of
F
and
\mathcal{F}
being even ensures that the above cohomology is concentrated in even degrees and free in odd degrees.
The case of
\mathcal{F}
being odd is similar. We can then conclude that
\pi_{*}
is even.

(2)

We can generalize the above example to multiple strata as follows. Let
\pi:H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X\to H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}Y
be a stratified morphism of
twisted (ind)-algebraic stacks.
Suppose that for each
\lambda\in\Lambda_{X,\mu}
,
X_{\lambda}
can be identified
H
-equivariantly with
Y_{\mu}\times F_{\lambda,\mu}
such that
F_{\lambda,\mu}
is a simply connected algebraic variety such that
H^{n}(F_{\lambda,\mu};\mathbbm{k})
is 0 when
n
is odd and a free
\mathbbm{k}
-module when
n
is even.
Moreover, under this identification suppose that
\pi_{\lambda,\mu}
is identified with the projection
Y_{\mu}\times F_{\lambda,\mu}\to Y_{\mu}
.
Then the argument for (1) shows that
\pi_{*}
is even.

2.3.
Mixed Categories

In
[
AR16
]
, Achar and Riche introduced the mixed modular derived category. It has served as a useful replacement for mixed sheaves in positive characteristic.
We will provide a brief overview of these categories and their key features in the twisted equivariant setting.
All the proofs in this section can be easily adapted from
[
AR16
]
, and as such will be omitted.
We only include the components of this theory necessary for the present work. In upcoming work, we will study these categories more systematically.

Throughout, we will fix an (ind-algebraic)
H
-stack
X
with an
H
-stable stratification
X=\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
and a multiplicative local system
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k})
.
We impose the following two constraints on
D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
:

(1)

The parity conditions should be satisfied for
D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
.

(2)

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
and
\mathcal{K}\in\Loc_{\textnormal{ff}}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X_{\lambda},\mathbbm{k})
, there exists an indecomposable parity extension

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{K})
of
\mathcal{K}
.

Definition 2.3.1
.

The
mixed derived category of
(H,\mathcal{L})
-equivariant constructible sheaves
is the following triangulated category

D^{m}_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\coloneq K^{b}\Par_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k}).

The mixed derived category admits two shift functors:

(1)

There is the internal shift inherited from
\Par_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
. We denote this shift by
(1):D^{m}_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\to D^{m}_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
.

(2)

There is the cohomological shift
[1]:D^{m}_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\to D^{m}_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
.

We also introduce the notation
\langle n\rangle\coloneq(-n)[n]
. The functor
\langle 1\rangle
is called the
Tate twist
.

Proposition 2.3.2

(
[
AR16
, Proposition 2.3]
)
.

Let
U
be an open union of strata in
X
. Let
Z
denote its complement. Let
j:U\to X
and
i:Z\to X
denote the inclusions.
There is a recollement diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D^{m}_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}Z,\mathbbm{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D^{m}_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D^{m}_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}U,\mathbbm{k})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{!}}

where
i_{*}
and
j^{*}
are the functors induced from the corresponding functors of parity sheaves.
2
2

2

Despite the notation,
i^{*},i^{!},j_{*}
, and
j_{!}
are not the
similarly denoted functors in the non-mixed category, since these functors do not send parity sheaves to parity sheaves.

Let
Y
be a locally closed union of strata in
X
. Let
h:Y\to X
denote the inclusion.
Proposition
2.3.2
allows one to define adjoint pairs of sheaf functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D^{m}_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}Y,\mathbbm{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\top}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D^{m}_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\top}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D^{m}_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}Y,\mathbbm{k})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{!}}

For example, we can write
h:Y\to X
as the composition
Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j}}{{\to}}\overline{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\to}}X
. We can then define
h_{!}=i_{!}\circ j_{!}
.
The fact that
h
is independent of the factorization follows from
[
AR16
, Lemma 2.6]
.

The following lemma follows from a standard argument using recollement.

Lemma 2.3.3
.

The category
D_{\Lambda}^{m}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
is generated under cohomological shifts and extensions by the standard (resp. costandard) sheaves
j_{\lambda!}\mathcal{K}(n)
(resp.
j_{\lambda*}(\mathcal{K})(n)
) for all
\lambda\in\Lambda
,
\mathcal{K}\in\Loc_{\textnormal{ff}}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X_{\lambda},\mathbbm{k})
, and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

If
f:H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X^{\prime}\to H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X
is a proper, smooth, and even (bounded) morphism of twisted (ind)-algebraic stacks, then
f_{*}
and
f^{*}

both take parity sheaves to parity sheaves.
As a result, there are induced functors

f^{*}:D_{\Lambda}^{m}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\rightleftarrows D_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{m}(H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X^{\prime},\mathbbm{k}):f_{*}.

Let
Y
be a locally closed union of strata in
X
. Let
h:Y\to X
denote the inclusion.
Consider the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}f^{-1}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}

Note that
f^{\prime}
is proper, smooth, and even. By the same argument given in
[
AR16
, Proposition 2.8]
, there are natural isomorphisms of functors

f_{*}\circ h_{*}^{\prime}\cong h_{*}\circ f_{*}^{\prime},\hskip 56.9055ptf_{*}\circ h_{!}^{\prime}\cong h_{!}\circ f_{*}^{\prime},

(h^{\prime})^{*}\circ f^{*}\cong(f^{\prime})^{*}\circ h^{*},\hskip 56.9055pt(h^{\prime})^{!}\circ f^{*}\cong(f^{\prime})^{*}\circ h^{!},

f^{*}\circ h_{*}\cong h_{*}^{\prime}\circ(f^{\prime})^{*},\hskip 56.9055ptf^{*}\circ h_{!}\cong h_{!}^{\prime}\circ(f^{\prime})^{*},

h^{*}\circ f_{*}\cong f_{*}^{\prime}\circ(h^{\prime})^{*},\hskip 56.9055pth^{!}\circ f_{*}\cong f_{*}^{\prime}\circ(h^{\prime})^{!}.

Finally, we conclude with a useful Hom-vanishing constraint.

Lemma 2.3.4

(
[
AR16
, Lemma 3.2]
)
.

Suppose that for all
\lambda\in\Lambda
, there is an
H
-equivariant isomorphism
X_{\lambda}\cong H\times Y_{\lambda}
where
Y_{\lambda}
is an affine space.
In this case,
\Loc_{\textnormal{ff}}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}H\times Y_{\lambda},\mathbbm{k})
is generated by the sheaf
\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{Y_{\lambda}}
.
We will write
\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\in\Loc_{\textnormal{ff}}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X_{\lambda},\mathbbm{k})
for the sheaf corresponding to
\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{Y_{\lambda}}
.
Moreover, for all
\lambda,\mu\in\Lambda
, we have

\Hom_{D_{\Lambda}^{m}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})}(j_{\lambda!}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda},j_{\mu*}\mathcal{K}_{\mu}\langle n\rangle[i])\cong\begin{cases}\mathbbm{k}&\lambda=\mu,n=\dim X_{\lambda}-\dim X_{\mu},i=-n,\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

3.
Monodromic Hecke Categories

Fix a Kac-Moody group
G
with Borel
B
containing a maximal torus
T
as in
1.4.3
.
Let
W
denote the Weyl group of
G
with respect to
T
. We will also denote
S
the set of simple reflections in
W
.
We return to the setting where
\mathbbm{k}
is any noetherian domain of finite global dimension.

3.1.
Endoscopic Weyl Groups

We review the theory of endoscopic Weyl groups following
[
LY20
]
.
We encounter additional problems when working with Kac-Moody groups since only real roots have associated coroots.
The definition of the endoscopic Weyl group only needs minor modifications; however, modified arguments need to be given for some foundations (c.f.,
[
Héb22
,
Héb23
]
).

Fix a multiplicative local system
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
. We define a
W
-action on
\Ch(T)
by
w\cdot\mathcal{L}=(w^{-1})^{*}\mathcal{L}
. Throughout the paper, we will fix a

W
-orbit
\mathfrak{o}
of
\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
.
For
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
, we set

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq\{w\in W\mid w(\mathcal{L})\cong\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\}.

If
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
, we will write
W_{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq{}_{\mathcal{L}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
, i.e., the stabilizer in
W
of
\mathcal{L}
.

3.1.1.
Definition

Let
\Phi_{\re}
denote the set of real roots in
\Phi
(i.e., roots of the form
w\cdot\alpha_{s}
for
w\in W
and
s\in S
).
To each real root
\alpha=w\cdot\alpha_{s}
, there is a corresponding real coroot
\alpha^{\vee}\coloneq w\cdot\alpha_{s}^{\vee}
.
We define a collection of real coroots

\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{\vee}\coloneq\{\alpha^{\vee}\in\Phi_{\re}^{\vee}\mid(\alpha^{\vee})^{*}\mathcal{L}\cong\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\}

where we view
\alpha^{\vee}
as a morphism
\mathbb{G}_{m}\to T
.
We can then define a collection of real roots
\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}
consisting of roots
\alpha\in\Phi_{\re}
such that
\alpha^{\vee}\in\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{\vee}
.
Denote
\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{+}=\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}\cap\Phi^{+}
.

Let
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
be the subgroup of
W
generated by reflections of roots in
\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}
.

Let
R
denote the set of reflections in
W
, i.e., elements of the form
wsw^{-1}
where
w\in W
and
s\in S
.
Let
R_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
denote the set of reflections in
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
For each
w\in W
, we can define
N(w)\coloneq\{r\in R_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}\mid rw^{-1}<w^{-1}\}
.
By
[
Dye90
, Theorem 3.3]
,
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
is a Coxeter group with simple reflections

S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}\coloneq\{t\in R\mid N(t)\cap W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}=\{t\}\}.

We call the reflections in
S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
the
endosimple
reflections.

Let
\leq_{\mathcal{L}}
and
\ell_{\mathcal{L}}
denote the Bruhat order and length on
(W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
.
Since
S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}\subset R
, for all
x,y\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
,

(3.1.1)

x\leq_{\mathcal{L}}y\implies x\leq y

Lemma 3.1.2

(
[
Héb22
, Lemma 5.13]
)
.

(1)

One has
R\cap W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}=R_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.

(2)

Let
\alpha\in\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{+}
. Then
r_{\alpha}\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
if and only if
r_{\alpha}\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{+}\cap\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{-}=\{-\alpha\}
.

(3)

Let
w\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
. Let
\underline{w}=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{k})
be a reduced expression in
(W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
.
Then

\{\alpha\in\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{+}\mid w\alpha<0\}=\{\alpha_{r_{k}},r_{k}\cdot\alpha_{r_{k-1}},\ldots,r_{k}\ldots r_{2}\cdot\alpha_{r_{1}}\}.

In particular,

\ell_{\mathcal{L}}(w)\coloneq\#\{\alpha\in\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{+}\mid w\alpha<0\}.

When
G
is finite type, Lemma
3.1.2
(2) shows that
S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
agrees with the Coxeter generators of
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
given in
[
LY20
]
.

3.1.3.
Blocks

Lemma 3.1.4
.

W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
is a normal subgroup of
W_{\mathcal{L}}
.

Proof.

Let
w\in W_{\mathcal{L}}
and
\alpha\in\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
By definition,
wr_{\alpha}w^{-1}=r_{w\alpha}
.
We can then compute

(w\alpha^{\vee})^{*}\mathcal{L}\cong(\alpha^{\vee})^{*}w\mathcal{L}\cong(\alpha^{\vee})^{*}\mathcal{L}\cong\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}.

∎

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
. We define the set of
blocks
as the set of cosets

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}/W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}.

By Lemma
3.1.4
, the set of blocks can also be defined by
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}=W_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}\backslash{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
.
Each block
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
inherits a partial order
\leq
from the Bruhat order in
W
.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
.
Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
and
\gamma\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
.
The set
\gamma\cdot\beta\coloneq\{xy\mid x\in\gamma,y\in\beta\}
is equal to
W_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}xy=xW_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}y=xyW_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
(for any

x\in\beta
and
y\in\gamma
).
This makes
\gamma\cdot\beta
an element of
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
. Moreover, there is a map

(-)\cdot(-):{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}.

This map is associative in the obvious sense.

The following is an analogue of
[
LY20
, Lemma 4.2]
and
[
LY20
, Corollary 4.3]
. However, our proofs differ slightly.

Lemma 3.1.5
.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
.
Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
,
\gamma\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
.

(1)

There exists a unique minimal length (in
W
) element
w^{\beta}\in\beta
such that
w^{\beta}\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{+}\subseteq\Phi^{+}
.

(2)

The minimal elements in each block satisfy
w^{\gamma}w^{\beta}=w^{\gamma\beta}
.

Proof.

We will just prove the first statement. The second statement follows the criterion for
w^{\beta}
given in the first statement (c.f.,
[
LY20
, Corollary 4.3]
).
Let
w^{\beta}
be of minimal length in
\beta
.
Let
r=r_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{+}
.
By assumption,
\ell(wr)>\ell(w)
. As a result,
w\cdot\alpha\in\Phi^{+}
for all
\alpha\in\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{+}
.

Suppose
v\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
with
v\neq e
.
Since
\ell_{\mathcal{L}}(v)>0
, there must exist some
\alpha\in\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{+}
such that
v\cdot\alpha\in\Phi^{-}
.
Therefore,
w^{\beta}v\cdot\alpha\in\Phi^{-}
, so the only minimal length element of
\beta
sending
\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{+}
to
\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{+}
is
w^{\beta}
.
∎

Lemma 3.1.6
.

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
. Then the map
w\mapsto w^{\beta}ww^{\beta,-1}
gives an isomorphism of Coxeter groups
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}W_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}
.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
3.1.5
that
\ell_{\mathcal{L}}(w)=\ell_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(w^{\beta}ww^{\beta,-1})
for all
w\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
Moreover, if
\alpha\in\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{+}
, then
w^{\beta}r_{\alpha}w^{\beta,-1}=r_{w^{\beta}\alpha}
and
w^{\beta}\alpha\in\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{+}
.
As a result, conjugation by
w^{\beta}
induces a bijection between
S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
and
S_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}
.
∎

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
.
We can define a length function
\ell_{\beta}:\beta\to\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
as follows.
For each
w\in\beta
, there exists a unique
v\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
such that
w=w^{\beta}v
. We then define
\ell_{\beta}(w)=\ell_{\mathcal{L}}(v)
.
By Lemma
3.1.5
, the only element of
\beta
with length 0 is
w^{\beta}
.
Similarly, we can define a partial order on
\beta
, denoted
\leq_{\beta}
. For
w.w^{\prime}\in\beta
, let
v,v^{\prime}\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
such that
w=w^{\beta}v
and
w^{\prime}=w^{\beta}v^{\prime}
.
We then define
w\leq_{\beta}w^{\prime}
if and only if
v\leq_{\mathcal{L}}v^{\prime}
.

The following lemmas are a summary of results found in
[
LY20
, §4]
. Their proofs can follow from those in
loc. cit.
after obvious modifications are made.

Lemma 3.1.7
.

Let
s\in S
be a simple reflection such that
s\mathcal{L}\neq\mathcal{L}
. Then
s
is the minimal element in the block
sW_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.

Lemma 3.1.8
.

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
.

(1)

If
\gamma\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
, the map
(\beta,\leq_{\beta})\to(\gamma\beta,\leq_{\gamma\beta})
given by
w\mapsto w^{\gamma}w
is an
isomorphism of posets.

(2)

If
\delta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}
, then the map
(\beta,\leq_{\beta})\to(\beta\delta,\leq_{\beta\delta})
given by
w\mapsto ww^{\delta}
is an
isomorphism of posets.

(3)

For
w,w^{\prime}\in\beta
, if
w\leq_{\beta}w^{\prime}
, then
w\leq w^{\prime}
.

Lemma 3.1.9
.

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
and
w\in\beta
.
Let
\underline{w}=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{k})
be a reduced expression of
w
.
Let
\mathcal{L}_{k+1}\coloneq\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}_{i}=s_{i}\ldots s_{k}\mathcal{L}
for
1\leq i\leq k
.
Then

(1)

\ell_{\beta}(w)=\#\{\alpha\in\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{+}\mid w\alpha<0\}
.

(2)

For
\gamma\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
, we have

\ell_{\gamma\beta}(w^{\gamma}w)=\ell_{\beta}(w).

(3)

We have
\ell_{\beta}(w)=\#\{1\leq i\leq k\mid\mathcal{L}_{i}=\mathcal{L}_{i+1}\}
.

3.2.
Hecke Categories

3.2.1.
Definition and Basic Structure

In this section, we recall the construction of the monodromic Hecke category and discuss some of its basic properties.
We regard
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}=\bigcup_{w\in W}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\leq w}
as an ind-scheme.
The algebraic group of pro-finite type
T\times B
acts on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}
via
(t,b)\cdot x=txb^{-1}
.
This action is compatible in the sense of §
A.5.3
. In more detail, for each
w\in W
, there exists a normal, finite codimension
subgroup
J_{w}
of
B
such that
J_{w}\subseteq U
and
J_{w}
acts trivially on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\leq w}
. Without loss of generality, by taking common intersections, we may assume
that
J_{x}\subset J_{w}
for
x\leq w
.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
. We can abuse notation and regard
\mathcal{L}
as a multiplicative local system on
B
via the pullback along the projection map
B\to T
.
We can then define the
monodromic Hecke category
as

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\coloneq D_{\cons}(T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}/_{\mathcal{L}}B,\mathbbm{k}).

It will be useful to unpack this definition. We can define

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\leq w,\mathbbm{k})=D_{\cons}(T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\leq w}/(U/J_{w}))/_{\mathcal{L}}T,\mathbbm{k})

and

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(w,\mathbbm{k})=D_{\cons}(T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}/(U/J_{w}))/_{\mathcal{L}}T,\mathbbm{k}).

Then the monodromic Hecke category is given as the limit of categories

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})=\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{w\in W}}}{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\leq w,\mathbbm{k}),

where the transition maps are given by pushforwards of the closed embeddings
i_{x,y}:\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\leq x}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\leq y}
.

When
G
is a reductive group, then the monodromic Hecke category’s definition can be simplified as follows:

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})=D_{\cons}(T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(U\backslash G/U)/_{\mathcal{L}}T,\mathbbm{k}).

Proposition 3.2.2
.

Fix
w\in W
and define a subset

\Gamma(w)=\{(wt,t)\in T\times T\mid t\in T\}.

We regard
\Gamma(w)
as an algebraic group isomorphic to
T
.

(1)

If
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\neq w(\mathcal{L})
, then
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(w,\mathbbm{k})=0
. If
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=w(\mathcal{L})
, then there is an equivalence of categories

{}_{w(\mathcal{L})\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(w,\mathbbm{k})\cong D_{\cons}(\Gamma(w)\backslash\{\dot{w}\},\mathbbm{k})

obtained by taking the stalk at
\dot{w}
for a lift
\dot{w}\in G
of
w
.

(2)

The category
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
satisfies the parity conditions.

Proof.

(1):
Note that since
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}\cong\mathbb{A}^{\ell(w)}\times\dot{w}T
, requiring that a sheaf on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}
be
(T\times T,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{-1})
-equivariant forces that
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=w\mathcal{L}
.
If
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=w\mathcal{L}
, we can take the stalk at a lift
\dot{w}
of
w
to obtain a functor,

i_{\dot{w}}^{*}:{}_{w(\mathcal{L})\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(w,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}((T\times T){}_{(w\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{-1})}\backslash\dot{w}T,\mathbbm{k})\cong D_{\cons}(\Gamma(w)\backslash\{\dot{w}\},\mathbbm{k}).

So
w\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{-1}
restricted to
\Gamma(w)
is a
constant sheaf. It is easy to check that since the cohomology of twisted equivariant constructible sheaves on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}
are local systems, that
i_{\dot{w}}^{*}
gives an
equivalence of triangulated categories.

(2):
The parity conditions follow immediately from (1) along with the observation that
D_{\cons}(T\backslash\pt,\mathbbm{k})
satisfies the parity
conditions since the equivariant cohomology
H_{T}^{\bullet}(\pt;\mathbbm{k})\cong H_{\Gamma(w)}(\{\dot{w}\};\mathbbm{k})
is a free
\mathbbm{k}
-module in even degrees and vanishes
in odd degrees.
∎

The previous proposition gives us the freedom to use standard, costandards, IC-extensions, parity sheaves, and simple perverse sheaves in
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
.
The general theory of these is given in Appendix
A
, and we will review it here.
For each
w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
, we define
\mathcal{K}_{\dot{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(w,\mathbbm{k})
as the sheaf corresponding to the constant sheaf on

\{\dot{w}\}/\Gamma(w)
under the
equivalence given in Proposition
3.2.2
.
Alternatively, under the isomorphism of schemes
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}\cong\mathbb{A}^{\ell(w)}\times\dot{w}T
,
\mathcal{K}_{\dot{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}
is characterized as the unique sheaf (up to
isomorphism) which satisfies
\For_{T\times T,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{-1}}\mathcal{K}_{\dot{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\ell(w)}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}
.
A priori,
\mathcal{K}_{\dot{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}
depends on the choice on the choice of lift
\dot{w}
.
It is easy to show that the isomorphism class of
\mathcal{K}_{\dot{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}
does not depend on the choice
\dot{w}
.
As a result, we will simply write
\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
to refer to any object whose isomorphism class is
\mathcal{K}_{\dot{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}
. We warn that
\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
is only
defined up to non-unique isomorphism– there is no canonical choice of
\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
except when
w=e
.

We can then define the following sheaves in
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})

\Delta_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq j_{w!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}[\ell(w)]\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\nabla_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq j_{w!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}[\ell(w)],

which are called the
standard
and
costandard
sheaves respectively.

Recall from Appendix
A
that
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
has a perverse
t
-structure. Explicitly a sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
is perverse if

\For_{T\times T,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{-1}}\mathcal{F}[2\dim T]
is a perverse on
U\backslash G/U
.
It is then easy to see that
\Delta_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
and
\nabla_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
are both perverse sheaves. We can define the IC-sheaves,

\IC_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}=\text{Im}(\Delta_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\nabla_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}).

The tensor product for twisted equivariant sheaves defines a bifunctor

(-)\otimes^{L}(-):{}_{\mathcal{L}_{4}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}_{3}}(\mathbbm{k})\times{}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}_{1}}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}_{4}\mathcal{L}_{2}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}_{3}\mathcal{L}_{1}}(\mathbbm{k})

for
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\mathcal{L}_{3},\mathcal{L}_{4}\in\mathfrak{o}
.
Similarly, the tensor product admits a right adjoint taking

\textbf{R}\mathcal{H}om(-,-):{}_{\mathcal{L}_{4}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}_{3}}^{\op}(\mathbbm{k})\times{}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}_{1}}\to{}_{\mathcal{L}_{4}^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{2}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}_{3}^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{1}}(\mathbbm{k}).

for
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\mathcal{L}_{3},\mathcal{L}_{4}\in\mathfrak{o}
.

3.2.3.
Parabolic Variants

Let
J\subseteq I
of finite type. Associated to such a
J
is a standard parabolic subgroup
P_{J}
containing
B
with Levi decomposition
P_{J}=U^{J}L_{J}
where
L_{J}
is
a connected reductive group containing
T
.
Let
W_{J}
be the subgroup of
W
generated by
J
, which can be identified with the Weyl group of
L_{J}
. Let
w_{J}
denote the element of
W_{J}
of maximal length.
Let
W^{J}\subseteq W
denote the minimal length representatives of the cosets in
W/W_{J}
.
The parabolic Bruhat decomposition states that the orbits of the right
P_{J}
-action on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}
are indexed by
W/W_{J}
.
For each
\overline{w}\in W/W_{J}
, we will write
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\overline{w}}^{J}=U\backslash B\dot{w}P_{J}
where
\dot{w}\in N_{G}(T)
is a lift of a representative of

\overline{w}
. We will also write
j_{\overline{w}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\overline{w}}^{J}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}
for the inclusion map.
The orbit closures are given by unions of strata from the Bruhat order

\overline{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\overline{w}}^{J}}=\bigcup_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\overline{x}\in W/W_{J}}}{{\overline{x}\leq\overline{w}}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\overline{x}}^{J}

where the order on
W/W_{J}
is given by the restricted Bruhat order from
W
. More precisely, the partial order on
W/W_{J}
is given by
\overline{x}\leq\overline{y}
if
x_{-}<y_{-}
where
x_{-}
(resp.
y_{-}
) is of minimal length in
\overline{x}
(resp.
\overline{y}
).

Let
\Ch_{J}(T,\mathbbm{k})
denote the subset of
\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of local systems
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
such that
s_{j}(\mathcal{L})\cong\mathcal{L}
for all
j\in J
.
As a result, the action of
W/W_{J}
on
\Ch_{J}(T,\mathbbm{k})
is well-defined. Precisely, if
\overline{w}\in W/W_{J}
and
w\in W
is a representative for the coset
\overline{w}
,
we can define
\overline{w}\cdot\mathcal{L}=w(\mathcal{L})
which is independent of the choice of representative.
If
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\Ch_{J}(T,\mathbbm{k})
are in the same
W
-orbit, we can define
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(W/W_{J})_{\mathcal{L}}=\{\overline{w}\in W/W_{J}\mid\overline{w}(\mathcal{L})=\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\}
.

Lemma 3.2.4
.

Let
\mathcal{L}\in\mathfrak{o}
and
J\subset I
of finite type.
Then
\mathcal{L}
can be extended to a multiplicative rank one local system
\mathcal{L}^{J}
on
L_{J}
if and only if
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch_{J}(T,\mathbbm{k})
.

Proof.

First, we observe that
\mathcal{L}\in\mathfrak{o}
is in
\Ch_{J}(T,\mathbbm{k})
if and only if
(\alpha_{j}^{\vee})^{*}(\mathcal{L})\cong\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
for all
j\in J
.

Note that
\pi_{1}(L_{J})\cong X_{*}(T)/\mathbb{Z}\Phi_{J}^{\vee}
where
\Phi_{J}^{\vee}=\{\alpha_{j}^{\vee}\}_{j\in J}
.
The inclusion of the maximal torus
T\hookrightarrow L_{J}
induces a quotient map

(3.2.1)

\pi_{1}(T)\cong X_{*}(T)\twoheadrightarrow X_{*}(T)/\mathbb{Z}\Phi_{J}\cong\pi_{1}(L_{J})

In particular, the irreducible representations of
\pi_{1}(L_{J})
are in 1-1 correspondence with
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
such that
(\alpha_{j}^{\vee})^{*}(\mathcal{L})\cong\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
for all
j\in J
.
For such a
\mathcal{L}
, let
\mathcal{L}^{J}
be the associated local system on
L_{J}
.
Computing
(\mathcal{L}^{J})\mid_{T}
is given algebraically by the composition of the irreducible representation for
\pi(L_{J})
with the quotient map of
(
3.2.1
).
It is easy to see that this corresponds with
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
.
∎

Fix
J\subset I
of finite type.
For each
\overline{w}\in W/W_{J}
, there exists a normal subgroup
J_{\overline{w}}
of
P_{J}
of finite codimension, contained in
U^{J}
,
such that the right action of
J_{\overline{w}}
on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\leq\overline{w}}^{J}
is trivial.
For a fixed
\overline{w}\in W/W_{J}
, we can pick
J_{\overline{x}}
such that if
\overline{w}\leq\overline{x}
, then
J_{\overline{w}}\subseteq J_{\overline{x}}
.
In particular, the action of
T\times P_{J}
on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}
is compatible.

Let
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch_{J}(L,\mathbbm{k})
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
. We can view
\mathcal{L}^{J}
as a multiplicative local system on
P_{J}
via the projection map
P_{J}\to L_{J}
.
We define the
parabolic monodromic Hecke category

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{J/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})=D_{\cons}(T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}/_{\mathcal{L}^{J}}P_{J},\mathbbm{k}).

It is also useful to consider the restriction of the parabolic monodromic Hecke category to the natural stratification. Let
\overline{w}\in W/W_{J}
. We then define

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{J/\mathcal{L}}(\overline{w},\mathbbm{k})\coloneq D_{\cons}(T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\overline{w}}^{J}/(U^{J}/J_{\overline{w}}))/_{\mathcal{L}^{J}}L_{J},\mathbbm{k}).

Lemma 3.2.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{o}
be a
W
-orbit in
\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
. Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\Ch_{J}(T,\mathbbm{k})\cap\mathfrak{o}
.

(1)

If
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\neq\overline{w}(\mathcal{L})
, then
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{J/\mathcal{L}}(\overline{w},\mathbbm{k})=0
. If
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=\overline{w}(\mathcal{L})
, then there is an equivalence
of categories

{}_{\overline{w}\mathcal{L}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{J/\mathcal{L}}(\overline{w},\mathbbm{k})\cong D_{\cons}(T\backslash\{\dot{w}\},\mathbbm{k})

obtained by taking the stalk at
\dot{w}
for a lift
\dot{w}\in N_{G}(T)
of
\overline{w}
.

(2)

The category
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{J/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
satisfies the parity conditions.

Proof.

Statement (2) follows from the same argument given for Proposition
3.2.2
(2).
We closely follow the argument given in
[
LY20
, §3.10]
.
Pick a lift
\dot{w}\in N_{G}(T)
of
\overline{w}\in W/W_{J}
. There is an isomorphism

(3.2.2)

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\overline{w}}^{J}/(U^{J}/J_{\overline{w}})\cong\dot{w}\cdot\left((\dot{w}^{-1}U\dot{w}\cap L_{J}J_{\overline{w}})\backslash L_{J}J_{\overline{w}}\right).

Under this isomorphism, the left action by
t\in T
on the left-hand side of (
3.2.2
) becomes the left
translation of
\dot{w}^{-1}t\dot{w}
on
\dot{w}\cdot\left((\dot{w}^{-1}U\dot{w}\cap L_{J}J_{\overline{w}})\backslash L_{J}J_{\overline{w}}\right)
.
By taking the stalk at
\dot{w}
we get an equivalence of categories

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{J/\mathcal{L}}(\overline{w},\mathbbm{k})\cong D_{\cons}(T\backslash_{(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\otimes\overline{w}\mathcal{L}^{J,-1})\mid_{T}}\{\dot{w}\},\mathbbm{k})\cong D_{\cons}(T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{-1}}\{\dot{w}\})

where the second isomorphism follows from
\mathcal{L}^{J}\mid_{T}\cong\mathcal{L}
.
As a result,
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{J/\mathcal{L}}(\overline{w},\mathbbm{k})=0
unless
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=\overline{w}\mathcal{L}
, in which case it
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{J/\mathcal{L}}(\overline{w},\mathbbm{k})\cong D_{\cons}(T\backslash\{\dot{w}\},\mathbbm{k})
.
∎

For each
\overline{w}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(W/W_{J})_{\mathcal{L}}
, we can define
\mathcal{K}_{\overline{w}}^{J,\mathcal{L}}\in{}_{\overline{w}\mathcal{L}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{J/\mathcal{L}}(\overline{w},\mathbbm{k})
as
the sheaf corresponding to the constant sheaf on
T\backslash\{\dot{w}\}
.
Alternatively,
\mathcal{K}_{\overline{w}}^{J,\mathcal{L}}
can be identified with
\mathcal{L}^{J}
viewed as a
(\dot{w}^{-1}U\dot{w}\cap L_{J})
-equivariant sheaf on
L_{J}
.
In particular, since
\mathcal{L}^{J}
is a multiplicative local system,
\mathcal{K}_{\overline{w}}^{J,\mathcal{L}}
does not depend on the choice of
\dot{w}
up to a (non-unique) isomorphism.

We can then define variations of
standard
and
costandard
sheaves in
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{J/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
.
Let
\overline{w}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(W/W_{J})_{\mathcal{L}}
and
w\in W^{J}
be a representative in the coset
\overline{w}
.

\Delta_{\overline{w}}^{J,\mathcal{L}}\coloneq j_{\overline{w}!}\mathcal{K}_{\overline{w}}^{J,\mathcal{L}}[\ell(w)]\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\nabla_{\overline{w}}^{J,\mathcal{L}}\coloneq j_{\overline{w}*}\mathcal{K}_{\overline{w}}^{J,\mathcal{L}}[\ell(w)].

It can be easily checked that
\Delta_{\overline{w}}^{J,\mathcal{L}}
and
\nabla_{\overline{w}}^{J,\mathcal{L}}
are perverse sheaves, so we can consider the IC-extension

\IC_{\overline{w}}^{J,\mathcal{L}}\coloneq\textnormal{Im}\left(\Delta_{\overline{w}}^{J,\mathcal{L}}\to\nabla_{\overline{w}}^{J,\mathcal{L}}\right).

3.2.6.
Averaging along Parabolics

Remark 3.2.7
.

Let
J\subseteq I
be of finite type. A Borel subgroup
B_{J}
of
L_{J}
containing
T
is given by
B\cap L_{J}
. Similarly, its unipotent radical
U_{J}
of
B_{J}
is
given by
U\cap L_{J}
.
Alternatively,
B_{J}
can be identified with the quotient
B/U^{J}
.

Despite
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{G}
being infinite dimensional, the quotient
U\backslash P_{J}
is finite dimensional.
There are isomorphisms of varieties (c.f.,
[
Kum02
, Example 7.1.7]
),

(3.2.3)

U\backslash P_{J}\cong U_{J}\backslash L_{J}=\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{L_{J}}.

Consider the special case when
J=\{s\}
for some
s\in S
. Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\leq s}=U\backslash P_{s}
, we obtain an equivalence of categories

(3.2.4)

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\leq s,\mathbbm{k})\cong D_{\cons}(T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{L_{s}}/U_{s})/_{\mathcal{L}}T,\mathbbm{k})

where the right-hand side of (
3.2.4
) is simply the monodromic Hecke category for
L_{s}
.

Consider the homomorphism
\varphi:B\hookrightarrow P_{s}
of group schemes (of pro-finite type).
They both act on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}
on the right.
If
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
and
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, by Lemma
3.2.4
, there is an extension
\mathcal{L}^{s}
of
\mathcal{L}
to
L_{s}
.
There is then a morphism of twisted ind-algebraic stacks

\pi_{s}:T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}/_{\mathcal{L}}B\to T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}/_{\mathcal{L}^{s}}P_{s}.

induced by the identity map
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}
and the morphism
\varphi:B\hookrightarrow P_{s}
.
We can then apply the general formalism of averaging and forgetting twisted equivariance to obtain adjoint functors

\pi_{s}^{*}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{s/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\rightleftarrows{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k}):\pi_{s*}.

Likewise, there are variants for
!
-pushforward/pullback.
Since
P_{s}/B\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
, there are natural isomorphisms of functors
\pi_{s*}\cong\pi_{s!}
and
\pi_{s}^{!}\cong\pi_{s}^{*}[2]
.

Lemma 3.2.8
.

Let
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
be a simple reflection in
W
. Suppose that
\ell(w)>\ell(ws)
, then there is an isomorphism

\pi_{s}^{*}\IC_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}[1]\cong\IC_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}.

Proof.

Since
\pi_{s}^{!}[-1]\cong\pi_{s}^{*}[1]
and
P_{s}/B\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
, it is clear that
\pi_{s}^{*}\IC_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}[1]
is a simple perverse sheaf supported on

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\leq w}
.
The lemma then follows after noting that the restriction
\pi_{s}^{*}\IC_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}[1]
along
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}
is nonzero since
\ell(w)>\ell(ws)
.
∎

Lemma 3.2.9
.

Let
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
be a simple reflection in
W
. Suppose that
\ell(w)>\ell(ws)
, then there are isomorphisms

\pi_{s*}\Delta_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\pi_{s*}\Delta_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}[-1].

Proof.

Let
x\in W
. Let
R(x)=\{\alpha\in\Phi^{+}\mid x\alpha<0\}
.
We can consider
U_{x}=\prod_{\alpha\in R(x)}U_{\alpha}
where
U_{\alpha}
is the root subgroup for
\alpha
of
G
.
Let
\dot{x}\in N_{G}(T)
be a lift of
x
.
There is a
B\times B
-equivariant isomorphism of schemes
U\dot{x}B\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}U_{x}\times\dot{x}B
where
B\times B
acts by
(b_{1},b_{2})\cdot(u,\dot{x}g)=(b_{1}ub_{1}^{-1},b_{1}\dot{x}gb_{2}^{-1})
.
Similarly, there is a
B\times P_{s}
-equivariant isomorphism of schemes
U\dot{ws}P_{s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}U_{ws}\times\dot{ws}P_{s}
where
B\times P_{s}
acts in an
analogous way.
By taking
x=ws
, it follows that the map

a_{ws}:\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{ws}\times^{B}P_{s}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\overline{w}}^{s}

induced by the right action of
B
on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{ws}
is an isomorphism of schemes.
Similarly, by taking
x=w
, the map

a_{w}:\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}\times^{B}P_{s}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\overline{w}}^{s}

is a trivial
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-fibration.

Note that the descent of
\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{s}
regarded as a sheaf on
T\times L_{s}
to a sheaf on
T\times^{T}L_{s}\cong L_{s}
is given by
\mathcal{L}^{s}
.
This observation along with the properties of
a_{w}
and
a_{ws}
discussed earlier yield isomorphisms

\pi_{s!}\mathcal{K}_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong a_{ws!}(\mathcal{K}_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{L}^{s})\cong\mathcal{K}_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\pi_{s!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong a_{w!}(\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{L}^{s})\cong\mathcal{K}_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}[-2].

The result readily follows.
∎

3.3.
Convolution

3.3.1.
Definition and Properties

Just as in the non-monodromic Hecke category, one can define a convolution bifunctor,

(-)\star(-):{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathbbm{k})\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k}).

We will begin with its construction. Consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\backslash G\times^{U}G/U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\backslash G\times^{B}G/U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\backslash G/U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\backslash G/U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\backslash G/U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

Let
T\times T\times T
act on
U\backslash G\times^{U}G/U
via
(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})\cdot(x,y)=(t_{1}xt_{2}^{-1},t_{2}yt_{3}^{-1})
.
We can define a morphism of twisted ind-algebraic stacks

p:\frac{T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}(U\backslash G\times^{U}G/U)/_{\mathcal{L}}T}{T}{}_{\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{T}}\to T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}(U\backslash G/U)/_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}T\times T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(U\backslash G/U)/_{\mathcal{L}}T

which is given by the projection maps
U\backslash G\times^{U}G/U\to U\backslash G/U\times U\backslash G/U
and on
T^{3}\to T^{4}
is given by
(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})\mapsto(t_{1},t_{2},t_{2},t_{3})
.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathbbm{k})
and
\mathcal{G}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
.
The
(T\times T\times T,\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\boxtimes\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{T}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{-1})
-equivariant sheaf
p^{*}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{G})
descends to a
(T\times T,\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{-1})
-equivariant sheaf
\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{G}
on
U\backslash G\times^{B}G/U
.
We then define

\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{G}\coloneq m_{!}(\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{G})

which naturally is also
(T\times T,\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{-1})
-equivariant, and thus can be regarded as an object on
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
.

Convolution for monodromic Hecke categories is surveyed well in other literature (c.f.,
[
LY20
,
Gou21
,
Li20
]
). We review some basic facts about convolution whose proofs
can be found in
loc. cit.

Lemma 3.3.2
.

Consider the object

\Delta_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\IC_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\nabla_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})

The functors

\IC_{e}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star(-):{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})

(-)\star\IC_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})

are naturally isomorphic to the identity functor.

Lemma 3.3.3
.

The convolution bifunctor is suitably associative. Namely, the following diagram commutes up to natural isomorphism.

(3.3.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbbm{k})\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathbbm{k})\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbbm{k})\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathbbm{k})\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\times\star}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\star\times\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\star}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\star}

Lemma 3.3.4
.

We have natural isomorphisms
3
3

3

Naturality here should be interpreted as after picking lifts
\dot{x}
and
\dot{y}
of
x
and
y
. Then
\dot{x}\dot{y}
is a
lift of
xy
, and all objects are fixed and not just isomorphism classes.

(1)

\Delta_{xy}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{x}^{y(\mathcal{L})}\star\Delta_{y}^{\mathcal{L}}
if
\ell(xy)=\ell(x)+\ell(y)
;

(2)

\nabla_{xy}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\nabla_{x}^{y(\mathcal{L})}\star\nabla_{y}^{\mathcal{L}}
if
\ell(xy)=\ell(x)+\ell(y)
;

The foundations of many inductive arguments on properties of convolution rely on understanding how convolving by an
\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
behaves for
s\in S
.
In the non-monodromic case, this is described by the so-called push-pull lemma. However, in the monodromic case, the description is more subtle.
When
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, then convolving by
\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
behaves like the non-monodromic case and is governed by a version of the push-pull lemma.
If
s\notin W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, then convolving by
\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
can be thought of “translating” between monodromic Hecke categories.

Lemma 3.3.5
.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
. Let
s\in W
be a simple reflection such that
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, then there is a canonical isomorphism of endofunctors,

(-)\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\pi_{s}^{*}\pi_{s*}(-)[1]

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
. By Remark
3.2.7
, we can regard
\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
as
\mathcal{L}^{s}[1]
viewed as a

U_{s}
-equivariant sheaf on
L_{s}
.
Let
a:\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}\times^{B}P_{s}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}
be the map given by the right action of
B
on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}
.
We then have an isomorphism

\mathcal{F}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong a_{!}(\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{L}^{s}[1])

where
\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{L}^{s}[1]
is the descent of
\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{s}[1]
to a sheaf on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}\times^{B}P_{s}
.
From the discussion in §A.4.11, we see that
