a_{!}(\mathcal{F}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{L}^{s}[1])
has the same underlying complex as
\pi_{s*}\mathcal{F}[1]
in

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{s/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
.
After only remembering
(T,\mathcal{L})
-equivariance on the right, we obtain an isomorphism of sheaves
\mathcal{F}\star\IC_{s}\cong\pi_{s}^{*}\pi_{s*}\mathcal{F}[1]
in
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
as desired.
∎

Lemma 3.3.6
.

Let
s\in S
be a simple reflection such that
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, and let
w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
.
Then

(3.3.2)

\textnormal{rank}H^{i}(j_{x}^{*}(j_{w!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}))\cong\begin{cases}1&\text{if }ws>w,i=-1,\text{ and }x\in\{w,ws\},\\
1&\text{if }ws<w,i=1,\text{ and }x\in\{w,ws\},\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

We will assume that
ws<w
, but we will compute both
j_{w!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
and
j_{ws!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
Lemma
3.3.5
and Lemma
3.2.9
gives isomorphisms

j_{ws!}\mathcal{K}_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong j_{\overline{w}!}\mathcal{K}_{w,ws}^{\mathcal{L}}[1]\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptj_{w!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong j_{\overline{w}!}\mathcal{K}_{w,ws}^{\mathcal{L}}[-1],

where
\mathcal{K}_{w,ws}^{\mathcal{L}}=\pi_{s}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}
.

We will first prove that
j_{ws}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{w,ws}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\mathcal{K}_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
Under the identification
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\overline{w}}^{s}/U\cong U\backslash(U_{ws}\times\dot{ws}L_{s}/U_{s})
, the sheaf
\mathcal{K}_{w,ws}^{\mathcal{L}}
corresponds to the
U\times U_{s}
-equivariant local system
\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{U_{ws}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{s}
.
4
4

4

Note that
\mathcal{K}_{w,ws}^{\mathcal{L}}
is not literally a sheaf
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\overline{w}}^{s}/U
since
this stack is not locally of finite type. Instead,
\mathcal{K}_{w,ws}^{\mathcal{L}}
is a sheaf on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\overline{w}}^{s}/(U/J_{w})
. We suppress this subtlety here and
throughout similar isomorphisms in this proof to make statements simpler. Since
U
is pro-unipotent, asking for
U
-equivariance is simply a constructibility
constraint which coincides with that imposed by
U/J_{w}
. As a result, this simplification does not lead to any ambiguous sheaf theory.

Similarly, under the isomorphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{ws}/U\cong U\backslash(U_{ws}\times\dot{ws}T)
, the sheaf
\mathcal{K}_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}
corresponds to the
U
-equivariant local
system
\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{U_{ws}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}
.
Since
\mathcal{L}^{s}|_{T}\cong\mathcal{L}
, we have that
j_{ws}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{w,ws}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\mathcal{K}_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}
.

We will now prove that
j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{w,ws}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
Under the isomorphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}/U\cong U\backslash(U_{w}\times\dot{w}T)
, the sheaf
\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
corresponds to the
U
-equivariant local system

\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{U_{w}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}
.
We claim that via the isomorphism
B\dot{s}B/U\cong U_{s}\times\dot{s}T
, there is an isomorphism of local systems
\mathcal{L}^{s}|_{U_{s}\times\dot{s}T}\cong\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{U_{s}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}
.
To this consider the sequence of maps

(3.3.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{s}\times\dot{s}T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{s}/U_{s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{s}/U_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where
i:T\to U_{s}\times\dot{s}T
is the map
i(t)=(1,\dot{s}t)
,
i_{s}
is the inclusion of
B\dot{s}B/U
into
L_{s}/U_{s}\cong P_{s}/U
, and
\mu
is the
isomorphism of varieties given by
\mu(x)=\dot{s}^{-1}x
.
It is clear from its definition that the composition given in
3.3.3
coincides with the inclusion map
B\hookrightarrow P_{s}
.
As a result, we obtain an isomorphism of local systems
\mathcal{L}\cong i^{*}i_{s}^{*}\mathcal{L}^{s}
. Since
i_{s}^{*}\mathcal{L}^{s}
is a rank-1 local system, we conclude that
i_{s}^{*}\mathcal{L}^{s}\cong\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{U_{s}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}
.
Via the isomorphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}/U\cong U\backslash U_{ws}\dot{ws}\times U_{s}\times\dot{s}T
, we can compute the restriction

j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{w,ws}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{U_{ws}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{s}|_{U_{s}\times\dot{s}T}\cong\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{U_{ws}}\boxtimes\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{U_{s}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}\cong\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}},

which completes the stalk computation for
\mathcal{K}_{w,ws}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
∎

Lemma 3.3.7
.

Let
s\in W
be a simple reflection. Then there are natural isomorphisms

\nabla_{s}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\Delta_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{s}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\nabla_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}.

Proof.

By Remark
3.2.7
, it suffices to consider the sheaves
\nabla_{s}^{s\mathcal{L}}
and
\Delta_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
as objects of
{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/s\mathcal{L}}(L_{s},\mathbbm{k})
and
{}_{s\mathcal{L}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(L_{s},\mathbbm{k})
, respectively.
Under this identification, the convolution
\nabla_{s}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\Delta_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
corresponds to the convolution on
U_{s}\backslash L_{s}/U_{s}
.
The result then follows from
[
Gou21
, Lemma 8.3.4]
when
s\notin W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
and Lemma 8.3.7 of
loc. cit.
when
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
∎

Corollary 3.3.8
.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
.
Let
s\in W
be a simple reflection with
s\notin W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.

(1)

The natural maps
\Delta_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\nabla_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
are isomorphisms.

(2)

The functor

(-)\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/s\mathcal{L}}\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}

is an equivalence of triangulated categories which is
t
-exact for the perverse
t
-structure.

(3)

Let
w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{s\mathcal{L}}
, then

\Delta_{w}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}},\hskip 28.45274pt\nabla_{w}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\nabla_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}},\hskip 28.45274pt\IC_{w}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\IC_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}.

Moreover, the obvious analogues of (2) and (3) obtained by convolving on the left by
\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
also hold.

Proof.

(1):
By Remark
3.2.7
, it suffices to prove the statement for
G
semisimple of rank 1. There is an open-closed distinguished triangle

\Delta_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\nabla_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\to j_{e*}j_{e}^{*}\nabla_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\to.

We will show that
j_{e}^{*}\nabla_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong 0
, which will show the desired isomorphism.

Since the forgetful functor
\For_{T\times T,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{-1}}
is conservative, it also suffices to simply forget the twisted equivariance on

\nabla_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
so that
\nabla_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
is a constructible sheaf in
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{L_{s}}
.
Explicitly, under the isomorphism
U_{s}\backslash B_{s}\dot{s}B_{s}\cong\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\dot{s}T
,
\nabla_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
is the
*
-extension of
\mathcal{L}^{L_{s}}\coloneq\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}
to
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{L_{s}}
.

Write
Z^{0}=Z^{0}(L_{s})
for the identity component of the center of
L_{s}
. By
[
Jan03
, Sfrm[o]–.18]
, there is a central isogeny

\nu:Z^{0}\times\SL_{2}\to L_{s}

This induces a map

\nu^{\prime}:Z^{0}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\SL_{2}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{L_{s}}

which satisfies
f(Z^{0}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\SL_{2},w})\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{L_{s},w}
for
w=e,s
. As a result, there is a cartesian diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Z^{0}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\SL_{2},e}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{0}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\SL_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Z^{0}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\SL_{2},s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{L_{s},e}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{L_{s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{L_{s},s}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{e}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{Z^{0}}\times j_{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{s}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{Z^{0}}\times j_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{s}}

where the vertical arrows are smooth, surjective, and with connected fibers.
As a result, in order to show that
j_{e}^{*}\nabla_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong j_{e}^{*}j_{s*}\mathcal{L}^{L_{s}}[2]=0
, we can instead show that
(\nu_{e}^{\prime})^{*}j_{e}^{*}j_{s*}\mathcal{L}^{L_{s}}=0
.
From functorality and smooth base change, we have an isomorphism

(\nu_{e}^{\prime})^{*}j_{e}^{*}j_{s*}\mathcal{L}^{L_{s}}\cong(\id_{Z^{0}}\times j_{e})^{*}(\id_{Z^{0}}\times j_{s})_{*}(\nu_{s}^{\prime})^{*}\mathcal{L}^{L_{s}}.

Recall that
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\SL_{2}}\cong\mathbb{A}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\SL_{2},s}\cong\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
, and
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\SL_{2},e}\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\{0\}
.
Under the above identifications, there are projection maps
p_{s}:\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{L_{s},s}\to T
and
p:\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\SL_{2},s}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
These projection maps are compatible in the sense that the following diagram commutes
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Z^{0}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\SL_{2},s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{L_{s},s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{s}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{s}^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{s}}

Since
\mathcal{L}^{L_{s}}\cong p_{s}^{*}\mathcal{L}
, functorality gives an isomorphism

(\nu_{s}^{\prime})^{*}\mathcal{L}^{L_{s}}\cong p^{*}(\alpha_{s}^{\vee})^{*}\mathcal{L}.

From the definition of
s\notin W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, we have that
(\alpha_{s}^{\vee})^{*}\mathcal{L}
must be a non-trivial rank-one local system
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
on
\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
A simple sheaf functor computation yields isomorphisms

(\id_{Z^{0}}\times j_{e})^{*}(\id_{Z^{0}}\times j_{s})^{*}(\nu_{s}^{\prime})^{*}\mathcal{L}^{L_{s}}\cong\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{Z^{0}}\boxtimes j_{e}^{*}j_{s*}(\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}).

But any non-trivial rank-one local system on
\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
is clean with respect to
\mathbb{A}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
; therefore,
j_{e}^{*}j_{s*}(\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})=0
.

Since the natural map
\Delta_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\nabla_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
is an isomorphism, clearly we will also have natural isomorphisms
\Delta_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\nabla_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
.

(2), (3):
By Lemma
3.3.7
, it is clear that
(-)\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.
Moreover, a combination of Lemma
3.3.7
and Lemma
3.3.4
shows that
\Delta_{w}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}

and
\nabla_{w}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\nabla_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}
for all
w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{s\mathcal{L}}
.
By naturality of these isomorphisms, we have that
\IC_{w}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\IC_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
As a result, it is clear that
(-)\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
is a perverse
t
-exact.
∎

Corollary 3.3.9
.

Let
s\in W
be a simple reflection. Then the functor

(-)\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/s\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})

has right adjoint also given by
(-)\star\IC_{s}^{s\mathcal{L}}
.

Proof.

If
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}
, then the statement follows from the proof of
[
LY20
, Corollary 3.9]
after replacing
[
LY20
, Lemma 3.8]
with Lemma

3.3.5
.
If
s\notin W_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}
, then the statement follows from Corollary
3.3.8
.
∎

We summarize all the various convolution rules for monodromic standards and costandards from this section in the following proposition.

Proposition 3.3.10
.

We have natural isomorphisms

(1)

\Delta_{xy}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{x}^{y(\mathcal{L})}\star\Delta_{y}^{\mathcal{L}}
if
\ell(xy)=\ell(x)+\ell(y)
;

(2)

\nabla_{xy}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\nabla_{x}^{y(\mathcal{L})}\star\nabla_{y}^{\mathcal{L}}
if
\ell(xy)=\ell(x)+\ell(y)
;

(3)

\nabla_{x^{-1}}^{x(\mathcal{L})}\star\Delta_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{x^{-1}}^{x(\mathcal{L})}\star\nabla_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}
.

Proof.

Statements (1) and (2) are the same verbatim as in Lemma
3.3.4
. Statement (3) follows from a combination of (1), (2), and Lemma

3.3.7
.
∎

3.3.11.
Blocks

Fix a
W
-orbit
\mathfrak{o}
in
\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
. Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
.
In general, since
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
need not be equal to
W_{\mathcal{L}}
, one has to be careful when extending results to all of
W_{\mathcal{L}}
. The general strategy will be to
analyze the
blocks and describe how they interact.
The block decomposition of
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
gives rise to a block decomposition of
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
.

Definition 3.3.12
.

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
be a block. Let
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
be the full triangulated subcategory of
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
generated by
\{\Delta_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\}_{w\in\beta}
.
We call
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
the
\beta
-block
of
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
.

When
\beta
is the coset
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
in
W_{\mathcal{L}}
, we will write
{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathbbm{k})
. This is called the
neutral block
of
{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
.

Proposition 3.3.13

(
[
LY20
, Proposition 4.11]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
. We have a direct sum decomposition of triangulated categories

(3.3.4)

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})=\bigoplus_{\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}}{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k}).

Proposition 3.3.14

(
[
LY20
, Proposition 4.13]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
. Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
and
\gamma\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
. If
\mathcal{G}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
and

\mathcal{F}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\gamma}(\mathbbm{k})
,
then
\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{G}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\gamma\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
.
In particular, convolution restricts to a bifunctor

(-)\star(-):{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\gamma}(\mathbbm{k})\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\gamma\beta}(\mathbbm{k}).

3.3.15.
Minimal IC Sheaves

As we saw, the skyscraper sheaf
\IC_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}
acts on the right of
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
as a unit.
We want to construct a version of these sheaves but on each block, rather than just the neutral block.
The definition of the minimal elements in a block
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
suggests that such a replacement should allow us to translate problems in non-neutral blocks to
neutral blocks.
The correct replacement for
\IC_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}
for non-neutral blocks will be the IC-sheaves corresponding to these minimal elements.

Definition 3.3.16
.

For
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
, the sheaf
\IC_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}
is called the
minimal IC sheaf
in
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
.

We have already seen examples of minimal IC sheaves in Corollary
3.3.8
. Namely, if
s\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
is a simple reflection in
W
such that
s\notin W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, then
s
is minimal in the block containing it by Lemma
3.1.7
.
In particular,
\Delta_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\nabla_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
is a minimal IC sheaf. The following propositions provide a generalization of Corollary

3.3.8
to all blocks. An analogous result is provided by
[
LY20
, Proposition 5.2]
, but we provide a slightly different argument.

Proposition 3.3.17
.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
, and let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
.

(1)

The natural maps
\Delta_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\IC_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\nabla_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}
are isomorphisms.

(2)

Let
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
and
\gamma\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
. The functors

\IC_{w^{\gamma}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star(-):{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\gamma\beta}(\mathbbm{k})

and

(-)\star\IC_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\gamma}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\gamma\beta}(\mathbbm{k})

are perverse
t
-exact equivalences of categories.

(3)

The equivalence
\IC_{w^{\gamma}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star(-)
satisfy

\IC_{w^{\gamma}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\Delta_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{w^{\gamma}x}^{\mathcal{L}}\hskip 8.5359pt\IC_{w^{\gamma}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\nabla_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\nabla_{w^{\gamma}x}^{\mathcal{L}}\hskip 8.5359pt\IC_{w^{\gamma}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\IC_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\IC_{w^{\gamma}x}^{\mathcal{L}}

for all
x\in\beta
. Analogous statements for the right convolution by
\IC_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}
also hold.

Proof.

We will prove all 3 statements by induction on
\ell(w^{\beta})
. If
\ell(w^{\beta})=0
, then
w^{\beta}=e
and the statements follow from Lemma
3.3.2
.
Suppose the statements hold for
\ell(w^{\beta})<n
. Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
such that
\ell(w^{\beta})=n
.
We can pick a simple reflection
s\in W
such that
\ell(w^{\beta}s)<\ell(w^{\beta})
. Note that
\ell(w^{\beta}s)<\ell(w^{\beta})
forces
s\notin W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
Let
\beta^{\prime}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{s\mathcal{L}}
be the block containing
w^{\beta}s
.
If
w^{\beta}s\neq w^{\beta^{\prime}}
, then
\ell_{\beta^{\prime}}(w^{\beta^{\prime}})<\ell_{\beta^{\prime}}(w^{\beta}s)
. By
3.1.1
, we must have that
\ell(w^{\beta^{\prime}})<\ell(w^{\beta}s)
.
As a consequence, we have inequalities

\ell(w^{\beta^{\prime}}s)\leq\ell(w^{\beta^{\prime}})+1\leq\ell(w^{\beta}s)<\ell(w^{\beta}).

This gives a contradiction since
w^{\beta^{\prime}}s\in\beta
and
w^{\beta}
is minimal in
\beta
. Therefore,
w^{\beta}s=w^{\beta^{\prime}}
.

By Corollary
3.3.8
(3),

\Delta_{w^{\beta^{\prime}}}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{w^{\beta^{\prime}}s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}.

Similarly,
\nabla_{w^{\beta^{\prime}}}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\nabla_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
Statement (1) then follows from the observation that the natural map
\Delta_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\nabla_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}
factors through isomorphisms

\Delta_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{w^{\beta^{\prime}}}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\nabla_{w^{\beta^{\prime}}}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\nabla_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}.

where the second isomorphism follows from induction.

Statement (2) follows from (1) and Proposition
3.3.10
. We will just prove (3) for standard sheaves. The proofs for the costandard and IC statements are
the same, mutatis mutandis.
By the inductive hypothesis,
\Delta_{w}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\IC_{w^{\beta^{\prime}}}^{s\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{ww^{\beta^{\prime}}}^{s\mathcal{L}}
. A short computation yields

\displaystyle\Delta_{w}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\IC_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle\cong\Delta_{w}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\IC_{w^{\beta^{\prime}}}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle\cong\Delta_{ww^{\beta^{\prime}}}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle\cong\Delta_{ww^{\beta}}

where the third isomorphism follows from Corollary
3.3.8
(3).
∎

3.4.
Parity Complexes

Throughout this section, we assume that
\mathbbm{k}
is a field or a complete local ring.
The goal of this section is to show that parity sheaves exist in the monodromic Hecke category and that convolution preserves parity sheaves.
We have already seen that
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
satisfies the parity conditions in Proposition
3.2.2
, so we already have that if
parity extensions exist than they are unique up to isomorphism.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
. We will write
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
for the full additive subcategory of
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
consisting of parity sheaves.
The category of parity sheaves inherits a block decomposition

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})=\bigoplus_{\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}}{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k}).

We can now state the main two results of this section.

Theorem 3.4.1
.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
. Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
.
For every
w\in\beta
, there is an indecomposable sheaf
\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
satisfying

(1)

\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
is supported on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\leq w}
;

(2)

j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}[\ell(w)]
.

Theorem 3.4.2
.

Convolution sends parity sheaves to parity sheaves, in particular, convolution restricts to a bifunctor between additive categories,

(-)\star(-):{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathbbm{k})\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k}).

These theorems will be proved more-or-less simultaneously. We briefly outline the strategy below.

(1)

Understand how parity changes under convolution by
\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
via Proposition
3.3.17
and Lemma
3.3.5
.

(2)

Use convolution to construct parity extensions.

(3)

Use Theorem
3.4.1
and Proposition
2.1.3
to conclude that convolution preserves parity sheaves.

Lemma 3.4.3
.

Let
s\in W
be a simple reflection and
s\notin W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
The functor
(-)\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/s\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
flips parity, i.e., sends even objects to odd objects (and vice versa).
Therefore,
(-)\star\IC_{s}^{s\mathcal{L}}
restricts to a functor,

(-)\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/s\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})

Proof.

This follows immediately from Corollary
3.3.8
.
∎

Our goal is to establish a similar parity preserving result when
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
. To do so, we will use the push-pull lemma (Lemma

3.3.5
) combined with Proposition
2.2.3
.
By Lemma
3.2.5
, the partial monodromic Hecke category
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{s/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
satisfies the parity conditions.

Lemma 3.4.4
.

The functors
\pi_{s*}
and
\pi_{s}^{*}
take parity sheaves to parity sheaves.

Proof.

We will first prove that
\pi_{s}^{*}
preserves parity sheaves. Let
\mathcal{F}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{s/\mathcal{L}}
be
*
-parity.
By definition,
\pi_{s}^{*}\mathcal{F}
is clearly
*
-parity.
On the other hand, if
\mathcal{G}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{s/\mathcal{L}}
is
!
-parity, then
\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G})\in{}_{(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})^{-1}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{s/\mathcal{L}^{-1}}
is
*
-parity.
Since
\pi_{s}^{!}\cong\pi_{s}^{*}[2]
, it follows that
\pi_{s}^{!}\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G})\cong\pi_{s}^{*}\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G})[2]
is also
*
-parity.

We will now prove that
\pi_{s*}
preserves parity complexes. By Proposition
2.2.3
, it suffices to check that
\pi_{s}
is an even morphism.
This follows from recollement and Lemma
3.2.9
.
∎

Corollary 3.4.5
.

Let
s\in W
be a simple reflection such that
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, the functor
(-)\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})

preserves parity sheaves. As a result, there is a functor

(-)\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})

Proof.

This follows immediately from Lemma
3.3.5
and Lemma
3.4.4
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.4.1
.

Let
w\in\beta
. Let
\underline{w}=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{k})
be a reduced expression for
w
.
Define

\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}=\IC_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}\ldots s_{k}(\mathcal{L})}\star\IC_{s_{2}}^{s_{3}\ldots s_{k}(\mathcal{L})}\star\ldots\star\IC_{s_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}}.

In particular,
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}
.
By Lemma
3.4.3
and Corollary
3.4.5
, we have that
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}
is a
parity sheaf.
Moreover,
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}
is supported on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{\leq w}
.

We claim that
j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}[\ell(w)]
. We will prove this by induction on
k
. The case of
k=1
is obvious.
Let
\underline{x}=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{k-1})
and write
x=xs_{k}
. Assume that
j_{x}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{s_{k}\mathcal{L}}\cong\mathcal{K}_{x}^{s_{k}\mathcal{L}}[\ell(x)]
.
If
s_{k}\notin W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, we are done by Proposition
3.3.17
. As a result, we may assume that
s_{k}\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
By induction, we have a distinguished triangle

(3.4.1)

\Delta_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}\to j_{<x*}j_{<x}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}

where
j_{<x}:\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{<x}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}
is the inclusion map.
Note that
j_{<x*}j_{<x}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}}
is supported on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{<w}
.
As a result, when we apply
j_{w}^{*}\left((-)\star\IC_{s_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}}\right)
to (
3.4.1
), we get an isomorphism
j_{w}^{*}(\Delta_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
By Lemma
3.3.6
, there is an isomorphism
j_{w}^{*}(\Delta_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}})\cong\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}[\ell(w)]
.

The result follows from taking the indecomposable summand
\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
of
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}
such that
j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}[\ell(w)]
.

\square

Proof of Theorem
3.4.2
.

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
and
\gamma\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
.
By Theorem
3.4.1
and Proposition
2.1.3
, one has that
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
is generated under direct sums and
shifts by
\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
for
w\in\beta
.
It suffices to check that for
w\in\beta
and
v\in\gamma
, one has that
\mathcal{E}_{v}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
is parity.
Let
\underline{w}=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{k})
be a reduced expression for
w
. The construction of
\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
in the proof of Theorem
3.4.1
gives that

\mathcal{E}_{v}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
is a direct summand of a shift of
\mathcal{E}_{v}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\IC_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}\ldots s_{k}(\mathcal{L})}\star\ldots\star\IC_{s_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
The latter is a parity sheaf by Lemma
3.4.3
and Corollary
3.4.5
. Since any direct summand of a parity sheaf is
also a parity sheaf, we obtain that
\mathcal{E}_{v}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
is parity.

\square

3.5.
Right Equivariant Hecke Category

Fix a
W
-orbit
\mathfrak{o}
of
\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
, and let
\mathcal{L}\in\mathfrak{o}
.
We call
\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\coloneq D_{\cons}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}/_{\mathcal{L}}B,\mathbbm{k})
the
Hecke category with right equivariant monodromy
\mathcal{L}
.
For
w\in W
, we write
\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(w,\mathbbm{k})=D_{\cons}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}/_{\mathcal{L}}B,\mathbbm{k})
.
As discussed in
[
Gou21
, §3.2]
, there is a direct sum decomposition of triangulated categories

\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\cong\bigoplus_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}}D_{\cons}(T\fatbslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}U\backslash G/U/_{\mathcal{L}}T,\mathbbm{k})

where
D_{\cons}(T\fatbslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}U\backslash G/U/_{\mathcal{L}}T,\mathbbm{k})
are the full subcategories of
\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
generated by the essential image of the forgetful functors

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{For}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k}).

Lemma 3.5.1
.

The Hecke category with right equivariant monodromy
\mathcal{L}
satisfies the following properties:

(1)

There are equivalences of categories

\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(w,\mathbbm{k})\cong D_{\cons}(T/_{\mathcal{L}}T,\mathbbm{k})\cong D(\mathbbm{k}\textnormal{-mod})

obtained by taking the stalk at
\dot{w}
for a lift
\dot{w}\in G
of
w
.

(2)

The category
\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
satisfies the parity conditions.

Proof.

Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}
is
T\times T
-equivariantly isomorphic to
\mathbb{A}^{\ell(w)}\times\dot{w}T
, the first isomorphism of (1) easily follows.
The second isomorphism is standard, for example see the proof of
[
Gou21
, Lemma 7.4.1]
. Statement (2) is routine, and is similar to that of
Proposition
3.2.2
(2).
∎

Routine modifications in the definition of the biequivariant convolution functor produces a right action of
{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
on
\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
. More generally.
there are bifunctors

(-)\star(-):\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathbbm{k})\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})

which are suitably associative.

The following shows how the biequivariant convolution and the above action is intertwined by the forgetful functor. Its proof follows readily from definitions.

Lemma 3.5.2
.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
.
For all
\mathcal{F}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathbbm{k})
and
\mathcal{G}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
, there is a canonical isomorphism

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{For}(\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{G})\cong{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{For}(\mathcal{F})\star\mathcal{G}.

Let
\mathbbm{k}
be a complete local ring or a field.
By Lemma
3.5.1
,
\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
satisfies the parity conditions.
We will write
\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
for the category of parity sheaves in
\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
.
The forgetful functor
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{For}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
takes parity sheaves to parity sheaves.
The following lemma then follows from Theorems
3.4.1
and
3.4.2
.

Lemma 3.5.3
.

Suppose that
\mathbbm{k}
is a complete local ring or a field.
For every
w\in W
, there is a unique indecomposable parity extension
\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
of
\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}[\ell(w)]
in
\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
.
Moreover, convolution restricts to a bifunctor of parity sheaves

(-)\star(-):\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathbbm{k})\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k}).

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
be a block.
We will also consider the full subcategory
\>{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\fatbslash}\Par_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
of
\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
which is generated under shifts and direct sums by
\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}

for
w\in\beta
.
When
\beta
is the neutral block, we will write
\>{}_{\mathcal{L}\fatbslash}\Par_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathbbm{k})\coloneq\>_{\mathcal{L}\fatbslash}\Par_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
.

3.6.
Monodromic Bott-Samelson Hecke Category

The category of sheaves generated by
\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
for
s\in S
under convolution in the monodromic Hecke category are still worthwhile to study even when
\mathbbm{k}
is not
a field or a complete local ring.
The main obstacle that presents itself is the fact that
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
need not be idempotent complete, so parity extensions may not exist.
To worry less about whether idempotents exist or not when
\mathbbm{k}
is not a complete local ring, we introduce the monodromic Bott-Samelson category.

Let
\underline{w}=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{k})
be an expression of
w\in W
. We define the
Bott-Samelson sheaf
,

\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq\IC_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}\ldots s_{k}\mathcal{L}}\star\ldots\star\IC_{s_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}},

which is regarded as either an object of
{}_{w\mathcal{L}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
or
\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
.
Note that when
w\in\beta
for some block
\beta\in{}_{w\mathcal{L}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
, then
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}\in{}_{w\mathcal{L}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
.

Definition 3.6.1
.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
.
Let
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})
denote the full subcategory of
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
whose objects are of the form
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n]
where
\underline{w}
is
an expression for
w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We call
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})
the
monodromic Bott-Samelson category
.

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
be a block. We can define a full subcategory
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
of
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})
whose
objects are of the form
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n]
where
\underline{w}
is an expression for
w\in\beta
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

The Bott-Samelson categories inherit a convolution bifunctor

(-)\star(-):{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\BS,\gamma}(\mathbbm{k})\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\beta}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\gamma\beta}(\mathbbm{k}),

\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[n]\star\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}[m]=\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n+m].

Let
x,y\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
. Let
\underline{x}
and
\underline{y}
be expressions for
x
and
y
.
We will write

\Hom_{{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n],\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[m])\coloneq\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\Hom_{{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n],\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[m+k])

Lemma 3.6.2
.

If
\mathbbm{k}
is a field or a complete local ring, then the Karoubian envelope of the additive hull of
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
is equivalent to

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
.

Proof.

It follows from Theorem
3.4.1
that
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
is a full subcategory of the Karoubian envelope of the additive hull
of
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
.
By Proposition
3.3.13
, any summand of the idempotent completion of
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
is also in the
\beta
-block. The
lemma readily follows.
∎

We can now describe how extension of scalars behaves with respect to the Bott-Samelson category. The following lemma follows from standard properties for parity sheaves
(c.f.
[
MR18
, Lemma 2.2 (2)]
).

Lemma 3.6.3
.

Let
\mathbbm{k}\to\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}
be a ring homomorphism. Let
\underline{x}
and
\underline{y}
be expressions of elements in
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
.

(1)

The graded left
H_{T}^{\bullet}(\pt;\mathbbm{k})
-module

\Hom_{{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n],\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[m])

is free.

(2)

Extension of scalars induces an isomorphism of
H_{T}^{\bullet}(\pt;\mathbbm{k}^{\prime})
-modules,

\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\Hom_{{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n],\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[m])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Hom_{{}_{\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(\mathcal{L})}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k}^{\prime})}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n],\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[m]).

It will also be useful to define a version of the Bott-Samelson category for the right equivariant category of parity sheaves.

Definition 3.6.4
.

Let
\mathcal{L}\in\mathfrak{o}
.
Let
\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})
denote the full category of
\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
whose objects are of the form
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}
where
\underline{w}
is an expression for
w\in W
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We call
\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})
the
right
\mathcal{L}
-monodromic Bott-Samelson category
.

If
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
is a block, we will also consider the full subcategory
\>{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\fatbslash}\Par_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\beta}(\mathbbm{k})
of
\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})
consisting
of objects
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n]
where
\underline{w}
is an expression for
w\in\beta
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
If
\beta=W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
is the neutral block, we will write
\>{}_{\mathcal{L}\fatbslash}\Par_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\beta}(\mathbbm{k})\coloneq\>_{\mathcal{L}\fatbslash}\Par_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}
.

Let
x,y\in W
. Let
\underline{x}
and
\underline{y}
be expressions for
x
and
y
.
We will write

\Hom_{\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n],\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[m])\coloneq\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\Hom_{\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n],\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[m+k]).

We obtain the following variant of Lemma
3.6.3
. It also follows from the same argument given in
[
MR18
, Lemma 2.2 (2)]
.

Lemma 3.6.5
.

Let
\mathbbm{k}\to\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}
be a ring homomorphism. Let
\underline{x}
and
\underline{y}
be expressions of elements in
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
.

(1)

The
\mathbbm{k}
-module

\Hom_{\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n],\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[m])

is free.

(2)

Extension of scalars induces an isomorphism of
\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}
-modules

\Hom_{\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n],\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[m])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Hom_{\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(\mathcal{L})}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k}^{\prime})}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n],\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[m]).

(3)

The forgetful functor
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{For}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
induces an isomorphism of graded
\mathbbm{k}
-modules

\mathbbm{k}\otimes_{H_{T}^{\bullet}(\pt;\mathbbm{k})}\Hom_{{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n],\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[m])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Hom_{\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS}(\mathbbm{k})}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n],\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[m]).

3.7.
Bicategorical Variants

Up until this point, we have mostly concerned ourselves with the 1-categorical and monoidal structure of the monodromic Hecke category.
It is useful to reformulate this in terms of bicategorical language.
While the monodromic Bott-Samelson categories can be assembled into a 2-category, the behavior of blocks is somewhat opaque.
In particular, the non-neutral block monodromic-endoscopic equivalence (Theorem
5.11.1
) is stated for parity sheaves rather than Bott-Samelson sheaves.
As a result, for this section, we will impose the constraint that
\mathbbm{k}
is a complete local ring or a field.
We will explain the necessary foundations from category theory before explaining how to construct bicategorical versions of the monodromic Hecke category.

3.7.1.
Bicategorical Preliminaries

Let
\mathfrak{C}
denote a (weak) 2-category.
5
5

5

Classically, the notion of a 2-category is synonymous with that of a
strict
2-category. Our notion of 2-category
has classically been called a bicategory and does not require that horizontal composition be strictly associative. C.f.
[
LY20
, §10.4]
for further discussion on this
topic.
Let
x,y\in\Ob(\mathfrak{C})
, we will write
{}_{y}\mathfrak{C}_{x}
for the morphism category
\Hom_{\mathfrak{C}}(x,y)
.
We can associate an ordinary 1-category from
\mathfrak{C}
, denoted
\pi_{\leq 1}\mathfrak{C}
. It has the same object set as
\mathfrak{C}
, but the morphism sets
\Hom_{\pi_{\leq 1}\mathfrak{C}}(x,y)
are given by the
isomorphism classes of objects in
{}_{y}\mathfrak{C}_{x}
.
Let
\Gamma
be a small groupoid. A
2-category over
\Gamma
is a 2-category
\mathfrak{C}
along with a functor
\omega:\pi_{\leq 1}\mathfrak{C}\to\Gamma
.
Note that any small groupoid
\Gamma
can be regarded as a 2-category over itself.

If
(\mathfrak{C},\omega)
is a 2-category over
\Gamma
, and
x,y\in\Ob(\mathfrak{C}),\xi\in{}_{\omega(y)}\Gamma_{\omega(x)}
, we define a full subcategory
{}_{y}\mathfrak{C}_{x}^{\xi}
of
{}_{y}\mathfrak{C}_{x}

consisting of objects which map to
\xi
under
\omega
.
It can be easily checked that composition restricts to a bifunctor

\circ:{}_{z}\mathfrak{C}_{y}^{\eta}\times{}_{y}\mathfrak{C}_{x}^{\xi}\to{}_{z}\mathfrak{C}_{x}^{\eta\xi}

for all
x,y,z\in\Ob(\mathfrak{C})
,
\xi\in{}_{\omega(y)}\Gamma_{\omega(x)}
, and
\eta\in{}_{\omega(z)}\Gamma_{\omega(y)}
.
When
\xi=\id_{\omega(x)}\in{}_{\omega(x)}\Gamma_{\omega(x)}
, we will often denote
{}_{x}\mathfrak{C}_{x}^{\circ}\coloneq{}_{x}\mathfrak{C}_{x}^{\id_{\omega(x)}}
.

3.7.2.
Monodromic Hecke 2-Categories

We will now apply these categorical preliminaries to construct 2-categorical versions of the monodromic Hecke category.
We will fix a
W
-orbit
\mathfrak{o}
of
\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
.
Let
\Xi
denote the groupoid defined as follows:

(1)

\Ob(\Xi)=\mathfrak{o}

(2)

\Hom_{\Xi}(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})={}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\Xi_{\mathcal{L}}=\{w^{\beta}\in W\mid\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}\}
.

We can construct a 2-category
{}_{\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/}(\mathbbm{k})
(resp.
{}_{\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/}(\mathbbm{k})
) over
\Xi
. Namely, the object set is
given by
\mathfrak{o}
.
The morphisms from
\mathcal{L}
to
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
are given by the categories
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
(resp.
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
)
where composition is given by convolution. By Proposition
3.3.14
and
3.4.2
, this data ensures that
{}_{\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/}(\mathbbm{k})

(resp.
{}_{\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/}(\mathbbm{k})
) is a well-defined 2-category over
\Xi
.

3.8.
Monodromic Hecke Algebra

The monodromic Hecke algebra was defined by Lusztig in the study of non-unipotent character sheaves
[
Lus19
,
Lus16
]
.
It serves as an analogue for the Hecke algebra for non-unipotent representations of finite reductive groups.
The semisimple complexes in the monodromic Hecke category give a categorification of the monodromic Hecke algebra. This gives rise to a geometric interpretation for the
canonical basis.

Some complications arise when moving from finite Weyl groups to infinite ones. Namely, the monodromic Hecke algebra is no longer well-defined due to some sums in the
defining relations having infinitely many terms.
This is a shadow of the fact that the monodromic Hecke category failing to be monoidal due to its lack of a unit.
As we saw in the previous section, the monodromic Hecke category can be reformulated into a bicategory.
The decategorification of a 2-category is a 1-category, and in this way, one expects that the monodromic Hecke bicategory decategorifies into a 1-categorical enhancement
of the monodromic Hecke algebra called the
monodromic Hecke algebroid
.
6
6

6

This should not be confused by the Williamson’s Hecke algebroid which is the
decategorification of the 2-category of singular Soergel bimodules.

As foreshadowed by the usual characteristic
p
story, the correct incarnation of the monodromic Hecke category will be via parity sheaves.

The monodromic Hecke algebroid possesses all the usual features of the Hecke algebra.
Namely, our categorification provides a theory of
p
-canonical bases and along with it
p
-Kazhdan-Lusztig cells.
If
G
is a reductive group, then we will be able to reduce to a 1-categorification of Lusztig’s monodromic Hecke algebra.

3.8.1.
Definitions

Definition 3.8.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{o}
be a
W
-orbit in
\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
.
The
monodromic Hecke algebroid
of
W
with monodromy
\mathfrak{o}
, denoted by
\mathbf{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}
, is a
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-linear category whose objects are elements
of
\mathfrak{o}
.
We write
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathfrak{o}}
for the morphism space
\Hom_{\mathbf{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}}(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
.
It is defined as the free
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-module with basis
\{T_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\}_{w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}}
.
Composition in
\mathbf{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}
is defined by the generating relation

(3.8.1)

T_{s}^{x\mathcal{L}}T_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq\begin{cases}T_{sx}^{\mathcal{L}}&sx>x,\\
(v^{2}-1)T_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}+v^{2}T_{sx}^{\mathcal{L}}&sx<x,s\in W_{x\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},\\
v^{2}T_{sx}^{\mathcal{L}}&sx<x,s\notin W_{x\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}.\end{cases}

We have the following inversion formula,

(T_{s}^{\mathcal{L}})^{-1}=\begin{cases}v^{-2}T_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}+(v^{-2}-1)T_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}&s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},\\
v^{-2}T_{s}^{s\mathcal{L}}&s\notin W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}.\end{cases}

3.8.3.
Modular Categorification

Our main result of this section gives a categorification of the monodromic Hecke algebroid in terms of parity sheaves.
For this section, we will assume that
\mathbbm{k}
is a field or a complete local ring.

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be a
\mathbb{Z}
-linear 2-category. We can define its
split Grothendieck algebroid

K_{\oplus}(\mathfrak{C})
as the category whose object set is the same as
\mathfrak{C}
.
The morphisms are given by taking the split Grothendieck group of the morphism categories in
\mathfrak{C}
.

Theorem 3.8.4
.

There is an equivalence of categories

\ch:K_{\oplus}({}_{\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/}(\mathbbm{k}))\to\mathbf{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}

defined by

(3.8.2)

\ch([\mathcal{F}])=\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}}}{{i\in\mathbb{Z}}}}(\textnormal{rank}H^{i}(j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{F}))v^{i}T_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}

for
\mathcal{F}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
. The equivalence satisfies
\ch([\mathcal{F}[1]])=v^{-1}\ch([\mathcal{F}])
. Moreover, the set
\{\ch([\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}])\}_{w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}}
forms a
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-basis for
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathfrak{o}}
.

Proof.

Almost all the work in proving the theorem is in establishing that
\ch
is actually a functor. We will delay the proof of this fact until the end.
It will be useful to also consider
\ch:K_{\oplus}({}_{\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/}(\mathbbm{k}))\to\mathbf{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}
(not necessarily a functor) defined by the same formula as in
(
3.8.2
).

Suppose
\ch
is a (not necessarily
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-linear) functor. Clearly,
\ch
is essentially surjective.
At the level of morphisms,
\ch
is a well-defined homomorphism of abelian groups which satisfies
\ch([\mathcal{F}[1]])=v^{-1}\ch(\mathcal{F})
. As a result,
\ch
is a homomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-modules where
v^{n}[\mathcal{F}]=[\mathcal{F}[-n]]
.
In particular,
\ch
is
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-linear.
By Proposition
3.4.1
, we have that

\ch([\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}])\in v^{-\ell(w)}T_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}+\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x\in{}_{w\mathcal{L}}W_{\mathcal{L}}}}{{x<w}}}\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]T_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}.

Since
\{T_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}\}_{w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}}
is a
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-basis for
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathfrak{o}}
, by the above equation we have that

\{\ch([\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}])\}_{w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}}
is a
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-basis for
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathfrak{o}}
.
Therefore,
\ch
is fully faithful and an equivalence of categories.

We will now show that
\ch
is actually a functor.

Step 1. Let
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}\to
be a distinguished triangle of
*
-even (resp.
*
-odd) complexes. Then
\ch([\mathcal{F}])=\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime}])+\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}])
.

Since all three terms are
*
-even (resp.
*
-odd), after applying
H^{i}(j_{w}^{*}(-))
to the distinguished triangle, we get short exact sequences

0\to H^{i}(j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\to H^{i}(j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{F})\to H^{i}(j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime})\to 0.

The claim follows from the rank being additive on short exact sequences.

Step 2. Let
\mathcal{F}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/s\mathcal{L}}
be a
*
-even object. Then we have
\ch([\mathcal{F}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}])=\ch([\mathcal{F}])\ch([\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}])
for
s\in W
a
simple reflection
.
By Step 1 and Proposition
3.2.2
, it suffices to take
\mathcal{F}=j_{w!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{s\mathcal{L}}[2n]
for
w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{s\mathcal{L}}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Let
k=\ell(ws)-\ell(w)
. If
s\notin W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, then by Proposition
3.3.17
,

\ch([j_{w!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{s\mathcal{L}}[2n]]\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}})=\ch([j_{ws!}\mathcal{K}_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}[2n+k]])=v^{-2n-k}T_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}.

On the other hand, directly from the definitions we see that

\ch([j_{w!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{s\mathcal{L}}[2n]])=v^{-2n}T_{w}^{s\mathcal{L}}\hskip 14.22636pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\ch([\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}])=v^{-1}T_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}.

Using the defining relations for the monodromic Hecke algebroid, we get equalities

\ch([j_{w!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{s\mathcal{L}}[2n]])\ch([\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}])=\begin{cases}v^{-2n-1}T_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}&k=1,\\
v^{-2n+1}T_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}&k=-1.\end{cases}

Suppose
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
. By Lemma
3.3.6
, we have that

\ch([j_{w!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}[2n]]\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}})=v^{-2n-k}(T_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}+T_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}).

Using the defining relations in the monodromic Hecke algebroid, we get equalities

\ch([j_{w!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}[2n]])\ch([\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}])=\begin{cases}v^{-2n-1}(T_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}+T_{w}^{\mathcal{L}})&k=1,\\
v^{-2n+1}(T_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}+T_{w}^{\mathcal{L}})&k=-1.\end{cases}

Therefore, for all simple reflections
s
, we have that

\ch([j_{w!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{s\mathcal{L}}[2n]])\ch([\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}])=\ch([j_{w!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{s\mathcal{L}}[2n]\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}]),

which completes step 2.

Step 3. Let
\mathcal{F}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}
, we have that
\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\star\mathcal{F}])=\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime}])\ch([\mathcal{F}]).
Therefore,
\ch
is a functor.

Since
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is a direct sum of a
*
-even object and
*
-object, without loss of generality, we may assume that
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is itself
*
-even (the
*
-odd case can be
recovered from
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-linearity).
It is also enough to prove the claim for
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
. We will induct on
\ell(w)
.
If
\ell(w)=0
, i.e.,
w=e
, then since
\ch([\IC_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}])=T_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}
the claim is obvious.
If
\ell(w)=1
, i.e.,
w=s
is a simple reflection, the claim follows from Step 2.

Suppose
\ell(w)>1
. Pick a reduced expression
w=s_{1}\ldots s_{k}
. By Theorem
3.4.1
, we have a decomposition

\IC_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}\ldots s_{k}\mathcal{L}}\star\ldots\star\IC_{s_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}}=\IC_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\oplus\mathcal{F}_{<w}

where
\mathcal{F}_{<w}
is a direct sum of shifts of
\mathcal{E}_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}
for
x<w
and
x\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
.
By induction, we have

\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\star\mathcal{F}_{<w}])=\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime}])\ch(\mathcal{F}_{<w}).

On the other hand, by iterating Step 2, we have that

\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\star\IC_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}\ldots s_{k}\mathcal{L}}\star\ldots\star\IC_{s_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}}])=\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime}])\ch([\IC_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}\ldots s_{k}\mathcal{L}}])\ldots\ch([\IC_{s_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}}]).

As a result,

\displaystyle\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\star\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}])

\displaystyle=\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\star\IC_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}\ldots s_{k}\mathcal{L}}\star\ldots\star\IC_{s_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}}])-\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\star\mathcal{F}_{<w}])

\displaystyle=\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime}])\ch([\IC_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}\ldots s_{k}\mathcal{L}}\star\ldots\star\IC_{s_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}}])-\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime}])\ch([\mathcal{F}_{<w}])

\displaystyle=\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime}])(\ch([\IC_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}\ldots s_{k}\mathcal{L}}\star\ldots\star\IC_{s_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}}])-\ch([\mathcal{F}_{<w}]))

\displaystyle=\ch([\mathcal{F}^{\prime}])\ch([\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}]).

This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Let
p
be 0 or a prime number, and let
\mathbbm{k}
be either a field of characteristic
p
or a complete Noetherian local ring of finite global dimension and residue characteristic
p
.
Let

{}^{p}\underline{H}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq\ch([\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}])\in{}_{w\mathcal{L}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathfrak{o}}.

By Theorem
3.8.4
, the set
\{{}^{p}\underline{H}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\}_{w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}}
is a basis for
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathfrak{o}}
.
We call this basis the
monodromic
p
-Kazhdan-Lusztig basis
. After writing in terms of the standard basis, we obtain expressions

{}^{p}\underline{H}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}=v^{-\ell(w)}\sum_{x\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}}{}^{p}P_{x,w}^{\mathcal{L}}T_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}.

The coefficients
{}^{p}P_{x,w}^{\mathcal{L}}\in\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
are called the
monodromic
p
-Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials
.
If
p=0
, we will write
\underline{H}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}:={}^{0}\underline{H}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
and
P_{x,w}^{\mathcal{L}}:={}^{0}P_{x,w}^{\mathcal{L}}
.

Remark 3.8.5
.

Other notions from
p
-Kazhdan-Lusztig theory should have obvious analogues in the monodromic setting. Most notably, there is a theory of
p
-Kazhdan-Lusztig cells.
Just as Theorem
5.11.1
dictates how monodromic
p
-Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials are computed from non-monodromic
p
-Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials,
it also gives a means of computing
p
-cells in the monodromic setting.

3.8.6.
Reduction for Reductive Groups

We will now explain how when
G
is a reductive group or more generally if
\mathfrak{o}
is a finite
W
-orbit, then Theorem
3.8.4
can be simplified to a
categorification of Lusztig’s monodromic Hecke algebra.

Definition 3.8.7
.

Let
\mathfrak{o}
be a finite order
W
-orbit in
\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
.
The
monodromic Hecke algebra
of
W
with monodromy
\mathfrak{o}
, denoted by
\mathcal{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}
, is a unital associative
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-algebra defined as follows.
The generators of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}
are
T_{w}(w\in W)
and
1_{\mathcal{L}}
(
\mathcal{L}\in\mathfrak{o}
). The multiplication is defined by the relations

(3.8.3)

\displaystyle 1_{\mathcal{L}}1_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}1_{\mathcal{L}},

\displaystyle\text{for }\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o};

(3.8.4)

\displaystyle T_{x}T_{y}

\displaystyle=T_{xy},

\displaystyle\text{if }x,y\in W\text{ and }\ell(xy)=\ell(x)+\ell(y);

(3.8.5)

\displaystyle T_{x}1_{\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=1_{x\mathcal{L}}T_{x},

\displaystyle\text{for }x\in W,\mathcal{L}\in\mathfrak{o};

(3.8.6)

\displaystyle T_{s}^{2}

\displaystyle=v^{2}T_{e}+(v^{2}-1)\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}\in\mathfrak{o}\textnormal{ s.t.}}}{{s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}}}T_{s}1_{\mathcal{L}},

\displaystyle\text{ for simple reflections }s\in W;

(3.8.7)

\displaystyle T_{e}

\displaystyle=1=\sum_{\mathcal{L}\in\mathfrak{o}}1_{\mathcal{L}}.

Notation 3.8.8
.

The
\mathfrak{o}
-monodromic Hecke 1-category is defined as the direct sum of additive categories

{}_{\backslash}^{1}\textnormal{Par}_{/}(\mathbbm{k})=\bigoplus_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}}{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})

By Theorem
3.4.2
,
{}_{\backslash}^{1}\textnormal{Par}_{/}
is a monoidal category.

As a
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-module,
\mathcal{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}
is a free module with basis
\{T_{w}1_{\mathcal{L}}\mid(w,\mathcal{L})\in W\times\mathfrak{o}\}
.
The monodromic Hecke algebra has an involution
\bar{\>}:\mathcal{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}
defined by
\overline{v}=v^{-1}
,
\overline{T_{w}}=T_{w^{-1}}^{-1}
, and
\overline{1_{\mathcal{L}}}=1_{\mathcal{L}}
for all
w\in W
and
\mathcal{L}\in\mathfrak{o}
.

Since
\mathfrak{o}
is finite, we have that
\bigoplus_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}}\Hom_{\mathbf{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}}(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
is a ring with unit given by
\sum_{\mathcal{L}\in\mathfrak{o}}T_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
The following lemma follows from definitions of the monodromic Hecke algebra/algebroid.

Lemma 3.8.9
.

The
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-module map

\varphi:\mathcal{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}\to\bigoplus_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}}\Hom_{\mathbf{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}}(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}).

T_{w}1_{\mathcal{L}}\mapsto T_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\in\Hom_{\mathbf{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}}(\mathcal{L},w\mathcal{L})

is a ring isomorphism.

Corollary 3.8.10
.

The equivalence of categories

\ch:K_{\oplus}({}_{\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/}(\mathbbm{k}))\to\mathbf{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}

induces a ring isomorphism

\ch:K_{\oplus}({}_{\backslash}^{1}\textnormal{Par}_{/}(\mathbbm{k}))\to\mathcal{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}.

The set
\{\ch([\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}])\}_{w\in W,\mathcal{L}\in\mathfrak{o}}
forms a
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-basis for
\mathcal{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathfrak{o}
is finite and by Theorem
3.8.4
, we have an isomorphism of rings

K_{\oplus}({}_{\backslash}^{1}\textnormal{Par}_{/}(\mathbbm{k}))\cong\bigoplus_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}}\Hom_{\mathbf{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}}(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}).

The result, then follows from Lemma
3.8.9
.
∎

Remark 3.8.11
.

Note that Verdier duality for parity sheaves does not give rise to the bar involution on the monodromic Hecke algebra.
In particular, if
\mathcal{L}\in\mathfrak{o}
, it can be the case that
\mathcal{L}^{-1}\notin\mathfrak{o}
.
Nonetheless, the
p
-canonical basis is self-dual under the bar involution (see for example,
[
LY20
, §3.14]
).
In order to give a geometric interpretation, consider the map

\textnormal{inv}:T\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}/_{\mathcal{L}}B\to T\backslash_{(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})^{-1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}/_{\mathcal{L}^{-1}}B

given by taking the identity on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}
and
\varphi:T\times B\to T\times B
defined by
\varphi(a,b)=(a^{-1},b^{-1})
.
Define a functor
\overline{\mathbb{D}}\coloneq\textnormal{inv}^{*}\mathbb{D}:(\>_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}D_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathbbm{k}))^{\op}\to\>_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}D_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathbbm{k})
.
It is easy to check that
\overline{\mathbb{D}}(\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}})\cong\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
for all
s\in S
.
As a consequence,
\overline{\mathbb{D}}(\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}})\cong\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
for all
w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
.
It then follows that under Corollary
3.8.10
that for
\mathcal{E}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
we have
\ch([\overline{\mathbb{D}}(\mathcal{E})])=\overline{\ch([\mathcal{E}])}
.

3.8.12.
Graded Hom Formulas

Let
s
be a simple reflection. Define an element of
{}_{s\mathcal{L}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{L}}
,

\underline{H}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}=\begin{cases}v^{-1}T_{s}+v^{-1}T_{e}&s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},\\
v^{-1}T_{s}&s\notin W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}.\end{cases}

For an expression
\underline{w}=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{k})
, we define

\underline{H}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}=\underline{H}_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}\ldots s_{k}\mathcal{L}}\ldots\underline{H}_{s_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}}.

Note that as defined in Theorem
3.8.4
,
\underline{H}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}=\ch([\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}])
.

We can define the pairing

\langle-,-\rangle:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{L}}\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{L}}\to\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]

by the relation
\langle T_{x},T_{y}\rangle=\delta_{x,y}
.
The pairing
\langle-,-\rangle
is called the
standard form
on
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{L}}
. It is a
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-linear form.
It satisfies a version of biadjunction: for all
A\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{L}}
,
B\in{}_{s(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})}\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{L}}
, and simple reflections
s
, we have

(3.8.8)

\langle\underline{H}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}A,B\rangle=\langle A,\underline{H}_{s}^{s(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})}B\rangle.

The following is a monodromic analogue of Soergel’s Hom formula for parity sheaves
[
MR18
, §3.10]
.

Proposition 3.8.13
.

For any expressions
\underline{x},\underline{y}
of elements in
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
, we have that
\Hom(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n])
is free of finite rank.
Moreover, the graded rank of these Hom-spaces is given by

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\textnormal{rank}_{\mathbbm{k}}\Hom(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n])v^{n}=\langle\underline{H}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}},\underline{H}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}\rangle.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
3.6.3

that
\Hom^{\bullet}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}})
is a graded free left
R
-module of finite rank.
As a result, in order to compute the graded
\mathbbm{k}
-rank of the Hom-space, it suffices to take
\mathbbm{k}
to be a field.

By (
3.8.8
) and Corollary
3.3.9
, it suffices to consider
\underline{x}=\varnothing
and
\underline{y}
an expression for an element in

{}_{\mathcal{L}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
.
A routine argument produces isomorphisms of graded
\mathbbm{k}
-modules
[
MR18
, §3.10]
,

\Hom^{\bullet}(\mathcal{E}_{\varnothing}^{\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}})\cong H^{\bullet}(j_{e}^{!}\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}})

Moreover, by Verdier self-duality of
\IC
-sheaves, we have that
H^{n}(j_{e}^{!}\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}})
and

H^{-n}(j_{e}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}})
have the same ranks.
As a result, by Theorem
3.8.4
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\textnormal{rank}_{\mathbbm{k}}\Hom(\mathcal{E}_{\varnothing}^{\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n])v^{n}

\displaystyle=\langle T_{e}^{\mathcal{L}},\ch([\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}])\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle T_{e}^{\mathcal{L}},\underline{H}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}\rangle.

∎

3.8.14.
Decategorified Endoscopy

Definition 3.8.15
.

Let
\mathfrak{o}
be a
W
-orbit in
\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
. The
endoscopic Hecke algebroid
of
W
, denoted by
\mathbf{E}^{\mathfrak{o}}
, is a
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-linear category
whose objects are
\mathfrak{o}
.
Write
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{L}}
for the morphism space
\Hom_{\mathbf{E}^{\mathfrak{o}}}(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
.
As a
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-module it is defined by

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{L}}=\bigoplus_{\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}}{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}

in which
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}=\mathcal{H}(W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
and
\mathcal{H}(W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
denotes the usual Hecke algebra for the endoscopic Weyl
group
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
Composition is defined via

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\gamma}\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\gamma\beta}

(A,B)\mapsto\beta^{-1}(A)\cdot B

where
\cdot
is the product in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}
and
\beta^{-1}:\mathcal{H}(W_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ})\to\mathcal{H}(W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
is the algebra isomorphism induced
by the isomorphism of Coxeter groups
W_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}\to W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
given by conjugation by
w^{\beta,-1}
.

The endoscopic Hecke algebroid was essentially introduced in
[
Lus19
, §1.6]
for finite Weyl groups.
7
7

7

In
loc. cit.
, Lusztig defines an algebra rather
than a category. There is really no difference, and Lusztig’s definition can be recovered from ours via the same process as Lemma
3.8.9
.

In particular, the work in checking that it is well-defined is covered in
loc. cit.

Note that
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}
is a free
\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
-module with basis
\tilde{T}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
for
w\in\beta
. Here
\tilde{T}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
denotes
the standard basis element indexed by
w
in
\mathcal{H}(W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
.

We recall the decategorified version of the endoscopic equivalence we will later encounter in Theorem
5.11.1
. The result is due to Lusztig for
monodromic Hecke algebras (i.e., finite Weyl groups).
Lusztig’s argument translates without any changes, so we omit it.

Proposition 3.8.16

(
[
Lus19
, 1.6(a)]
)
.

There is a well-defined functor

\theta:\mathbf{E}^{\mathfrak{o}}\to\mathbf{H}^{\mathfrak{o}}

defined by

\tilde{T}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\mapsto T_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}.

Moreover,
\theta
is an isomorphism of categories.

Corollary 3.8.17
.

Let
\langle-,-\rangle_{\beta}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}\to\mathbb{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
denote the standard form of

\mathcal{H}(W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
.
Then for all
A,B\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}
, we have equalities

\langle\theta(A),\theta(B)\rangle=\langle A,B\rangle_{\beta}

Let
t
be a simple reflection in
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
which is not necessarily simple in
W
. Define an element of
{}_{\mathcal{L}}\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{L}}
,

\underline{\tilde{H}}_{t}^{\mathcal{L}}=v^{-1}T_{t}+v^{-1}T_{e}.

For an expression
\underline{w}=(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k})
of an element
w\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, we define

\underline{\tilde{H}}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}=\underline{\tilde{H}}_{t_{1}}^{\mathcal{L}}\ldots\underline{\tilde{H}}_{t_{k}}^{\mathcal{L}}.

Let
\underline{w}^{\prime}
be an expression of
w
in terms of simple reflections in
W
obtained by replacing the
t_{i}
in
\underline{w}
with reduced expressions in
W
.
It then follows from Proposition
3.8.16
that

\theta\left(\underline{\tilde{H}}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}\right)=\underline{H}_{\underline{w}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{L}}.

Corollary 3.8.18
.

Let
\underline{x}
and
\underline{y}
be expressions of simple reflections in
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
Let
\underline{x}^{\prime}
and
\underline{y}^{\prime}
be expressions of simple reflections in
W
obtained from
\underline{x}
and
\underline{y}
by substituting simple reflections in
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
with reduced
expressions in
W
.
Then

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\textnormal{rank}_{\mathbbm{k}}\Hom(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n])v^{n}=\langle\underline{\tilde{H}}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}},\underline{\tilde{H}}_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}}\rangle.

3.9.
Mixed Categories

The goal of this section is to construct a mixed version of the monodromic Hecke category.
We will prove various mixed versions of statements from §
3.3
.
Throughout this section we assume that
\mathbbm{k}
is a field or a complete local ring.

3.9.1.
Biequivariant Category

Consider the mixed derived category defined in §
2.3
,

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{m}(\mathbbm{k})\coloneq K^{b}{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k}).

It inherits a convolution product

\star:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{m}(\mathbbm{k})\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{m}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{m}(\mathbbm{k})

inherited from the convolution of parity sheaves (Theorem
3.4.2
).
For all
w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
, we can define the
standard
and
costandard
sheaves

\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq j_{w!}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}(\ell(w))\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\underline{\nabla}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq j_{w*}\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}(\ell(w)).

When
s\in S
is a simple reflection, we can give an explicit description of the chain complex for
\underline{\Delta}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
and
\underline{\nabla}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}

using the construction given in
[
AR16
, Lemma 2.4]
.
If
s\not\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, there are isomorphisms

\underline{\Delta}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\underline{\nabla}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}.

If
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, there are isomorphisms

(3.9.1)

where the bullet marks indicate the terms in cohomological degree 0, and the morphisms are those induced by the adjunction unit/counit morphisms.

3.9.2.
Parabolic Category

Let
\mathfrak{o}
be a
W
-orbit in
\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
. Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}

Let
s\in S
be a simple reflection such that
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
We can then extend
\mathcal{L}
to a multiplicative local system
\mathcal{L}^{s}\in\Ch(L_{s},\mathbbm{k})
via Lemma
3.2.4
.
Consider the mixed derived category,

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{s/\mathcal{L}}^{m}(\mathbbm{k})\coloneq K^{b}\left({}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{s/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\right).

where
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{s/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
is the full subcategory of
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{s/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
whose objects are parity sheaves.
For all
\overline{w}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(W/\langle s\rangle)_{\mathcal{L}}
, we can define the
standard
and
costandard
sheaves

\underline{\Delta}_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}\coloneq j_{\overline{w}!}\mathcal{K}_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}(\ell(w))\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\underline{\nabla}_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}\coloneq j_{\overline{w}*}\mathcal{K}_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}(\ell(w)).

Recall that there is a proper, smooth, and even morphism
\pi_{s}:\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}/_{\mathcal{L}}B\to\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}/_{\mathcal{L}^{s}}P_{s}
of twisted ind-algebraic stacks.
This gives to functors (see Lemma
3.4.4
),

\pi_{s}^{*}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{s/\mathcal{L}}^{m}(\mathbbm{k})\rightleftarrows{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{m}(\mathbbm{k}):\pi_{s*}.

Moreover, we have that
\pi_{s}^{*}
has left adjoint
\pi_{s*}(-2)
.

3.9.3.
Convolution of Standards and Costandards

Lemma 3.9.4
.

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
be a block. There is an equivalence of categories

\underline{\Delta}_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star(-):{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{m}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{m}(\mathbbm{k})

Moreover, for all
w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
, there are isomorphisms

\underline{\Delta}_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\underline{\Delta}_{w^{\beta}w}^{\mathcal{L}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\underline{\Delta}_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\underline{\nabla}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\underline{\nabla}_{w^{\beta}w}^{\mathcal{L}}.

Proof.

It is obvious from Proposition
3.3.17
that
\underline{\Delta}_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star(-)
is an equivalence of categories.

We will now prove that
\underline{\Delta}_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\underline{\Delta}_{w^{\beta}w}^{\mathcal{L}}
. The costandard version follows from a similar argument.
Let
\mathcal{F}^{i}
denote the degree
i
term in the complex for
\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
By Proposition
3.3.17
, we have that

\underline{\Delta}_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\mathcal{E}_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\mathcal{E}_{w^{\beta}x}^{\mathcal{L}}

for all
x\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
. As a result, for each
s\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
, by computing on each term of
j_{w^{\beta}x}^{*}(\underline{\Delta}_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}})
, we obtain an isomorphism

\displaystyle\Hom(\underline{\Delta}_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}},\underline{\nabla}_{w^{\beta}x}^{\mathcal{L}}(i)[n])

\displaystyle\cong\Hom(\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}},\underline{\nabla}_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}(i)[n])

\displaystyle\cong\begin{cases}\mathbbm{k}&x=w,n=0,i\geq 0\text{ even},\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

By recollement, this gives an isomorphism
\underline{\Delta}_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\underline{\Delta}_{w^{\beta}w}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
∎

Lemma 3.9.5
.

Let
s
be a simple reflection such that
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
. Let
w\in W
such that
ws<w
.

(1)

There are isomorphisms

\pi_{s*}\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\underline{\Delta}_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}(-1)\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\pi_{s*}\underline{\Delta}_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\underline{\Delta}_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}.

(2)

Let
\eta:\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\pi_{s}^{*}\underline{\Delta}_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}(1)
and
\epsilon:\pi_{s}^{*}\underline{\Delta}_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}(1)\to\underline{\Delta}_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}(1)
be the maps induced from the isomorphisms in (1) and the unit and counit maps for
\pi_{s*}
, respectively.
Then there exists a morphism
f:\underline{\Delta}_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}(1)\to\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}[1]
such that

\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta}}{{\to}}\pi_{s}^{*}\underline{\Delta}_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}(1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\epsilon}}{{\to}}\underline{\Delta}_{ws}^{\mathcal{L}}(1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}

is a distinguished triangle.

Proof.

The first statement follows from the same argument given in Lemma
3.2.9
.

We will now prove the second statement.
Consider
\mathcal{K}_{w,ws}^{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq\pi_{s}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{\overline{w}}^{s,\mathcal{L}}
. Since
\pi_{s}^{*}
preserves parity,
\mathcal{K}_{w,ws}^{\mathcal{L}}
is a parity sheaf.
Let
