s_{j}\cdot(\alpha_{i}^{\vee}+2X_{*}(T))\subseteq\alpha_{i}^{\vee}+2X_{*}(T)
.
As a result, for all
u\in W
,
f(u\cdot\alpha_{j}^{\vee})=-1
, and so
u\alpha_{j}^{\vee}\notin\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{\vee}
.
By
[
Kum02
, Lemma 1.3.14]
,
\{\alpha\in\Phi^{\vee,+}\mid v\alpha<0\}
consists of elements of the form
u\cdot\alpha_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
for all but one element.
In particular,

\{\alpha\in\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}^{\vee,+}\mid v\alpha<0\}=\{\alpha\in\Phi^{\vee,+}\mid v\alpha<0\}\cap\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}=\{ws_{3}\alpha_{4}^{\vee}\}.

By Lemma
3.1.2
, we conclude that
ws_{3}s_{4}s_{3}w^{-1}
is endosimple for all
w\in W^{\prime}
.
Since
W^{\prime}
is infinite, we conclude that
W_{\mathcal{L}_{\chi}}^{\circ}
has infinite rank as a Coxeter group.
By
[
Héb22
, Remark 2.5 (2)]
, it follows that there is no finite rank presentation of
W_{\mathcal{L}_{\chi}}^{\circ}
either.

In light of Example
5.2.1
, whenever we discuss the endoscopic Kac-Moody group, we will assume that
G
is either of finite or affine type.
We review the construction of endoscopic groups which can be found in
[
LY20
]
in finite type or in
[
Li20
]
in affine type.
Let
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
. To
\mathcal{L}
we associated a collection of real roots
\Phi_{\textnormal{re},\mathcal{L}}\subset X_{*}(T)
, (endo)simple roots
\{\alpha_{s}\}_{s\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}\subset X_{*}(T)
, and (endo)simple coroots
\{\alpha_{s}^{\vee}\}_{s\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}\subset X^{*}(T)
.
The subgroup
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
of
W
generated by reflections in
\Phi_{\textnormal{re},\mathcal{L}}
is canonically a crystallographic Coxeter group of finite rank.
We can then consider the Kac-Moody root datum
(S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},X_{*}(T),\{\alpha_{s}\}_{s\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}},\{\alpha_{s}^{\vee}\}_{s\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}})
for
the generalized Cartan matrix
(\alpha_{s}(\alpha_{t}^{\vee}))_{t,s\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}
.
This root datum gives rise to a (connected) complex Kac-Moody group
H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
with canonical Borel subgroup
B_{\mathcal{L}}
.
The maximal torus of
B_{\mathcal{L}}
is
T
, and the Weyl group of
H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
is
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
We write
U_{\mathcal{L}}
for the pro-unipotent radical of
B_{\mathcal{L}}
. The group
H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
is called the
endoscopic group
of
G
corresponding to
\mathcal{L}
.

We let
\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}\coloneq B_{\mathcal{L}}\backslash H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
(resp.
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}\coloneq U_{\mathcal{L}}\backslash H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
)
denote the (resp. enhanced) flag variety of
H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
with respect to
B_{\mathcal{L}}
.
The Bruhat stratification gives a decomposition of the flag variety into locally closed varieties,

\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}=\bigsqcup_{w\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},w},

where
\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},w}
is isomorphic to an affine space of dimension
\ell_{\mathcal{L}}(w)
. The Bruhat stratification lifts to a stratification of the enhanced
flag variety,

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}=\bigsqcup_{w\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},w},

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},w}\cong\mathbb{A}^{\ell_{\mathcal{L}}(w)}\times T
.
We write
j_{w}^{H}:\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},w}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}
for the inclusion maps, and we use the same notation for the enhanced flag variety variants.
The closure of
\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},w}
is given by
\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},\leq w}=\bigcup_{x\leq_{\mathcal{L}}w}\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},x}
.
It is also useful to consider the complement of the closure which is given by
\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},>w}=\bigcup_{x>_{\mathcal{L}}w}\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},x}
. There
are obvious variants of these Schubert varieties for the enhanced flag variety which we denote similarly.

Let
D_{\cons}(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
denote the bounded derived category of
B_{\mathcal{L}}
-equivariant sheaves on

\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}
.
9
9

9

As with the monodromic Hecke category, this definition needs some justification since neither
B_{\mathcal{L}}
nor

\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}
is of finite type. By taking unipotent monodromy, one can simply use the definition from the monodromic Hecke category.

Note that
D_{\cons}(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
is a monoidal category with respect to convolution
\star
.
For each
s\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, we will write
\mathcal{E}_{s}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\coloneq\IC_{s}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
, i.e., the simple perverse sheaf on
\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},\leq s}
.
For an expression
\underline{w}=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{k})
in
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, we will write
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{H,\mathcal{L}}=\mathcal{E}_{s_{1}}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\star\ldots\star\mathcal{E}_{s_{k}}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
.
We will write
\Par_{\BS}(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
for the Bott-Samelson category of parity sheaves for
\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}
. That is the full
subcategory of
D_{\cons}(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
consisting of the objects
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n]
.
Note that
\Par_{\BS}(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
is a monoidal category with respect to convolution.
When
\mathbbm{k}
is a field or a complete local ring, we can consider also consider the category of parity sheaves for
\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}
, denoted
\Par(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
.
In this case, the indecomposables of
\Par(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
are indexed by
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}\times\mathbb{Z}
. For all
w\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, we
write
\mathcal{E}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
for the parity extension of
\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},w}}[\ell_{\mathcal{L}}(w)]
.

We can also consider right equivariant versions of the endoscopic categories. Let
D_{\cons}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
denote the bounded derived
category of right
B_{\mathcal{L}}
-equivariant constructible sheaves on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}
.
We regard any sheaf on
\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}
as a sheaf in
D_{\cons}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
via pullback along the
T
-torsor

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}\to\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}
.
In particular, for each expression
\underline{w}
in
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, we can regard
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
as a sheaf on the enhanced flag variety.
We write
\Par_{\BS}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
for the Bott-Samelson category of right-equivariant parity sheaves for
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}
.
That is the full subcategory of
D_{\cons}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
consisting of direct sums of objects of the form
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n]
.
When
\mathbbm{k}
is a field or a complete local ring, we can consider also consider the category of parity sheaves for
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}
, denoted
\Par(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
.
For all
w\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, we write
\mathcal{E}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
for the parity extension of
\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},w}}[\ell_{\mathcal{L}}(w)]
.

5.3.
Neutral Block Endoscopy

Let
\mathscr{Q}
be a field of characteristic 0.
If
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
is finite, then we can take
\Theta=S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
. In which case, Theorem
4.6.1
simplifies to an equivalence of
monoidal categories

\mathbb{H}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}:{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathscr{Q})\to\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}_{W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathscr{Q}}^{*}).

The classical theory of Soergel bimodules gives an equivalence of monoidal categories,

\Par(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathscr{Q})\cong\mathbb{S}\textnormal{Bim}_{W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathscr{Q}}^{*}).

By composition of these equivalences with obtain an equivalence,

\Par(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathscr{Q})\cong{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathscr{Q}).

which provides an analogue of
[
LY20
, Theorem 9.2]
.
In the following sections, we will further extend this equivalence in three key ways:

(1)

We will enlarge the allowable coefficient rings to any Noetherian domain of finite global dimension
\mathbbm{k}
which admits a weak-Cohen lift.

(2)

We will remove the finiteness condition on
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
(for the neutral block).

(3)

We will extend the result beyond the neutral blocks.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
denote the Bott-Samelson diagrammatic Hecke category introduced in
[
EW16
]
.
The objects of
\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
are indexed by pairs
(\underline{w},n)
where
\underline{w}
is an expression in
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We denote the object in
\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
indexed by
(\underline{w},n)
by
B_{\underline{w}}(n)
.
We can also consider the category
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
obtained from
\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
by
tensoring all morphism spaces as left
R_{\mathbbm{k}}
-modules with the trivial module
\mathbbm{k}
.
If
\mathbbm{k}
is a complete local ring of a field, we will write
\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
for the Karoubian envelope of the additive hull of
\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
.

Theorem 5.3.1

(Monodromic-Endoscopic equivalence for the neutral block)
.

Let
\mathbbm{k}
be a ring which admits a weak-Cohen lift.

(1)

There is an equivalence of monoidal additive categories,

\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}:\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}(\mathbbm{k}).

(2)

Assume that
G
is either of finite or affine type.
There is an equivalence of monoidal categories,

\Psi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}:\Par_{\BS}(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}(\mathbbm{k}).

If
\mathbbm{k}
is a complete local ring,
\Psi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}
induces an equivalence of monoidal additive categories

\Psi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}:\Par(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathbbm{k})

such that for all
w\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, we have that
\Psi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathcal{E}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}})\cong\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
.

Along the way, we will also prove the following variant of Theorem
5.3.1
for right equivariant Hecke categories.

Theorem 5.3.2
.

Let
\mathbbm{k}
be a ring which admits a weak-Cohen lift.

(1)

There is an equivalence of additive categories,

\tilde{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}:\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\to\>_{\mathcal{L}\fatbslash}\Par_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}(\mathbbm{k}).

(2)

Assume that
G
is either of finite or affine type. Then there is an equivalence of additive categories,

\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}:\Par_{\BS}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})\to\>_{\mathcal{L}\fatbslash}\Par_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}(\mathbbm{k}).

If
\mathbbm{k}
is a complete local ring,
\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}
induces an equivalence of additive categories

\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}:\Par(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})\to\>_{\mathcal{L}\fatbslash}\Par_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathbbm{k})

such that for all
w\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, we have that
\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathcal{E}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}})\cong\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
.

Remark 5.3.3
.

(1)

While we only stated Theorem
5.3.2
for the right equivariant Hecke categories, an obvious variant
exists with left equivariant Hecke categories instead.

(2)

Theorems
5.3.1
and
5.3.2
are compatible in the sense that for
sheaves
\mathcal{F}\in\Par(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
and
\mathcal{G}\in\Par(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
, there are natural isomorphisms

\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{G})\cong\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathcal{F})\star\Psi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathcal{G}).

(3)

There are derived versions of Theorems
5.3.1
and
5.3.2
where we
take the bounded homotopy category of both sides of the equivalences.
This produces an equivalence of mixed derived categories. This perspective will be used in the proof of Theorem
5.3.2
.

(4)

We can slightly relax the coefficient constraints in Theorems
5.3.1
and

5.3.2
.
Namely, in their proof, we just require the existence of a characteristic 0 Noetherian global domain of finite global dimension
\mathscr{Z}
along with ring morphisms

\mathscr{Z}\to\mathbbm{k}
and
\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{Z}
such that there exists some
\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{Z}}\in\Ch(T,\mathscr{Z})
satisfying
\mathbbm{k}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{Z}})\cong\mathcal{L}
and

\Phi_{\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{Z}}}=\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}
.
However, we will require
\mathbbm{k}
to admit a weak-Cohen lift to prove the non-neutral block equivalence.
We conjecture that Theorems
5.3.1
and
5.3.2
hold as long as there is
a ring homomorphism
\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\to\mathbbm{k}
.

The proof of Theorems
5.3.1
and
5.3.2
will occupy the remainder of the
section. We will first explain how to deduce Theorem
5.3.1
(2) from Theorem
5.3.1
(1), and
likewise for the right equivariant categories.

If
G
is either of finite or affine type, by
[
RW18
, Theorem 10.5]
, we have an equivalence of monoidal categories

(5.3.1)

\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\cong\Par_{\BS}(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})

that intertwines (1) with [1] and sends
B_{\underline{w}}(n)
to
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{w}}[n]
for expressions
\underline{w}
of
w\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
If
\mathbbm{k}
is a field or a complete local ring, the idempotent completion of (
5.3.1
) induces an equivalence,

\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\cong\Par(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k}).

We can then compose the equivalence (
5.3.1
) with
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
to produce the desired equivalence for Theorem

5.3.1
(2).

By applying
\mathbbm{k}\otimes_{R_{\mathbbm{k}}}(-)
to the Hom-spaces on both sides of (
5.3.1
), we obtain an analogous equivalence of additive categories

\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\cong\Par_{\BS}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
.
The previous argument can then be copied to shows that Theorem
5.3.2
(1) implies Theorem

5.3.2
(2).

5.4.
Construction of
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}

Let
\mathscr{Z}\to\mathbbm{k}
be a weak-Cohen lift of
\mathbbm{k}
.
We define
\mathscr{Q}=\textnormal{Frac}\mathscr{Z}
, the field of fractions for
\mathscr{Z}
.
We then have ring homomorphisms

\mathscr{Q}\leftarrow\mathscr{Z}\to\mathbbm{k}.

Note that
\mathscr{Z}\to\mathscr{Q}
is unit-injective.

The main principle of the construction of
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
is to define the functor on the defining objects and morphisms of
\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathscr{Z}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
over
\mathscr{Z}
.
We will then check that the defining relations in
\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathscr{Q}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
are satisfied by extending scalars to
\mathscr{Q}
.
Finally, we will extend scalars to construct
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
over
\mathbbm{k}
.

First, for each
s\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, we will fix an endo-reduced expression
\underline{s}
of
s
in
W
.
By Lemma
4.3.1
,
\underline{s}
is of the form
\underline{s}=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{k},t,s_{k},\ldots,s_{1})
such that for
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=s_{k}\ldots s_{1}\mathcal{L}
, the element

s_{k}\ldots s_{1}
is minimal in its block, and
t\in W_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}
is an externally simple reflection.
It is easy to define
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
for objects: we set

\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(B_{s}(n))=\mathcal{E}_{\underline{s}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n]

for
s\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.

The defining morphisms in
\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
are given by the diagrams
f
\ldots
\ldots

where
f\in R
is homogeneous.

\mathcal{E}_{\varnothing}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}

5.4.3.
2m_{st}
-valent vertices.

Let
s,t\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
with
m_{s,t}<\infty
. Write
w=sts\ldots
with
m_{s,t}
-terms.
Let
\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq\mathcal{E}_{st\ldots}^{\mathcal{L}}
and
\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq\mathcal{E}_{ts\ldots}^{\mathcal{L}}
where the expressions in both subscripts have
m_{s,t}
-terms.

Lemma 5.4.4
.

The pullback
j_{w}^{*}
induces an isomorphism of
\mathscr{Z}
-modules

(5.4.1)

\Hom(\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathcal{L}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Hom(j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}},j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathcal{L}}).

Moreover, both of sides are free
\mathscr{Z}
-modules of rank 1.

Proof.

By Corollary
3.8.18
and Lemma
3.6.3
, the
\mathscr{Z}
-module
\Hom(\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathcal{L}})
is free of rank 1.
An argument similar to the one given in Theorem
3.4.1
shows that
j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
Note that
\End(\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}})
is also a free
\mathscr{Z}
-module of rank 1.
Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{w}
is open in the support of
\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
and
\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathcal{L}}
, by a variation of Proposition
2.1.2
, we have that the map
(
5.4.1
) is surjective.
We are now done since surjective maps of rank 1 free modules are automatically isomorphisms.
∎

Define the map
f_{s,t}:\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathcal{L}}
as the pre-image under (
5.4.1
) of the canonical map
j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\to j_{w}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathcal{L}}
corresponding to
\id_{\mathcal{K}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}[m_{s,t}]}
.
We can then define

\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}\left(\scalebox{1}{
\hbox to71.93pt{\vbox to58.03pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-248.56165pt\lower-28.85275pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{273.6644pt}{18.83957pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{65}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}

5.5.
Verification of Defining Relations

In order to check that
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
is a well-defined functor, one must check that the image of a morphism under
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
is invariant under the
defining relations in
\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathscr{Z}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
.
By Lemma
3.6.3
, it suffices to check this after extending scalars to
\mathscr{Q}
-coefficients.
Since all relations only depend on parabolic subgroups of
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
of finite-type and by Theorem
4.6.1
, we can further reduce to
checking the defining relations after composition with
\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}
for some finite-type subset
\Theta\subset S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.

Let
\Theta\subseteq S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
be given by
\Theta=\{s,t\}
for
m_{s,t}<\infty
.
We will now calculate the image of our morphisms under
\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}
.

(1)

Polynomials:

\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}(\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(f))
for
f\in R_{\mathscr{Q}}
is given by multiplication by
f
on
R_{\mathscr{Q}}
.

(2)

The upper dot:
Any morphism of graded
R_{\mathscr{Q}}
-bimodules
B_{s}\to R_{\mathscr{Q}}(1)
is a scalar multiple of
m_{s}:B_{s}\to R_{\mathscr{Q}}(1)
taking
m_{s}(f\otimes g)=fg
.
By Proposition
4.4.5
, there is a unique morphism
\xi_{s}:\mathcal{K}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}[-1]
such that
(\epsilon_{s}[-1])\circ\xi_{s}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{L}}
.
In fact,
\xi_{s}
is explicitly given by the unit of the adjunction
\id\to j_{\leq s*}j_{\leq s}^{*}
applied to
\mathcal{K}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
Moreover, note that
\xi_{s}
identifies with
1\otimes 1\in B_{s}=R\otimes_{R^{s}}R(1)
under the isomorphism
\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}})\cong B_{s}
.
Similarly,
\epsilon_{\mathcal{L}}
identifies with
1\in R
under the isomorphism
\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{E}_{e}^{\mathcal{L}})\cong R
.
As a result, the relation
(\epsilon_{s}[-1])\circ\xi_{s}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{L}}
becomes
(\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}(\epsilon_{s}))(1\otimes 1)=1
. Hence,

\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}(\epsilon_{s})=m_{s}
.

(3)

The lower dot:
Any morphism of graded
R_{\mathscr{Q}}
-bimodules
R_{\mathscr{Q}}\to B_{s}(1)
is a scalar multiple of
\delta_{s}:R_{\mathscr{Q}}\to B_{s}(1)
taking

\delta_{s}(1)=\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{s}\otimes 1+1\otimes\alpha_{s})
.
In particular,
\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}(\eta_{s})=\lambda\delta_{s}
for some
\lambda\in\mathscr{Q}
.
As a result,
\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}(\epsilon_{s}\circ\eta_{s})=\lambda\alpha_{s}
.
By Lemma
4.5.5
, it suffices to check that
\lambda=1
in the case where
s
is an externally simple reflection.
In this case,
\epsilon_{s}\circ\eta_{s}
can be computed via the canonical morphism
\kappa:j^{!}\mathcal{L}^{s}[1]\to j^{*}\mathcal{L}^{s}[1]
where
j:T\hookrightarrow L_{s}

is the inclusion map.
Consider
\kappa\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}^{-1}:j^{!}\underline{\mathscr{Q}}_{L_{s}}[1]\to j^{*}\underline{\mathscr{Q}}_{L_{s}}[1]
. It follows from the non-monodromic case (c.f.,

[
RW18
, §10.5]
) that
\kappa\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}^{-1}=\alpha_{s}\in H_{T}^{2}(\pt;\mathscr{Q})\cong\Hom(\underline{\mathscr{Q}}_{T}[-1],\underline{\mathscr{Q}}_{T}[1])
.
As a result,
\kappa=\alpha_{s}\in H_{T}^{2}(\pt;\mathscr{Q})\cong\Hom(\mathcal{L}[-1],\mathcal{L}[1])
, and hence
\lambda=1
.

(4)

The trivalent vertices:
It follows from classical theory, that there are decompositions
B_{s}\otimes_{R}B_{s}\cong B_{s}(1)\oplus B_{s}(-1)
. Moreover, we
have isomorphisms of vector spaces

\hskip 28.45274pt\Hom(B_{s},B_{s}\otimes_{R}B_{s}(-1))\cong\mathscr{Q},\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Hom(B_{s}\otimes_{R}B_{s},B_{s}(-1))\cong\mathscr{Q}.

These morphism spaces are generated by the maps
t_{1}:B_{s}\to B_{s}\otimes_{R}B_{s}(-1)
defined by
f\otimes g\mapsto f\otimes 1\otimes g
and
t_{2}:B_{s}\otimes_{R}B_{s}\to B_{s}(-1)
defined by
f\otimes g\otimes h\mapsto f(\partial_{s}g)\otimes h

where we have identified
B_{s}\otimes_{R}B_{s}\cong R\otimes_{R^{s}}R\otimes_{R^{s}}R(2)
. Therefore,
\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}(b_{i})
is a scalar multiple of
t_{i}
for
i=1,2
.
Note that
t_{1}
(resp.
t_{2}
) are uniquely characterized by the identity

\hskip 28.45274pt(m_{s}(-1)\otimes_{R}\id_{B_{s}})\circ t_{1}=\id_{B_{s}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{resp.}\hskip 14.22636ptt_{2}(1)\circ(\delta_{s}\otimes_{R}\id_{B_{s}})=\id_{B_{s}}.

Therefore,
\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}(\nu_{s})=t_{1}
and
\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mu_{s})=t_{2}
follows from Lemma
4.3.6
.

(5)

2m_{s,t}
-valent vertices:
Write
w
for the longest element in the Weyl group
\langle s,t\rangle\subset W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
. The space of morphisms

\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}})\to\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathcal{L}})
is of dimension 1 over
\mathscr{Q}
.
It follows from the description given in Lemma
5.4.4
that
\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}
is the unique morphism

B_{s}\otimes_{R}B_{t}\otimes\ldots\to B_{t}\otimes_{R}\otimes B_{s}\otimes_{R}\ldots

which induces the identity morphism on
B_{w}(m_{s,t})
.

To conclude the proof, we must check that the defining relations for
\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathscr{Q}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
are satisfied.
Each relation involves only a subset
\Theta\subseteq S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
of finite type with
\lvert\Theta\rvert\leq 3
.
By faithfulness of
\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}
it suffices to show that the relations are satisfied after composing with
\mathbb{H}_{\Theta}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
The relations have already been checked at this point using localization (see
[
EW16
, §5.5]
).

As a result, we have a monoidal functor

\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}:\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathscr{Z}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}(\mathscr{Z})

for all
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(T,\mathscr{Z})
.

By Lemma
3.6.3
and Lemma
5.1.3
, for all
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
, we can define a monoidal functor

\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}:\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}(\mathbbm{k})

by extending scalars from
\mathscr{Z}
.

By Lemma
3.6.5
, we can tensor graded Hom-spaces by
\mathbbm{k}\otimes_{R}(-)
to produce a functor

\tilde{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}:\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\to\>_{\mathcal{L}\fatbslash}\Par_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}(\mathbbm{k}).

It is clear from their definitions that both
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
and
\tilde{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
are essentially surjective.

5.6.
Proof of Theorems
5.3.1
and
5.3.2
.

We will prove that
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
and
\tilde{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
are equivalences assuming the following claim.

Claim 5.6.1
.

\tilde{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
is an equivalence of categories when
\mathbbm{k}
is a field or a complete local ring.

We will prove Claim
5.6.1
in the next section.

Proof of Theorem
5.3.2
.

Consider the morphism of free
\mathbbm{k}
-modules of finite rank

\alpha:\Hom_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})}(B_{\underline{x}},B_{\underline{y}}(n))\to\Hom_{\>{}_{\mathcal{L}\fatbslash}\Par_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}(\mathbbm{k})}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{L}}[n]).

given by
\tilde{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
By localization theory and faithfully flat descent along completions of local rings,
\alpha
is an isomorphism if and only if
\hat{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\alpha

is an isomorphism for all maximal ideals
\mathfrak{m}
of
\mathbbm{k}
(where
\hat{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is the completion of
\mathbbm{k}
at
\mathfrak{m}
).
Since
\mathbbm{k}
is noetherian, there is an embedding
\mathbbm{k}\hookrightarrow\hat{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathfrak{m}}
.
In particular,
\mathbbm{k}\hookrightarrow\hat{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is unit-injective, as a result the endoscopy group does not change after extension of scalars (Lemma

5.1.6
).
As a result,
\hat{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\alpha
is induced from
\tilde{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
over
\hat{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathfrak{m}}
.
We then have that
\hat{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\alpha
is an isomorphism by Claim
5.6.1
.

\square

Proof of Theorem
5.3.1
.

Consider the morphism of graded left
R_{\mathbbm{k}}
-modules

\beta:\Hom_{\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})}^{\bullet}(B_{\underline{x}},B_{\underline{y}})\to\Hom_{{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}(\mathbbm{k})}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{E}_{\underline{y}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{L}}).

given by
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
As graded left
R_{\mathbbm{k}}
-modules, the left-hand side of
\beta
is free of finite rank by
[
EW16
, Theorem 6.11]
and the right-hand side of
\beta
is free of finite
rank by Lemma
3.6.3
.
By the graded Nakayama lemma, to show that
\beta
is an isomorphism it suffices to show that
\mathbbm{k}\otimes_{R}\beta
is an isomorphism.
Note that
\mathbbm{k}\otimes_{R}\beta
is the map induced from
\tilde{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
It follows from Theorem
5.3.2
that
\mathbbm{k}\otimes_{R}\beta
and hence
\beta
is an isomorphism.

\square

5.7.
Passage to Mixed Derived Categories

Let
\mathbbm{k}
be a field or a complete local ring.

We can consider the biequivariant and right equivariant mixed derived categories on the endoscopic side,

D^{m}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\coloneq K^{b}\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}),\hskip 28.45274pt\widetilde{D}^{m}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\coloneq K^{b}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}).

These categories have been thoroughly studied by Achar-Riche-Vay
[
ARV20
]
.
Despite these not necessarily being of geometric origin, they share many similar features to the mixed derived categories.
In particular, all the constructions and facts from §
2.3
hold after making appropriate changes.
We use the same notation as in §
5.2
, despite the endoscopic Kac-Moody group not necessarily being well-defined. We refer to
loc. cit.
on how to
make these constructions precise.

The functor
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}:\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}(\mathbbm{k})
induces a monoidal functor

\underline{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}:D^{m}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{m}(\mathbbm{k}).

For each
w\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, there are
standard

\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
and
costandard
objects
\underline{\nabla}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}}

which we can regard as objects in either
D^{m}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
or
\widetilde{D}^{m}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
.

Lemma 5.7.1
.

For all
w\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, there are isomorphisms

\underline{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}})\cong\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\underline{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\underline{\nabla}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}})\cong\underline{\nabla}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}.

Proof.

We will just prove the first isomorphism. The second isomorphism follows from a similar argument.

By monoidality of
\underline{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
and Corollary
3.9.7
(see also
[
ARV20
, Proposition 6.11]
), it suffices to consider the
case of
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
an endosimple reflection.
In
D^{m}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
there is a distinguished triangle

(5.7.1)

\underline{\Delta}_{s}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\to\mathcal{E}_{s}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\to\Delta_{e}^{H,\mathcal{L}}(1)\to.

We can then apply
\underline{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
to (
5.7.1
) to product a distinguished triangle

(5.7.2)

\underline{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}\left(\underline{\Delta}_{s}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\right)\to\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\underline{\Delta}_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}(1)\to,

where the second map is given by the unit map
\id\to j_{e*}j_{e}^{*}
in the non-mixed category.
Since
j_{x}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{x}^{\mathcal{L}}=0
for all
x\neq e
and
x\neq s
, the distinguished triangle (
5.7.2
) coincides with the open-closed distinguished
triangle for the pair (
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{e},\widetilde{\mathcal{F}\ell}_{>e}
).
As a result, we get an isomorphism
\underline{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}\left(\underline{\Delta}_{s}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\right)\cong\underline{\Delta}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
as desired.
∎

The functor
\tilde{\Phi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}:\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\to\>_{\mathcal{L}\fatbslash}\Par_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\BS,\circ}(\mathbbm{k})
induces a functor

\underline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}:\widetilde{D}^{m}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\to\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{m}(\mathbbm{k})

By Lemma
5.7.1
, we have isomorphisms

(5.7.3)

\underline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}})\cong\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\underline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\underline{\nabla}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}})\cong\underline{\nabla}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}

We now have the preliminaries need to prove Claim
5.6.1
.

Proof of Claim
5.6.1
.

It suffices to prove that
\underline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
is fully faithful.
By Lemma
2.3.4
, we have that

(5.7.4)

\Hom_{\widetilde{D}^{m}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})}(\underline{\Delta}_{x}^{H,\mathcal{L}},\underline{\nabla}_{y}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\langle m\rangle[n])\cong\begin{cases}\mathbbm{k}&\text{if }x=y\text{ and }n=m=0,\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

and

(5.7.5)

\Hom_{\mathcal{D}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{m}(\mathbbm{k})}(\underline{\Delta}_{x}^{\mathcal{L}},\underline{\nabla}_{y}^{\mathcal{L}}\langle m\rangle[n])\cong\begin{cases}\mathbbm{k}&\text{if }x=y\text{ and }n=m=0,\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}.

In view of this, (
5.7.3
), and Beĭlinson’s lemma
[
ABG04
, Lemma 3.9.3]
, it suffices to prove that for all
w\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, there exists a
morphism
f:\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\to\underline{\nabla}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
corresponding to a unit in
\mathbbm{k}
under the isomorphism (
5.7.4
) such that

\underline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(f):\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\underline{\nabla}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
corresponds to a unit in
\mathbbm{k}
under the isomorphism (
5.7.5
).

Let
\underline{w}
be a reduced expression of
w
in
W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
. Let
\underline{x}
be the endo-reduced expression of
w
in
W
obtained by replacing
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}

with
\underline{s}
.
By Proposition
3.9.6
, Corollary
3.9.7
, and the chain complex description of
\underline{\Delta}_{s}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
(resp.

\underline{\Delta}_{\underline{s}}^{\mathcal{L}}
) for
s\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, the complex
\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
(resp.
\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
) is isomorphic to a complex
\mathcal{F}_{\underline{w}}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
(resp.
\mathcal{F}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}
)
such that

(\mathcal{F}_{\underline{w}}^{H,\mathcal{L}})^{n}=\begin{cases}B_{\underline{w}}&n=0,\\
0&n<0,\end{cases}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt(\mathcal{F}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}})^{n}=\begin{cases}\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}&n=0,\\
0&n<0.\end{cases}

Moreover, for
n>0
,
j_{w}^{H*}(\mathcal{F}_{\underline{w}}^{H,\mathcal{L}})^{n}=0
and
j_{w}^{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}})^{n}=0
. Similarly,
\underline{\nabla}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
(resp.

\underline{\nabla}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
) is isomorphic to a complex
\mathcal{G}_{\underline{w}}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
(resp.
\mathcal{G}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}
)
such that

(\mathcal{G}_{\underline{w}}^{H,\mathcal{L}})^{n}=\begin{cases}B_{\underline{w}}&n=0,\\
0&n>0,\end{cases}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt(\mathcal{G}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}})^{n}=\begin{cases}\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}&n=0,\\
0&n>0.\end{cases}

Moreover, for
n<0
,
j_{w}^{H*}(\mathcal{G}_{\underline{w}}^{H,\mathcal{L}})^{n}=0
and
j_{w}^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}})^{n}=0
.

Consider the map
f:\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\to\underline{\nabla}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
defined by the morphism of chain complexes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{F}_{\underline{w}}^{H,\mathcal{L}})^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B_{\underline{w}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B_{\underline{w}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{F}_{\underline{w}}^{H,\mathcal{L}})^{-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

It is clear from its construction that under the isomorphism (
5.7.4
) that
f
corresponds to a unit in
\mathbbm{k}
.
Under the isomorphisms
\underline{\Delta}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\mathcal{F}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}
and
\underline{\nabla}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\mathcal{G}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}
, the map
\mathcal{F}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\mathcal{G}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}
induced by
\underline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(f)
corresponds to the chain map given by
(\mathcal{F}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}})^{0}=\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\id}}{{\to}}\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}=(\mathcal{G}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}})^{0}
and
(\mathcal{F}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}})^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 0}}{{\to}}(\mathcal{G}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}})^{n}
for all
n\neq 0
.
In particular,
j_{w}^{*}\underline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(f)
is an isomorphism.
As a result, under the isomorphism (
5.7.4
), the map
\underline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(f)
corresponds to a unit in
\mathbbm{k}
.

\square

5.8.
Whittaker Category and Rigidified Minimal IC Sheaves

For the remainder of the paper, we will assume that
\mathbbm{k}
is a field or a complete local ring and that
G
is of finite type.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}
,
\delta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}
,
\gamma\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
, and
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
. By
Proposition
3.3.17
, there
exists isomorphisms

c_{\gamma,\beta}:\IC_{w^{\gamma}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\IC_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\IC_{w^{\gamma\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}.

However, these isomorphisms need not be canonical (since the minimal IC sheaves themselves are not canonically defined).
We would like to fix a particular representative of the isomorphism class of the minimal IC sheaves as well as isomorphisms between their convolutions in a coherent way.
In particular, we would like the isomorphisms to make the following diagram commute

(5.8.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\IC_{w^{\delta}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\star\IC_{w^{\gamma}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\IC_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\IC_{w^{\delta\gamma}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\IC_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\IC_{w^{\delta}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\star\IC_{w^{\gamma\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\IC_{w^{\delta\gamma\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\delta,\gamma}\star\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\star c_{\gamma,\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\delta\gamma,\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\delta,\gamma\beta}}

If one is too flippant about making choices, (
5.8.1
) can indeed fail to commute (see
[
LY20
, Example 6.7]
).

A solution given in
[
LY20
]
is to use a version of the Whittaker category to rigidify the minimal IC’s to ensure that (
5.8.1
) commutes.
Unfortunately, the usual Whittaker category does not make sense when working with the analytic topology since no version of the Artin-Schreier sheaf exists.
A partial solution is given in
[
Gou21
]
and
[
BR22
]
by temporarily changing the sheaf theoretic setting to étale sheaves, and then comparing the resulting models of sheaves.
While this method would yield desirable results, it has the drawback of restricting our coefficients to finite fields and their algebraic closures.
To get around these issues, we will instead use a version of the Kirillov model for the Whittaker category introduced by Gaitsgory which does not require changing our
sheaf theoretic setting or restricting our coefficients
[
Gai20
]
.
While the Whittaker category can be defined when
G
is not of finite type, it is too large to rigidify the minimal IC sheaves which necessitates our restriction.

5.8.1.
Kirillov Model

Let
X/_{\mathcal{L}}H
be a twisted algebraic stack with a
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-action on
X
which commutes with the
H
-action.
Assume that the
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-action on
X
extends to an action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\mathbb{G}_{a}
where
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on
\mathbb{G}_{a}
by dilation.
Fix a cocharacter
\lambda:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to H
, and write
\lambda_{+}:\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\mathbb{G}_{a}\to H
for the composition of
\lambda
with the quotient
\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\mathbb{G}_{a}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
We define multiplicative local systems on
\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\mathbb{G}_{a}
respectively by
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\coloneq\lambda^{*}\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda_{+}}\coloneq\lambda_{+}^{*}\mathcal{L}\cong\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\boxtimes\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
.
Consider the
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-averaging functor

\Av_{!}^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}:D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}X/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\mathbb{G}_{a}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda_{+}}}X/_{\mathcal{L}}H).

We define the
\mathcal{L}
-monodromic Whittaker category

\mathcal{W}(X/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})
as the full subcategory of
D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}X/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of objects
\mathcal{F}
such that
\Av_{!}^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}\mathcal{F}=0
.
Note that the Whittaker category does depend on the choice of
\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\mathbb{G}_{a}
-action and
\lambda
; although, we omit this data from the notation to increase readability.

Let
f:X/_{\mathcal{L}}H\to Y/_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}H^{\prime}
be a
\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\mathbb{G}_{a}
-equivariant morphism of twisted stacks.
It is easy to check that the functors
f_{!}
and
f^{*}
(when defined) send
\mathcal{W}(X/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})
and
\mathcal{W}(Y/_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}H^{\prime},\mathbbm{k})
to one another.
Note that the analogous statement for
f_{*}
and
f^{!}
is
not
true.

5.8.2.
Whittaker Category for
\mathbb{A}^{1}

A particularly important example is the case of
X=\mathbb{A}^{1}\times H
where
H
is an algebraic group. In this case, we let
\mathbb{G}_{a}
act on
\mathbb{A}^{1}\times H
by translation on

\mathbb{A}^{1}
and trivially on
H
.
The
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
by dilations and with the action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
on
H
by a (possibly trivial) homomorphism
\lambda:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to H
.
The equivariance constraint ensures that every object of
D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\mathbb{A}^{1}\times H/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})
is constructible with respect to
the strata
Z=\{0\}\times H
and
V=\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times H
.
Let
u:\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
and
s:\{0\}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
denote the obvious inclusion maps.

One must consider the following two cases when computing the Whittaker category: (i)
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\cong\underline{\mathbbm{k}}
and (ii)
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\not\cong\underline{\mathbbm{k}}
.
In case (ii), we have that
D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}Z/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})=0
and
D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\mathbb{G}_{a}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda_{+}}}\mathbb{A}^{1}\times H/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})=0
.
In particular,
\Av_{!}^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}=0
.
As a result, we have an equivalence of categories

\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times H/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})\cong D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}V/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})\cong D(\mathbbm{k}\textnormal{-mod})

Moreover, the Whittaker category is generated by
u_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}
.

We will now handle the case when
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\cong\underline{\mathbbm{k}}
.
The category
D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash V/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})\cong D(\mathbbm{k}\textnormal{-mod})
is generated as a triangulated category by objects of the form
\underline{\mathbbm{k}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}
.
We can compute

(5.8.2)

\Av_{!}^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(u_{*}\underline{\mathbbm{k}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L})\cong R\Gamma_{c}(u_{*}\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})\boxtimes\mathcal{L}=0

where the vanishing follows from a classical result of Laumon that
R\Gamma_{c}(u_{*}\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})=0

[
Lau03
]
.
10
10

10

The sheaf
u_{*}\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
is sometimes called the Fourier-Laumon kernel. It has previously been used as a replacement for the Artin-Schreier sheaf in the context of
the homogeneous Fourier transform. It is useful to think of it as playing an analogous role for the Whittaker category.

As a consequence, for any sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{1}\times H/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})
, the distinguished triangle

\Av_{!}^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(s\times\id_{H})_{*}(s\times\id_{H})^{!}\mathcal{F}\to\Av_{!}^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}\mathcal{F}\to\Av_{!}^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(u\times\id_{H})_{*}(u\times\id_{H})^{*}\mathcal{F}\to

yields isomorphisms

(5.8.3)

\Av_{!}^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}\mathcal{F}\cong\Av_{!}^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(s\times\id_{H})_{*}(s\times\id_{H})^{!}\mathcal{F}.

As a result, it follows from a simple sheaf functor argument that
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{A}^{1}/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})
if and only if the costalk of
\mathcal{F}
at
(0,1)\in\mathbb{A}^{1}\times H
, denoted
\mathcal{F}\mid_{(0,1)}^{!}
, vanishes.
In particular,
\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times H/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})
is generated as a triangulated category by the sheaf
u_{*}\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}
.
By adjunction,
\End^{\bullet}(u_{*}\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L})\cong\mathbbm{k}
.
As a result, we obtain an equivalence of categories
\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{A}^{1}/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})\cong D(\mathbbm{k}\textnormal{-mod})
.
We summarize the results thus far in the following lemma.

Lemma 5.8.3
.

The
\mathcal{L}
-monodromic Whittaker category for
\mathbb{A}^{1}\times H
has the following description.

(1)

There is an equivalence of categories

R\Hom(u_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\boxtimes\mathcal{L},-):\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times H/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})\cong D(\mathbbm{k}\textnormal{-mod}).

(2)

A sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\mathbb{A}^{1}\times H/_{\mathcal{L}}H,\mathbbm{k})
is in the Whittaker category if and only if
\mathcal{F}\mid_{(0,1)}^{!}=0
.

5.8.4.
The Monodromic Whittaker Category

Let
U^{-}
be the unipotent radical of the Borel subgroup opposite to
B
with respect to the maximal torus
T
.
The
U^{-}
-orbits on
G/B
are affine spaces parametrized by
w\in W
.
We will denote
G_{w}^{-}\coloneq U^{-}\dot{w}B
and note that
G_{w}^{-}/U\cong\mathbb{A}^{\ell(w_{0})-\ell(w)}\times T
.
Let
j_{w}^{-}:G_{w}^{-}/U\hookrightarrow G/U
denote the inclusion maps.

We fix an isomorphism
U_{\alpha}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}
for each
\alpha\in\Phi^{-}
.
We can then consider the homomorphism

\chi:U^{-}\to U^{-}/[U^{-},U^{-}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\prod_{s\in S}U_{-\alpha_{s}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\prod_{s\in S}\mathbb{G}_{a}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textnormal{sum}}}{{\to}}\mathbb{G}_{a}.

Let
K=\ker\chi
.
Fix a nontrivial cocharacter
\lambda:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to T
and consider the conjugation map
\Ad:T\to\Aut(U^{-1})
.
Since
\Ad(t)(U_{\alpha})\subseteq U_{\alpha}
for all
\alpha\in\Phi
and
t\in T
, we have that
\Ad(t)(K)\subseteq K
.
As a result, the
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-action on
K\backslash G/U
extends to a
\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\mathbb{G}_{a}
-action on
K\backslash G/U
afforded by
\lambda
.
To ensure that
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action is well-behaved, we will assume that
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts non-trivially on
K\backslash U^{-}
(such a condition is generic for the choice of
\lambda
).
As a consequence, we may fix an isomorphism
K\backslash U^{-1}\cong\mathbb{A}^{1}
so that the induced
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action is by dilation.

We can then consider Whittaker sheaves on
K\backslash G/U/_{\mathcal{L}}T
.
To simplify notation, we will write
\mathcal{W}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\coloneq\mathcal{W}(K\backslash G/U/_{\mathcal{L}}T,\mathbbm{k}).

Similarly, for each
w\in W
, we will write
\mathcal{W}_{/\mathcal{L}}(w,\mathbbm{k})\coloneq\mathcal{W}(K\backslash G_{w}^{-}/U/_{\mathcal{L}}T,\mathbbm{k})
.
As before
\mathcal{W}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
depends on the choice of
\chi
and
\lambda
; although, we omit it from the notation to increase readability.

The definition of convolution from §
3.3
can be easily adapted to produce bifunctors

\star:D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime}}K\backslash G/U/_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}T,\mathbbm{k})\times\>_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}D_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}K\backslash G/U/_{\mathcal{L}}T,\mathbbm{k}),

\star:D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime}}U^{-}\backslash G/U/_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}T,\mathbbm{k})\times\>_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}D_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}U^{-}\backslash G/U/_{\mathcal{L}}T,\mathbbm{k}).

It follows from the definitions that if
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash_{\lambda^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}K\backslash G/U/_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}T,\mathbbm{k})
and
\mathcal{G}\in\>_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}D_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
, we have a natural isomorphism
\Av_{!}^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{G})\cong\Av_{!}^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(\mathcal{F})\star\mathcal{G}
.
As a result, convolution descends to a bifunctor,

(-)\star(-):\mathcal{W}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathbbm{k})\times\>_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}D_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to\mathcal{W}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathbbm{k}),

which is suitably associative.

Lemma 5.8.5
.

The category
\mathcal{W}_{/\mathcal{L}}(w,\mathbbm{k})=0
unless
w=e
.

Proof.

Assume that
w\neq e
. Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime}}K\backslash G_{w}^{-}/U/_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}T,\mathbbm{k})
.
Note that for all
\beta\in\Phi^{+}
, we have that
U_{-\beta}\dot{w}B/U
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant.
Since
w\neq e
, there exists a simple root
\alpha\in\Phi^{+}
such that
w^{-1}(-\alpha)\in\Phi^{+}
.
This implies that
U_{-\alpha}\dot{w}B/U\subseteq U_{-\alpha}\dot{w}B/U
.
Since
U^{-}=KU_{-\alpha}
, we have isomorphisms of stacks

K\backslash U^{-}\dot{w}B/U\cong K\backslash K^{-}\dot{w}B/U\cong\dot{w}(\Ad{\dot{w}^{-1}}K\cap T)\backslash T.

Therefore,
\mathcal{F}
is
U^{-}
-equivariant.
Since
\mathbb{G}_{a}
unipotent, the forgetful functor
\For_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
is fully faithful. As a result, the counit of the adjunction
\Av_{!}^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}\For_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\id
is a natural isomorphism.
Since
\mathcal{F}
occurs in the essential image of
\For_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
, we must have that
\Av_{!}^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}\mathcal{F}=0
if and only if
\mathcal{F}=0
.
∎

From our earlier discussion, we have fixed an isomorphism of stacks
K\backslash G_{e}^{-}/U=K\backslash U^{-}B/U=K\backslash U^{-}\times T\cong\mathbb{A}^{1}\times T

such that the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
is by dilation.
By Lemma
5.8.3
, we then have that
\mathcal{W}_{/\mathcal{L}}(e,\mathbbm{k})\cong D(\mathbbm{k}\textnormal{-mod})
and is generated by the sheaf

\chi^{*}u_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}\in D_{\cons}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}K\backslash U^{-}\times T/_{\mathcal{L}}T,\mathbbm{k})

where
u:\mathbb{C}^{\times}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is the open inclusion.
We define a unique simple perverse Whittaker sheaf

\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq j_{e!}^{-}\chi^{*}u_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}[\dim U^{-}+\dim T]\in\mathcal{W}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k}).

The following lemma can be easily obtained from the discussion above and Lemma
5.8.5
.

Lemma 5.8.6
.

There is an equivalence of categories

R\Hom(\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{L}},-):\mathcal{W}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\cong D(\mathbbm{k}\textnormal{-mod}).

Lemma 5.8.7
.

For all
w\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{L}}
, there is an isomorphism

\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\begin{cases}\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{L}}&w=w^{\beta}\text{ for some block }\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}},\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proof.

The following argument is adapted from
[
LY20
, Lemma 5.10]
.
By induction on
\ell(w)
, it suffices to just consider the case when
w=s
is a simple reflection.

If
s\notin W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
, then since
\IC_{s}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\IC_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}
, we have that
(-)\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}:\mathcal{W}_{/s\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\to\mathcal{W}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
is an equivalence of categories.
As a result,
\mathcal{F}^{s\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{L}}[n]
for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
It is a routine exercise that convolution by standards (resp. costandards) is left (resp. right) perverse
t
-exact.
Since
\nabla_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
, it then follows that
(-)\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}
is perverse
t
-exact. As a result, we must have that
n=0
.

Now suppose
s\in W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
. We can then extend
\mathcal{L}
to a sheaf
\mathcal{L}^{s}\in\Ch(L_{s},\mathbbm{k})
. We regard
\mathcal{L}^{s}
as a multiplicative local system on
P_{s}
.
We can now consider the partial Whittaker category
\mathcal{W}(K\backslash G/_{\mathcal{L}^{s}}P_{s},\mathbbm{k})
.
There are push-pull functors

\pi_{s}^{*}:\mathcal{W}(K\backslash G/_{\mathcal{L}^{s}}P_{s},\mathbbm{k})\rightleftarrows\mathcal{W}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k}):\pi_{s*}.

Note that
\pi_{s}^{*}
is faithful since it is just a forgetful functor.
If
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{W}(K\backslash G/_{\mathcal{L}^{s}}P_{s},\mathbbm{k})
, then
\pi_{s}^{*}\mathcal{F}=0
since its support is contained outside
U^{-}B/U
.
As a result, the partial Whittaker category
\mathcal{W}(K\backslash G/_{\mathcal{L}^{s}}P_{s},\mathbbm{k})=0
.
By an easy analogue of Lemma
3.3.5
, we have a natural isomorphism

\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\pi_{s}^{*}\pi_{s*}\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{L}}[1].

However, since
\mathcal{W}(K\backslash G/_{\mathcal{L}^{s}}P_{s},\mathbbm{k})=0
, we must have that
\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}=0
.
∎

5.8.8.
Rigidified Minimal IC Sheaves

Definition 5.8.9
.

A
rigidified minimal IC sheaf
consists of a pair
(\mathcal{G}_{\beta}^{\mathcal{L}},\epsilon)
where
\mathcal{G}_{\beta}^{\mathcal{L}}
is a representative of the isomorphism class

\IC_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}
and
\epsilon
is an isomorphism in

\mathcal{W}_{/\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})
,

\epsilon:\mathcal{F}^{w^{\beta}\mathcal{L}}\star\mathcal{G}_{\beta}^{\mathcal{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{L}}.

By Lemma
5.8.7
, rigidified minimal IC sheaves exist for every block.
Since
\End(\IC_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}})\cong\mathbbm{k}
and
\End(\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{L}})\cong\mathbbm{k}
(see Lemma
5.8.6
), for any two rigidified
minimal IC sheaves
(\mathcal{G}_{\beta}^{\mathcal{L}},\epsilon)
,
(\mathcal{H}_{\beta}^{\mathcal{L}},\epsilon^{\prime})
, there exists a unique isomorphism
\alpha:\mathcal{G}_{\beta}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\beta}^{\mathcal{L}}
such
that
\epsilon^{\prime}\circ(\id\star\alpha)=\epsilon
.
For each block
\beta
, we will fix a rigidified minimal IC sheaf. We abuse notation and write
\IC_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}
for the underlying object and
\epsilon_{\beta}
for
the map making (
\IC_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}
,
\epsilon_{\beta}
) into a rigidified minimal IC sheaf.

Lemma 5.8.10
.

For
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
and
\gamma\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
, there exists a canonical isomorphism of rigidified minimal IC sheaves,

b_{\gamma,\beta}:\IC_{w^{\gamma}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\IC_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\IC_{w^{\gamma\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}.

Moreover, if
\delta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
, there is an equality of morphisms

b_{\delta\gamma,\beta}(b_{\delta,\gamma}\times\id)=b_{\delta,\gamma\beta}(\id\times b_{\gamma,\beta}).

Proof.

The lemma follows from the same argument given in the proof of
[
LY20
, Lemma 5.12]
.
∎

Corollary 5.8.11
.

Then the assignment
{}_{\mathcal{L}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}
given by
\beta\mapsto\IC_{w^{\beta}}^{\mathcal{L}}
extends to a 2-functor of
\IC_{\min}:\Xi\to{}_{\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/}(\mathbbm{k})
.

Remark 5.8.12
.

If
G
is instead of affine type, then the Whittaker category decomposes into a direct sum of categories which look like the Whittaker categories of reductive groups.
A crucial step towards developing a version of Corollary
5.8.11
for Kac-Moody groups of affine type is to find a means of identifying these
categories.
Recent work of Dhillon–Li–Yun–Zhu
[
DLYZ25
]
suggests that this should be doable, but we will not investigate this any further in this paper.

5.9.
The Endoscopic Groupoid

In order to extend Theorem
5.3.1
to all blocks, we need to describe a 2-categorical enhancement of parity sheaves on the endoscopic group.
To accomplish this, we will construct a groupoid
\mathfrak{H}
in schemes. The groupoid
\mathfrak{H}
carries a morphism of groupoids
\mathfrak{H}\to\Xi
whose fibers over
\mathcal{L}\in\Xi
are given by the endoscopic groups
H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
The geometric aspects of this section are essentially covered in
[
LY20
, §10]
.

5.9.1.
Groupoid of Lifts

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
. Consider the scheme
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\tilde{\Xi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}
given by the moduli space of lifts of
w^{\beta}
,

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\tilde{\Xi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}\coloneq\{\dot{w}\in N_{G}(T)\mid\dot{w}T=w^{\beta}\}.

We can then construct a groupoid
\tilde{\Xi}
in schemes whose object set is
\mathfrak{o}
, and the morphism set is given by

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\tilde{\Xi}_{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq\bigsqcup_{\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}}{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\tilde{\Xi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}.

Composition in
\tilde{\Xi}
is given by multiplication in
N_{G}(T)
.

Definition 5.9.2
.

A
relative pinning
for the endoscopic group
H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
is a collection of isomorphisms
H_{\mathcal{L},\alpha}^{\circ}\to G_{\alpha}
for each simple root

\alpha\in\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{+}
where
H_{\mathcal{L},\alpha}^{\circ}
(resp.
G_{\alpha}
) is the root subgroup for
\alpha
of
H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
(resp.
G
).

Relative pinnings for endoscopic groups always exist and are unique up to a unique isomorphism. For each
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(T,\mathbbm{k})
, we will fix a relative pinning of

H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
For each
\dot{w}\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\tilde{\Xi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}
, there is a unique isomorphism

\sigma(\dot{w}):H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}\to H_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}

which preserves the fixed relative pinning. In more detail,
\sigma|_{T}
is given by the action of
w
. Moreover, it induces an isomorphism of Kac-Moody root data
between
(S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},X_{*}(T),\{\alpha_{s}\}_{s\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}},\{\alpha_{s}^{\vee}\}_{s\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}})
and
(S_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ},X_{*}(T),\{\alpha_{s}\}_{s\in S_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}},\{\alpha_{s}^{\vee}\}_{s\in S_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}})
.
For each simple root
\alpha\in\Phi_{\re,\mathcal{L}}
,
\sigma(\dot{w})
restricts to an isomorphism of root subgroups
H_{\mathcal{L},\alpha}^{\circ}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}H_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime},w\alpha}^{\circ}
which makes the following diagram commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathcal{L},\alpha}^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime},w\alpha}^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{w\alpha}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma(\dot{w})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{Ad}(\dot{w})}

5.9.3.
Translations of Endoscopic Groups

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
.
We can then define a scheme

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}\coloneq{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\tilde{\Xi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}\times^{T}H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}

where the action of
T
on
H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
is by left translation, and its action on
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\tilde{\Xi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}
is by right translation.
There is a canonical isomorphism

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}\coloneq{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\tilde{\Xi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}\times^{T}H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}\cong H_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}\times^{T}{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\tilde{\Xi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}

given by
(\dot{w},h)\mapsto(\sigma(\dot{w})(h),\dot{w})
.
As a result, for blocks
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
and
\gamma\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
, we can define a multiplication map

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}H_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\gamma}\times{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\gamma\beta}

(\dot{y},h^{\prime},\dot{x},h)\mapsto(\dot{y}\dot{x},\sigma(\dot{x}^{-1})(h^{\prime})h).

The composition map is associative over the set of blocks. As a result, we can define a groupoid
\mathfrak{H}
in schemes whose object set is
\mathfrak{o}
and morphisms are given
by
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}
.

5.9.4.
Parity Sheaves on
\mathfrak{H}

We define the flag variety
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}
associated to
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}
as the scheme

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}\coloneq B_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\backslash{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}.

It is naturally acted on the right by the Borel
B_{\mathcal{L}}
of
H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
This gives rise to a Bruhat decomposition of
B_{\mathcal{L}}
-orbits.

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}=\bigsqcup_{w\in\beta}{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L},w}^{\beta}

where each
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L},w}^{\beta}
is isomorphic to an affine space of dimension
\ell_{\beta}(w)
. We will write
j_{w}^{H}:{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L},w}^{\beta}\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}
for the obvious inclusion map.
Moreover, there is a
B_{\mathcal{L}}
-invariant isomorphism of ind-schemes

(5.9.1)

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}

induced from the non-canonical isomorphism
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\tilde{\Xi}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}\cong T
. The isomorphism (
5.9.1
) induces
isomorphisms of Bruhat strata

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L},w}^{\beta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},w^{\beta}w}.

We can consider the derived category of
B_{\mathcal{L}}
-equivariant constructible sheaves on
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}
, denoted by
D_{\cons}({}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
.
The groupoid structure on
\mathfrak{H}
gives rise to convolution bifunctors

(-)\star(-):D_{\cons}({}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\gamma}/B_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}},\mathbbm{k})\times D_{\cons}({}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}({}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\gamma\beta}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k}).

which carries an associativity natural transformation and satisfies a variant of the pentagon axiom.

For each
w\in\beta
, we can define the standard and costandard sheaves, respectively,

\Delta_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\coloneq j_{w!}^{H}\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L},w}^{\beta}}[\ell_{\beta}(w)]\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\nabla_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\coloneq j_{w*}^{H}\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L},w}^{\beta}}[\ell_{\beta}(w)].

When
w=w^{\beta}
, the stratum
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L},w^{\beta}}^{\beta}
is closed, and we get a canonical isomorphism
\Delta_{w^{\beta}}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\cong\nabla_{w^{\beta}}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
.
The following lemma is an endoscopic variation of Proposition
3.3.10
.
The proof follows the standard argument given for the usual Hecke category.

Lemma 5.9.5
.

Let
x\in\gamma
and
y\in\beta
where
\gamma\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
and
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
.
We have natural isomorphisms

(1)

\Delta_{xy}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{x}^{H,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\Delta_{y}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
if
\ell_{\gamma\beta}(xy)=\ell_{\gamma}(x)+\ell_{\beta}(y)
;

(2)

\nabla_{xy}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\cong\nabla_{x}^{H,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\nabla_{y}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
if
\ell_{\gamma\beta}(xy)=\ell_{\gamma}(x)+\ell_{\beta}(y)
;

(3)

\nabla_{y^{-1}}^{H,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\Delta_{y}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{e}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\cong\Delta_{y^{-1}}^{H,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\nabla_{y}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
.

Corollary 5.9.6
.

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
.
There are equivalences of categories

\Delta_{w^{\beta,-1}}^{H,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star(-):D_{\cons}({}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k}),

and

(-)\star\Delta_{w^{\beta,-1}}^{H,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}:D_{\cons}({}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}},\mathbbm{k}).

The isomorphism (
5.9.1
) ensures that
D_{\cons}({}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
satisfies the parity conditions.
As a result, when
\mathbbm{k}
is a field or a complete local ring, we can consider the subcategory of parity sheaves
\Par({}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
.
Moreover, parity extensions always exist.
More precisely, for each
w\in\beta
, there is an indecomposable parity extensions
\mathcal{E}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
of
\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L},w}^{\beta}}[\ell_{\beta}(w)]
.

Lemma 5.9.7
.

Assume that
\mathbbm{k}
is a field or a complete local ring.
Let
\gamma\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
and
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
.
The convolution bifunctor

(-)\star(-):D_{\cons}({}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\gamma}/B_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}},\mathbbm{k})\times D_{\cons}({}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}({}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\gamma\beta}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k}).

takes parity sheaves to parity sheaves.

Proof.

Let
x\in\gamma
and
y\in\beta
.
By Corollary
5.9.6
, it can easily be checked that there are isomorphisms

\Delta_{w^{\gamma,-1}}^{H,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\star\mathcal{E}_{x}^{H,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\cong\mathcal{E}_{w^{\gamma,-1}x}^{H,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{E}_{y}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\star\Delta_{w^{\beta,-1}}^{H,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\cong\mathcal{E}_{yw^{\beta,-1}}^{H,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}.

The result then follows from associativity of convolution and the fact that convolution takes parity sheaves to parity sheaves in the ordinary Hecke category

D_{\cons}(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
.
∎

As a consequence of Lemma
5.9.7
, when
\mathbbm{k}
is a field or a complete local ring, we can now construct a 2-category
\textnormal{Par}^{H}(\mathbbm{k})

over
\Xi
as follows.
The object set of
\textnormal{Par}^{H}(\mathbbm{k})
is
\mathfrak{o}
. The morphism categories are given by

\Hom_{\textnormal{Par}^{H}(\mathbbm{k})}(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})\coloneq{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\textnormal{Par}^{H}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbbm{k})\coloneq\bigsqcup_{\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}}\Par({}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})

where composition is given by convolution.
We call
\textnormal{Par}^{H}(\mathbbm{k})
the
endoscopic Hecke 2-category of parity sheaves
.

5.10.
Block Conjugation Equivariance

We again are keeping the assumption that
G
is of finite type. This ensures that the Whittaker model of §
5.8.4
can be used.

5.10.1.
Groupoid Actions on Bicategories

Let
\Gamma
be a small groupoid.

Definition 5.10.2
.

(1)

Let
\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}=\{\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\circ}\}_{x\in\Ob(\Gamma)}
denote a collection of monoidal categories. Define a 2-category
\textnormal{Free}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ})
over
\Gamma
whose morphism categories are given by

\Hom_{\textnormal{Free}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ})}(x,y)\coloneq\Fun^{\simeq,\textnormal{mon}}(\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\circ},\mathcal{C}_{y}^{\circ})
where
\Fun^{\simeq,\textnormal{mon}}(\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\circ},\mathcal{C}_{y}^{\circ})
denotes the category of monoidal equivalences
\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\circ}\to\mathcal{C}_{y}^{\circ}
.

(2)

Let
\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}=\{\mathcal{D}_{x}^{\circ}\}_{x\in\Ob(\Gamma)}
denote another collection of monoidal categories. For each
x\in\Ob(\Gamma)
, let
F_{x}:\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\circ}\to\mathcal{D}_{x}^{\circ}
be a monoidal equivalence along with inverse
F_{x}^{-1}:\mathcal{D}_{x}^{\circ}\to\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\circ}
.
We can then define a 2-functor
F:\textnormal{Free}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ})\to\textnormal{Free}(\mathfrak{D}^{\circ})
which is the identity on objects and on morphism
categories is given by

\Fun^{\simeq,\textnormal{mon}}(\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\circ},\mathcal{C}_{y}^{\circ})\to\Fun^{\simeq,\textnormal{mon}}(\mathcal{D}_{x}^{\circ},\mathcal{D}_{y}^{\circ})\hskip 28.45274ptG\mapsto F_{y}\circ G\circ F_{x}^{-1}.

Definition 5.10.3
.

(1)

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be a 2-category over
\Gamma
. An
action
of
\Gamma
on
\mathfrak{C}
is a 2-functor
\Gamma\to\mathfrak{C}.
If
\mathfrak{D}
is a 2-category over

\Gamma
with a
\Gamma
-action
\Gamma\to\mathfrak{D}
, then a
\Gamma
-functor
from
\mathfrak{C}
to
\mathfrak{D}
is a 2-functor
\mathfrak{C}\to\mathfrak{D}
such that the
following diagram commutes up to natural isomorphism.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}}

(2)

Let
\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}\coloneq\{\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\circ}\}_{x\in\Ob(\Gamma)}
denote a collection of monoidal categories.
We define an
action
of
\Gamma
on
\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}
to be
\Gamma
-action on
\textnormal{Free}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ})
. Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\circ}\coloneq\{\mathcal{D}_{x}^{\circ}\}_{x\in\Ob(\Gamma)}
denote another collection of monoidal categories.
For each
x\in\Ob(\Gamma)
, let
F_{x}:\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\circ}\to\mathcal{D}_{x}^{\circ}
be a monoidal equivalence along with inverse
F_{x}^{-1}:\mathcal{D}_{x}^{\circ}\to\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\circ}
.
We say that the collection of functors
\{F_{x},F_{x}^{-1}\}_{x\in\Ob(\Gamma)}
is
\Gamma
-compatible
if the induced 2-functor
F:\textnormal{Free}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ})\to\textnormal{Free}(\mathfrak{D}^{\circ})
is a
\Gamma
-functor.

Let
\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}\coloneq\{\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\circ}\}_{x\in\Gamma}
be a collection of monoidal categories. Let
A^{\circ}:\Gamma\to\textnormal{Free}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ})
be a
\Gamma
-action on
\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}
.
We can then define the semidirect product
\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}\rtimes\Gamma
as follows.

(1)

\Ob(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}\rtimes\Gamma)=\Ob(\Gamma)
.

(2)

For
x,y\in\Ob(\Gamma)
, we define the morphism categories

\Hom_{\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}\rtimes\Gamma}(x,y)\coloneq\bigsqcup_{\xi\in{}_{y}\Gamma_{x}}{}_{y}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}\rtimes\Gamma)_{x}^{\xi}

where each
{}_{y}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}\rtimes\Gamma)_{x}^{\xi}
is defined to be a copy of
\mathcal{C}_{y}^{\circ}
.

(3)

For
\xi\in{}_{y}\Gamma_{x}
and
\eta\in{}_{z}\Gamma_{y}
, the composition of 1-morphisms is given by

{}_{z}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}\rtimes\Gamma)_{y}^{\eta}\times{}_{y}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}\rtimes\Gamma)_{x}^{\xi}\to{}_{z}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}\rtimes\Gamma)_{x}^{\eta\xi}

(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\mapsto\mathcal{G}\circ(A^{\circ}(\eta))(\mathcal{F}).

The semidirect product comes equipped with a canonical
\Gamma
-action,
\tilde{A}:\Gamma\to\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}\rtimes\Gamma
.
Explicitly,
\tilde{A}
is the identity on objects, and for
\xi\in{}_{y}\Gamma_{x}
, we define
\tilde{A}(\xi)=\mathds{1}_{y}\in\mathcal{C}_{y}^{\circ}\eqcolon{}_{y}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}\rtimes\Gamma)_{x}^{\xi}
.

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be a 2-category over
\Gamma
with
\Gamma
-action
A:\Gamma\to\mathfrak{C}
.
Consider the collection of monoidal categories
\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}=\{{}_{x}\mathfrak{C}_{x}^{\id_{x}}\}_{x\in\Ob(\Gamma)}
.
For each
\xi\in{}_{y}\Gamma_{x}
, we can define a monoidal equivalence

A^{\circ}(\xi):{}_{x}\mathfrak{C}_{x}^{\id_{x}}\to{}_{y}\mathfrak{C}_{y}^{\id_{y}}.

These monoidal equivalences can be assembled into a 2-functor
A^{\circ}:\Gamma\to\textnormal{Free}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ})
. In other words,
A:\Gamma\to\mathfrak{C}

induces a
\Gamma
-action on
\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}
.

The following lemmas follow easily from the definitions.

Lemma 5.10.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be a 2-category over
\Gamma
with
\Gamma
-action. Consider the collection of monoidal categories
\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}=\{{}_{x}\mathfrak{C}_{x}^{\id_{x}}\}_{x\in\Ob(\Gamma)}
.
Then there is a canonical
\Gamma
-functor

\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}\rtimes\Gamma\to\mathfrak{C}.

Lemma 5.10.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}=\{\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\circ}\}_{x\in\Ob(\Gamma)}
and
\mathfrak{D}^{\circ}=\{\mathcal{D}_{x}^{\circ}\}_{x\in\Ob(\Gamma)}
be collections of monoidal categories.
For each
x\in\Ob(\Gamma)
, let
F_{x}:\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\circ}\to\mathcal{D}_{x}^{\circ}
be a monoidal equivalence along with inverse
F_{x}^{-1}:\mathcal{D}_{x}^{\circ}\to\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\circ}
such that the collection
\{F_{x},F_{x}^{-1}\}_{x\in\Ob(\Gamma)}
is
\Gamma
-compatible.
Then there is a canonical
\Gamma
-functor

\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}\rtimes\Gamma\to\mathfrak{D}^{\circ}\rtimes\Gamma

which is an equivalence of 2-categories.

5.10.6.
Uniqueness of Frobenius Structures

Let
s\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
. Let
\underline{s}
be an endo-reduced expression for
s
in
W
.
In §
4.3
, we constructed a Frobenius algebra structure (
\mu_{\underline{s}},\nu_{\underline{s}},\epsilon_{\underline{s}},\eta_{\underline{s}}
) on
\underline{s}
.
11
11

11

In
§
4.3
, we denote the Frobenius structure maps by (
\mu_{s},\nu_{s},\epsilon_{s},\eta_{s}
). We have changed the notation in this section to
emphasize that, for now, the Frobenius structure depends on the choice of endo-simple expression
\underline{s}
.

The goal of this section is to compare the Frobenius structures for different choices of endo-reduced expressions.

Lemma 5.10.7
.

Let
s\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
. Let
\underline{s}
and
\underline{s}^{\prime}
be endo-reduced expressions for
s
in
W
.
Then there exists a unique isomorphism of Frobenius algebras
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{s}}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\mathcal{E}_{\underline{s}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{L}}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{s}}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\cong\mathcal{E}_{\underline{s}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{L}}
, the
\mathbbm{k}
-module of homomorphisms
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{s}}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\mathcal{E}_{\underline{s}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{L}}
is rank 1.
There is then a unique isomorphism
\alpha:\mathcal{E}_{\underline{s}}^{\mathcal{L}}\to\mathcal{E}_{\underline{s}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{L}}
such that
\epsilon_{\underline{s}^{\prime}}\circ\alpha=\epsilon_{\underline{s}}
.

We claim that
\alpha
is an isomorphism of Frobenius algebras.
By Lemma
4.3.6
,
\Hom(\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}},\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\star\IC_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}[-1])\cong\mathbbm{k}
. As a result, the counit equations along with

\epsilon_{\underline{s}^{\prime}}\circ\alpha=\epsilon_{\underline{s}}
imply that
\alpha
is an isomorphism of coalgebras.
By Theorem
5.3.1
, under the isomorphism
\Hom(\IC_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}[-1],\IC_{e}^{\mathcal{L}}[1])\cong H_{T}^{2}(\pt;\mathbbm{k})
, both

\epsilon_{\underline{s}}\circ\eta_{\underline{s}}
and
\epsilon_{\underline{s}^{\prime}}\circ\eta_{\underline{s}^{\prime}}
correspond to
\alpha_{s}\in H_{T}^{2}(\pt;\mathbbm{k})
.
As a result, we can conclude that
\alpha\circ\eta_{\underline{s}}=\eta_{\underline{s}^{\prime}}
.
The unit equations and another application of Lemma
4.3.6
force
\alpha
to also be an isomorphism of algebras.
∎

5.10.8.
Compatibilities with Block Conjugation

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
.
We have an isomorphism of Coxeter groups
(W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ},S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\to(W_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ},S_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ})
which induces monoidal equivalences of categories
