{}^{\beta}(-):\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\to\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt{}^{\beta}(-):\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})\to\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}).

Let
\underline{x}
be an endo-reduced expression for
w^{\beta}
. Let
\overline{\underline{x}}
denote the reversed expression for
\underline{x}
. The functor

{}^{\underline{x}}(-):{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\circ,\BS}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ,\BS}(\mathbbm{k})\hskip 28.45274pt\mathcal{F}\mapsto\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}\star\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{E}_{\overline{\underline{x}}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}

is an equivalence of monoidal categories.

Lemma 5.10.9
.

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
. Let
\underline{x}
be an endo-reduced expression for
w^{\beta}
.
There is a natural isomorphism of functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ,\BS}(\mathbbm{k})}
{}^{\underline{x}}\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(-)
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}{}^{\beta}(-)
C_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}

Moreover, for all
\gamma\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
and endo-reduced expression
\underline{y}
of
w^{\gamma}
, we have that
C_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\circ{}^{\underline{y}}C_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}=C_{\underline{y}\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}
.

Proof.

Since
{}^{\underline{x}}\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(-)
and
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}{}^{\beta}(-)
are monoidal functors, it suffices to define
C_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}
just on
B_{s}
where

s\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
.
Let
u=w^{\beta}sw^{\beta,-1}
which is an endosimple reflection for
W_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}
.
By Lemma
5.10.7
, there is then a unique Frobenius algebra isomorphism

C_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}(B_{s}):\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}\underline{s}\overline{\underline{x}}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{E}_{\underline{u}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}

where
\underline{s}
(resp.
\underline{u}
) is the endo-reduced expression for
s
(resp.
u
) fixed in the construction of
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
(resp.
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}
).

It suffices to check that
C_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}
defines a natural transformation just on generators for the morphisms in
\mathcal{D}_{\BS}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})
.
The compatibility conditions for one-color morphisms follows from
C_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}(B_{s})
being an isomorphism of Frobenius algebras.
As a result, we only need to check the compatibility condition for the
2m
-valent vertex.
Let
t\in S_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
and
v=w^{\beta}tw^{\beta,-1}
. Note that
m_{s,t}=m_{u,v}
. We use the notation introduced in §
5.4
.
We must show that the following diagram commutes

(5.10.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\underline{x}}(\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\underline{x}}(\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathcal{L}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{v}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\underline{x}}f_{s,t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{u,v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}}

Consider the following “all-dot” maps:

{}^{\underline{x}}\eta_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}\coloneq\id_{\mathcal{E}_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}}\star\underbrace{\epsilon_{\underline{s}}\star\epsilon_{\underline{t}}\star\ldots}_{m_{s,t}\text{ terms}}\star\id_{\mathcal{E}_{\overline{\underline{x}}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}:\mathcal{E}_{\varnothing}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[-m_{s,t}]\to{}^{\underline{x}}(\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}),

\epsilon_{v}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\coloneq\underbrace{\epsilon_{\underline{v}}\star\epsilon_{\underline{u}}\star\ldots}_{m_{s,t}\text{ terms}}:\mathcal{F}_{v}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{E}_{\varnothing}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[m_{s,t}].

Since
C_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}
is a morphism of Frobenius algebras on simple reflections and Theorem
5.3.1
, under the isomorphism
\Hom(\mathcal{E}_{\varnothing}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[-m_{s,t}],\mathcal{E}_{\varnothing}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[m_{s,t}])\cong H_{T}^{2m_{s,t}}(\pt;\mathbbm{k})
, we have that

\epsilon_{v}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\circ(C_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}\circ{{}^{\underline{x}}f_{s,t}})\circ{}^{\underline{x}}\eta_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}=\mu_{s,t}=\epsilon_{v}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\circ(f_{u,v}\circ C_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}})\circ{}^{\underline{x}}\eta_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}

where
\mu_{s,t}
is the product of all positive roots in the root subsystem corresponding to
s,t
.
Since
\Hom({}^{\underline{x}}(\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\mathcal{L}}),\mathcal{F}_{v}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}})\cong\mathbbm{k}
, we can conclude that
C_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}\circ{{}^{\underline{x}}f_{s,t}}=f_{u,v}\circ C_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}
.

Finally, if
\gamma\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
and endo-reduced expression
\underline{y}
of
w^{\gamma}
, then
C_{\underline{y}}^{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\circ{}^{\underline{y}}C_{\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}=C_{\underline{y}\underline{x}}^{\mathcal{L}}
by the uniqueness constraint of Lemma
5.10.7
.
∎

Lemma 5.10.10
.

Assume that
\mathbbm{k}
is a field or a complete local ring.
There is a natural isomorphism making the following diagram commutative

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathbbm{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*},W_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}(\mathbbm{k})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\beta}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\beta}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}}

Moreover, these isomorphisms are compatible for composable blocks.

Proof.

The statement follows from composing natural isomorphism given in Lemma
5.10.9
(after taking the additive hull and idempotent
completion) with the natural isomorphisms given in Lemma
5.8.10
.
∎

Let
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
. Let
\dot{w}
be a lift of
w^{\beta}
in
N_{G}(T)
.
The isomorphism
\sigma(\dot{w}):H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}\to H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
induces an equivalence of categories

{}^{\beta}(-):\Par(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})\to\Par(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}},\mathbbm{k}).

Explicitly, for
\mathcal{F}\in\Par(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})
, there is a canonical isomorphism

{}^{\beta}\mathcal{F}\cong\Delta_{w^{\beta}}^{H,\mathcal{L}}\star\mathcal{F}\star\Delta_{w^{\beta,-1}}^{H,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}.

In particular,
{}^{\beta}(-)
is independent of the choice of lift
\dot{w}
.

The following lemma is then immediate from Lemma
5.10.10
and the uniqueness of relative pinnings.

Lemma 5.10.11
.

Assume that
\mathbbm{k}
is a field or a complete local ring.
There is a natural isomorphism making the following diagram commutative

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Par(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\>{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\Par_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathbbm{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Par(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}},\mathbbm{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\>{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\Par_{/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}(\mathbbm{k})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\beta}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\beta}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{\circ}}

Moreover, these isomorphisms are compatible for composable blocks.

5.11.
Monodromic-Endoscopic Equivalence for All Blocks

We can now state the extension of Theorem
5.3.1
to the non-neutral blocks.

Theorem 5.11.1

(Monodromic-Endoscopic equivalence for all blocks)
.

Let
\mathbbm{k}
be a complete local ring or a field which admits a weak-Cohen lift.
Assume that
G
is of finite type.
There is an equivalence of 2-categories over
\Xi
,

\Psi:\textnormal{Par}^{H}(\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/}(\mathbbm{k}).

For all blocks
\beta\in{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\underline{W}_{\mathcal{L}}
,
\Psi
restricts to an equivalence of categories

{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\Psi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}:\Par({}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})\to{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}(\mathbbm{k})

such that for all
w\in\beta
,
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\Psi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\beta}
takes
\mathcal{E}_{w}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
to
\mathcal{E}_{w}^{\mathcal{L}}
.
Moreover, when
\beta
is the neutral block,
{}_{\mathcal{L}}\Psi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
is the equivalence
\Psi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}
from Theorem
5.3.1
.

Proof.

By Corollary
5.8.11
, there is a
\Xi
-action on
{}_{\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/}(\mathbbm{k})
.
By Lemma
5.10.4
, this action induces a
\Xi
-functor

(5.11.1)

{}_{\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/}^{\circ}(\mathbbm{k})\rtimes\Xi\to{}_{\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/}(\mathbbm{k})

By Proposition
3.3.17
, the 2-functor (
5.11.1
) is an equivalence of 2-categories over
\Xi
.

Similarly, one can define a 2-functor
\Xi\to\textnormal{Par}^{H}(\mathbbm{k})
by
\beta\mapsto\Delta_{w^{\beta}}^{H,\mathcal{L}}
.
This induces a
\Xi
-functor

(5.11.2)

\Par^{H,\circ}(\mathbbm{k})\rtimes\Xi\to\textnormal{Par}^{H}(\mathbbm{k})

By Corollary
5.9.6
, the 2-functor (
5.11.2
) is also an equivalence of 2-categories over
\Xi
.

By Theorem
5.3.1
, for each
\mathcal{L}\in\mathfrak{o}
, there is an equivalence of monoidal categories

\Psi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}:\Par(\mathcal{F}\ell_{H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}}/B_{\mathcal{L}},\mathbbm{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{}_{\mathcal{L}\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}(\mathbbm{k})

From Lemma
5.10.11
, the collection
\{\Psi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}\}_{\mathcal{L}\in\mathfrak{o}}
is a
\Xi
-compatible collection.
By Lemma
5.10.5
, we get an equivalence of 2-categories over
\Xi
,

(5.11.3)

\Par^{H,\circ}(\mathbbm{k})\rtimes\Xi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{}_{\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/}^{\circ}(\mathbbm{k})\rtimes\Xi.

The proof then follows from combining the equivalences (
5.11.1
), (
5.11.2
), and (
5.11.3
).
∎

Remark 5.11.2
.

The only place where finite type is essentially used is in the construction of the 2-functor
\IC_{\min}:\Xi\to{}_{\backslash}\textnormal{Par}_{/}(\mathbbm{k})
from Corollary

5.8.11
.
We conjecture that this functor should exist even when
G
is of affine type. Whenever
\IC_{\min}
can be constructed, the argument for Theorem

5.11.1
we have provided will generalize.

Appendix A
Six-Functor Formalisms

Six-functor formalisms for étale constructible sheaves has been well-developed (c.f.,
[
LZ17
,
Man22
,
Sch22
]
).
However, a six-functor formalism
for the complex analytic topology is not detailed in the literature.
We will develop
such a six-functor formalism for three notable settings:

(1)

Constructible sheaves on schemes over
\mathbb{C}
;

(2)

Constructible sheaves on algebraic stacks over
\mathbb{C}
;

(3)

Twisted equivariant constructible sheaves on stacks over
\mathbb{C}
.

The scheme and algebraic stack settings require essentially no modifications from work covered in existing literature.
For these, we will only recall the basic constructions and some key results.

The twisted equivariant setting is not substantively covered in existing literature.
The main goal of this appendix is then to provide a thorough treatment of the twisted equivariant setting which allows for more general coefficient rings and monodromy
parameters than covered by
[
Gou21
]
and
[
LY20
]
.

A.0.1.
Preliminaries on
(\infty,1)
-categories

Unlike the rest of the paper, this appendix will work with the formalism of
\infty
-categories, and more precisely the model of quasicategories presented in
[
Lur09
]

and
[
Lur17
]
.

We denote by
\infty\textnormal{-}\Cat
(resp.
\infty\textnormal{-}\Cat_{\mathbbm{k}}
) the
\infty
-category of small stable
\infty
-categories (resp. the
\infty
-category of
\mathbbm{k}
-linear small
\infty
-categories).
Similarly, we denote by
Pr
(resp.
\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}
) the
\infty
-category of presentable stable
\infty
-categories (resp. the
\infty
-category of
\mathbbm{k}
-linear presentable

\infty
-categories).
We will write
\textnormal{Pr}^{L}_{\mathbbm{k}}
(resp.
\textnormal{Pr}^{R}_{\mathbbm{k}}
or
\textnormal{Pr}^{LR}_{\mathbbm{k}}
) for the 1-full subcategory of
\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}
where we restrict morphisms to continuous (resp. cocontinuous
or both continuous and cocontinuous) functors.

The Lurie tensor product
\otimes
equips
\textnormal{Pr}^{L}_{\mathbbm{k}}
with the structure of a symmetric monoidal category.
If
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}\in\Alg(\textnormal{Pr}_{L}^{\mathbbm{k}})
are algebras, then we can consider the category of
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
-bimodules
{}_{\mathcal{A}}\textnormal{Bimod}_{\mathcal{B}}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})
.
The relative Lurie tensor product defines a functor

(-)\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}(-):{}_{\mathcal{A}}\textnormal{Bimod}_{\mathcal{B}}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})\times{}_{\mathcal{B}}\textnormal{Bimod}_{\mathcal{C}}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})\to{}_{\mathcal{A}}\textnormal{Bimod}_{\mathcal{C}}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})

Similarly, if we just consider left
\mathcal{B}
-modules,
\Mod_{\mathcal{B}}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})
, the relative tensor product gives rise to a functor

(-)\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}(-):{}_{\mathcal{A}}\textnormal{Bimod}_{\mathcal{B}}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})\times\Mod_{\mathcal{B}}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})\to\Mod_{\mathcal{A}}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L}).

There is a functor

\Ind:\infty\textnormal{-}\Cat\to\textnormal{Pr}^{L}

called the
ind-completion
which freely adds filtered colimits to a small
\infty
-category
[
Lur17
, §5.5]
.
Similarly, there is a functor

(-)^{c}:\textnormal{Pr}^{L}\to\infty\textnormal{-}\Cat

which takes a presentable
\infty
-category to its small subcategory of compact objects.

For a (dg-)algebra
A
over
\mathbbm{k}
, we will write
D(A)
for the
\infty
-derived category of
A
.

A.1.
Six-Functor Formalisms

We first recall some preliminaries on 3-functor and 6-functor formalisms. For a comprehensive treatise, see works of Scholze
[
Sch22
]
and Mann
[
Man22
]
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a category admitting all finite limits. Let
E
be a class of morphisms of
\mathcal{C}
which is stable under pullback, composition, and contains all isomorphisms.
The pair
(\mathcal{C},E)
is called a
geometric setup
.
Associated to a geometric setup
(\mathcal{C},E)
is the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\Corr(\mathcal{C},E)
of correspondences.
If
E
consists of all morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
, we will shorten
\Corr(\mathcal{C},E)
to
\Corr(\mathcal{C})
.
The objects of
\Corr(\mathcal{C},E)
are the same as the objects of
\mathcal{C}
. The 1-morphisms
X\to Y
in
\Corr(\mathcal{C},E)
are given by correspondence diagrams

X\leftarrow Z\to Y

where the maps
Z\to Y
belong to
E
. The monoidal structure on
\Corr(\mathcal{C},E)
is via the Cartesian product in
\mathcal{C}
.

Definition A.1.1
.

A
3-functor formalism
is a lax-symmetric monoidal functor
D:\Corr(\mathcal{C},E)\to\infty\textnormal{-}\Cat
.

Let
D:\Corr(\mathcal{C},E)\to\infty\textnormal{-}\Cat
be a 3-functor formalism. Let
f:X\to Y
.

(1)

We can form a correspondence
Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\leftarrow}}X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle=}}{{\to}}X
. The
pullback
functor along
f
, denoted by
f^{*}:D(Y)\to D(X)
, is the
functor obtained by applying
D
to the aforementioned correspondence.

(2)

If
f\in E
, we can form a correspondence
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle=}}{{\leftarrow}}X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}X
. The
proper pushforward
functor along
f
, denoted by
f_{!}:D(X)\to D(Y)
, is the functor obtained by applying
D
to the aforementioned correspondence diagram.

(3)

Since
D
is a lax-symmetric monoidal functor, we can equip
D(X)
with the structure of a symmetric monoidal category given by the composition of functors

D(X)\times D(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\boxtimes}}{{\to}}D(X\times X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta^{*}}}{{\to}}D(X)

where the first map, called the
external tensor product
, comes from the monoidality constraint and the second map is pullback along the diagonal map
\Delta:X\to X\times X
.
The resulting bifunctor on
D(X)
, denoted
\otimes^{L}
, is called the
sheaf tensor product
.

Definition A.1.2
.

A 3-functor formalism
D:\Corr(\mathcal{C},E)\to\infty\textnormal{-}\Cat
is said to be a
6-functor formalism
if all the functors
f^{*}
,
f_{!}
and
A\otimes^{L}-
admit right adjoints.

Let
D:\Corr(\mathcal{C},E)\to\infty\textnormal{-}\Cat
be a 3-functor formalism. Let
f:X\to Y
.

(1)

The
pushforward
functor along
f
, denoted by
f_{*}:D(X)\to D(Y)
, is the right adjoint of
f^{*}
.

(2)

Assume that
f\in E
. The
exceptional pullback
functor along
f
, denoted by
f^{!}:D(Y)\to D(X)
, is the right adjoint of
f_{!}
.

(3)

The
sheaf Hom
functor, denoted by
\textbf{R}\mathcal{H}om(-,-):D(X)^{\op}\times D(X)\to D(X)
, is the right adjoint of
\otimes^{L}
.

Remark A.1.3
.

It is often useful to consider 3-functor/6-functor formalisms taking values in
Pr
,
\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}
, or
\infty\textnormal{-}\Cat_{\mathbbm{k}}
instead of
\infty\textnormal{-}\Cat
.
All the definitions given in this section make sense with these replacements.

A.2.
Constructible Sheaves on Schemes

Let
\Sch_{\mathbb{C}}
be the category of separated schemes of finite type over
\mathbb{C}
.
Given a complex scheme
X
, we can equip its
\mathbb{C}
-points,
X(\mathbb{C})
, with its analytic topology. There exists a six-functor formalism

D(-,\mathbbm{k}):\Corr(\Sch_{\mathbb{C}})\to\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L},\hskip 28.45274ptX\mapsto D(X,\mathbbm{k})

where
D(X,\mathbbm{k})
is the unbounded
\infty
-derived category of
\mathbbm{k}
-valued sheaves on
X(\mathbb{C})
(see
[
Lur09
]
).
The construction of this six-functor formalism is well-known. For example, such a six-functor formalism is carefully detailed in
[
Sch22
]
where
\Sch_{\mathbb{C}}
is replaced
by locally compact Hausdorff topological spaces.
The only substantive difference when working instead with
\Sch_{\mathbb{C}}
is that the Stone-Čech compactification should be replaced by the Nagata compactification.

There are a few features of sheaves which are not encoded in abstract six-functor formalisms. Since they will be significant for our purposes, we will detail them here.

(1)

If
\mathbbm{k}\to\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}
is a ring homomorphism, there is an
extension of scalars
functor

\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}(-):D(X,\mathbbm{k})\to D(X,\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}).

Extension of scalars commutes with
\boxtimes
and
f^{*}
.

(2)

Let
\Sh(X,\mathbbm{k})
denote the ordinary abelian category of sheaves on
X(\mathbb{C})
with
\mathbbm{k}
-coefficients. It is the heart of the natural
t
-structure on
D(X,\mathbbm{k})
.
In particular, for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, there are cohomology functors

H^{n}:D(X,\mathbbm{k})\to\Sh(X,\mathbbm{k})

which are intertwined by the suspension functor,
H^{0}((-)[1])=H^{1}(-)
.

We can now begin the process of restricting the six-functor formalism to the so-called
constructible sheaves
.
Let
\Loc(X,\mathbbm{k})
denote the full (abelian) subcategory of
\Sh(X,\mathbbm{k})\subset D(X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of local systems on
X
.
We will write
\Loc_{\textnormal{f}}(X,\mathbbm{k})
for the full subcategory of
\Loc(X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of finite rank local systems.

Definition A.2.1
.

A bounded complex of sheaves
\mathcal{F}\in D(X,\mathbbm{k})
is said to be
lisse
if
H^{n}(\mathcal{F})\in\Loc_{\textnormal{f}}(X,\mathbbm{k})
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Let
D_{\lisse}(X,\mathbbm{k})
denote the full subcategory of
D(X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of lisse sheaves.

Define the category of
ind-lisse
sheaves, denoted
D_{\indlisse}(X,\mathbbm{k})
, as the full subcategory of
D(X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of sheaves which are equivalent to
filtered colimits of lisse sheaves.

Definition A.2.2
.

A bounded complex of sheaves
\mathcal{F}\in D(X,\mathbbm{k})
is said to be
constructible
if there exists a stratification
\{X_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
such that

\mathcal{F}|_{X_{\lambda}}
is
lisse for all
\lambda\in\Lambda
.
Let
D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})
denote the full subcategory of
D(X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of constructible sheaves.

Define the category of
ind-constructible
sheaves, denoted
D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
as the full subcategory of
D(X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of sheaves which are equivalent
to filtered colimits of constructible sheaves.

Lemma A.2.3

(
[
HRS23
, Proposition 8.2]
)
.

A sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
is compact if and only if
\mathcal{F}
is constructible. Likewise,
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\indlisse}(X,\mathbbm{k})
is compact if and only if
\mathcal{F}
is lisse.

Corollary A.2.4
.

The inclusions
D_{\lisse}(X,\mathbbm{k})
and
D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})\subset D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
extend to a colimit-preserving equivalences

\Ind(D_{\lisse}(X,\mathbbm{k}))\cong D_{\indlisse}(X,\mathbbm{k})

\Ind(D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k}))\cong D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})

As a result,
D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
is presentable.

Lemma A.2.5
.

The six sheaf functors take constructible sheaves to constructible sheaves. As a result, there are six-functor formalisms

\displaystyle D_{\cons}(-,\mathbbm{k}):\Corr(\Sch_{\mathbb{C}})

\displaystyle\to\infty\textnormal{-}\Cat_{\mathbbm{k}},

\displaystyle D_{\ic}(-,\mathbbm{k}):\Corr(\Sch_{\mathbb{C}})

\displaystyle\to\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L},

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle\mapsto D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k}),

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle\mapsto D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k}).

Proof.

The six-functor formalism for constructible sheaves follows from the classical fact that the six functors preserve constructible sheaves.
The extension to ind-constructible follows from Corollary
A.2.4
.
∎

Remark A.2.6
.

Let
\mathbbm{k}\to\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}
be a ring homomorphism. extension of scalars preserves constructibility, so there is a functor

\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}(-):D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}).

Similarly, there is an extension of scalars functor for ind-constructible sheaves.

Remark A.2.7
.

The homotopy category of
D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})
recovers the usual bounded derived category of constructible sheaves on
X
with coefficients in
\mathbbm{k}
.

A.3.
Constructible Sheaves on Stacks

Let
\Stk_{\mathbb{C}}
denote the category of algebraic stacks locally of finite type over
\mathbb{C}
.
12
12

12

On occasion, we write stack to refer to a (2,1)-sheaf with the fppf
topology. We reserve algebraic stack to mean a stack which has representable diagonal and a smooth atlas.

For an algebraic stack
X
, we can define the over slice category
\Sch_{/X}
as the category of representable morphisms
S\to X
where
S
is a scheme.

Definition A.3.1
.

Let
X\in\Stk_{\mathbb{C}}
. Define a category
D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
given by,

D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})=\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longleftarrow}}{{S\in\Stk_{/X}}}}D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k}),

where the transition maps are given by
*
-pullbacks.

We define
D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})\subset D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
as the full subcategory of
D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
of objects
\mathcal{F}
such that for all
f:S\to X
with
S\in\Sch_{\mathbb{C}}
, one has that
f^{*}\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(S,\mathbbm{k})
.

We call the objects of
D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})
(resp.
D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
) are called
constructible
(resp.
ind-constructible
) sheaves on
X
.

Proposition A.3.2

(
[
Man22
, Proposition A.5.16]
)
.

There is an extension of the 6-functor formalism for schemes to a 6-functor formalism

D_{\ic}(-,\mathbbm{k}):\Corr(\Stk_{\mathbb{C}})\to\textnormal{Pr}^{L}\hskip 28.45274ptX\mapsto D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k}).

A.3.3.
Smooth Descent

In the definition of ind-constructible sheaves on algebraic stacks, we take a limit over a large class of objects.
This is beneficial since it allows us to build the six-formalism via Kan extensions. However, in practice
this also makes constructing sheaves on an algebraic stack rather difficult due to the extensive
number of compatibilities one must check. A convenient way to simplify this complexity is via smooth
*
-descent.

Definition A.3.4
.

Let
D
be a 6-functor formalism on
\Sch_{\mathbb{C}}
. Let
f:X\to Y
be a smooth surjective morphism of schemes.
Let
X^{n/Y}
denote the
n
-fold product of
X
over
Y
. This comes equipped with projection maps
f_{n}:X^{n/Y}\to Y
.
We say that
D
satisfies
smooth
*
-descent along
f
if

(f_{\Delta}^{*}):D(Y)\to\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longleftarrow}}{{\Delta}}}D(X^{n/Y})

is an isomorphism where the transition maps of the limit are given by
*
-pullback.

Proposition A.3.5
.

The 6-functor formalism
D_{\ic}
on
\Sch_{\mathbb{C}}
satisfies smooth
*
-descent.

Proof.

By
[
Sch22
, Proposition 6.18]
, it suffices to show that for any smooth surjective morphism
f:X\to Y
, the functor
f^{*}:D_{\ic}(Y,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})

is conservative. This can be shown via a simple stalk computation.
∎

Let
\pi:U\to X
be a smooth atlas of
X
. Consider the Čech nerve
C_{\bullet}(\pi)
. It is the simplicial object in
\Sch_{\mathbb{C}}
whose degree
n
term is
U^{n/X}
.
Note that
U^{n/X}\in\Sch_{/X}
.

Lemma A.3.6
.

The induced functor

\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longleftarrow}}{{S\in C_{\bullet}(\pi)}}}D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k})\to\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longleftarrow}}{{S\in\Sch_{/X}}}}D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k})

is an equivalence of categories.

Proof.

The lemma amounts to showing that for every
\mathcal{F}\in\lim_{S\in C_{\bullet}(\pi)}D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k})
, we can uniquely define
\mathcal{F}(T)
for all
T\in\Sch_{/X}
such
that for any
f:T\to T^{\prime}
in
\Sch_{/X}
, there is an isomorphism
f^{*}\mathcal{F}(T^{\prime})\cong\mathcal{F}(T)
.
Moreover, these isomorphisms should extend those given in
\mathcal{F}(U^{n/X})
.

Let
Z=T\times_{X}U
which is a scheme equipped with maps
p_{T}:Z\to T
and
p_{U}^{0}:Z\to U
. Consider the commutative cube

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{1/T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{1/X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{U}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{U}^{0}}

where
p_{U}^{1}:Z^{T/1}\to U^{X/1}
follows from the universal property of pullbacks. We can repeat this process to obtain morphisms
p_{U}^{n}:Z^{n/T}\to U^{n/X}

which fit in the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{2/X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{1/X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{2/T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{1/T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{U}^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{U}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{U}^{0}}

Note that
C_{\bullet}(\pi)=U^{\bullet/X}
and the above diagram gives a morphism of simplicial schemes
p_{U}:Z^{\bullet/T}\to U^{\bullet/X}
. Therefore, we can
define
\mathcal{G}\in\lim D_{\ic}(Z^{n/T},\mathbbm{k})
by
p_{u}^{*}\mathcal{G}
.
By Proposition
A.3.5
, we have that there is a unique object
\mathcal{F}(T)\in D_{\ic}(T)
such that its
*
-pullback to
Z^{n/T}
is
\mathcal{G}
.
It is clear that this object satisfies the pullback compatibilities extending
\mathcal{F}
uniquely to an object in
D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
.
∎

A.3.7.
Equivariant Sheaves

Let
H
be an algebraic group over
\mathbb{C}
. By algebraic group, we will always mean a group scheme of finite type which is not necessarily reduced. Suppose
H
acts on a
scheme
S\in\Sch_{\mathbb{C}}
. We write
H\backslash S
for the quotient stack afforded by this action.
The
H
-equivariant constructible sheaves on
S
should be thought of as the category of constructible sheaves on
H\backslash S
.

In the remainder of the section, we will describe
D_{\ic}(H\backslash S,\mathbbm{k})
in the language of categorical representation theory. This perspective
is particularly nice as it allows one to construct
D_{\ic}(H\backslash S,\mathbbm{k})
from
D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k})
via homological algebra.
This perspective will be invaluable when defining twisted equivariant sheaves.

We can equip
D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})
with a monoidal structure. Let
m:H\times H\to H
denote the multiplication map. The convolution is defined as

\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{G}\coloneq m_{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{G})

where
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})
and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
.

One can also define a
D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})
-module structure on
D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k})
through similar means. Namely, let
a:H\times S\to S
denote the action map. We can then define

\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{G}\coloneq a_{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{G})

where
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(H,\mathbbm{k})
and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\cons}(S,\mathbbm{k})
.

Definition A.3.8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\Mod_{D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})}(\textnormal{Pr}^{L})
. We define

(1)

the category of
H
-invariants

\mathcal{C}^{H}\coloneq\Hom_{D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})}(D(\mathbbm{k}),\mathcal{C})
,

(2)

the category of
H
-coinvariants

\mathcal{C}_{H}\coloneq\mathcal{C}\otimes_{D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})}D(\mathbbm{k})
.

Theorem A.3.9

(
[
Gai20
, Theorem B.1.2]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\Mod_{D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})}(\textnormal{Pr}^{L})
.
The natural forgetful functor
\For_{H}:\mathcal{C}^{H}\to\mathcal{C}
admits a left adjoint
\Av_{H!}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{H}
which factors through an equivalence of categories

\mathcal{C}_{H}\to\mathcal{C}^{H}.

Proof.

The argument given in
[
Gai20
]
is for
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
sheaves; however, the only characteristic dependent part of the argument is the statement that
R\Gamma(\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{H})\in D(\mathbbm{k})
is compact.
Since
\mathbbm{k}
has finite global dimension and since
H
is finite dimensional,
R\Gamma(\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{H})
is a perfect
\mathbbm{k}
-module.
∎

Proposition A.3.10
.

There is an equivalence of categories,

D_{\ic}(H\backslash S,\mathbbm{k})\cong D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k})^{H}

Proof.

Let
\pi:S\to H\backslash S
be the quotient map. Note that
\pi
is a smooth atlas for
H\backslash S
.
From the same argument given in Theorem
A.3.9
,
D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})
is compactly generated and in fact self-dual via the Verdier duality functor.
We can then compute

\displaystyle\Hom_{\textnormal{Pr}^{L}}(D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})^{\otimes n}\otimes\Vect,D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k}))

\displaystyle\cong\Hom_{\textnormal{Pr}^{L}}(D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})^{\otimes n},D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k}))

\displaystyle\cong(D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})^{\otimes n})^{\vee}\otimes D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k})

\displaystyle\cong D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})^{\otimes n}\otimes D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k})

One can then compute
D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k})^{H}
as the totalization of the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})\otimes D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})^{\otimes 2}\otimes D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots{}}

By
[
Gai20
, 1.4.6]
, this can be rewritten as the following the totalization of the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D_{\ic}(S,\mathbbm{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D_{\ic}(H\times S,\mathbbm{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D_{\ic}(H^{2}\times S,\mathbbm{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots{}}

whose transition maps are given by pullbacks along partial multiplication maps.

Likewise, the Čech complex
C_{\bullet}(\pi)
is given by
S^{n/(H\backslash S)}\cong H^{n}\times S
where the projection maps
S^{n/(H\backslash S)}\to S^{(n-1)/(H\backslash S)}
become the partial multiplication maps
H^{n}\times S\to H^{n-1}\times S
.
As a result, the proposition then follows from Lemma
A.3.6
.
∎

We will finish this section by comparing the homotopy category of
D_{\cons}(H\backslash S,\mathbbm{k})
with the Bernstein-Lunts triangulated derived category of

H
-equivariant sheaves on
S
.

Proposition A.3.11
.

There is an equivalence of triangulated categories,

\Ho(D_{c}(H\backslash S,\mathbbm{k}))\cong hD_{H}(S,\mathbbm{k})

where
hD_{H}(S,\mathbbm{k})
is the triangulated bounded derived category of
H
-equivariant constructible sheaves on
S
.

Proof.

Let
\Sch_{/(H\backslash S)}^{\textnormal{resn}}
be the category of
H
-resolutions over
S
.
Explicitly, the objects in
\Sch_{/(H\backslash S)}^{\textnormal{resn}}
consist of pairs
(H\backslash P,\pi)
where
P
is a principle
H
-bundle equipped with a
smooth
H
-equivariant map
\pi:P\to S
. We will also write
\pi:H\backslash P\to H\backslash S
for the induced map on quotient stacks.
For a fixed
H
-resolution
P
,
P\times_{S}P
is also an
H
-resolution, and
\pi:H\backslash P\to H\backslash S
is a smooth atlas.
By Lemma
A.3.6
, one has that

D_{\ic}(H\backslash S,\mathbbm{k})\cong\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longleftarrow}}{{H\backslash P\in\Sch_{/(H\backslash S)}^{\textnormal{resn}}}}}D_{\ic}(H\backslash P,\mathbbm{k}).

We can then construct the functor,

\Phi:\Ho(D_{\cons}(H\backslash S,\mathbbm{k}))\to hD_{H}(S,\mathbbm{k})

by
\Phi(\mathcal{F})=(P\mapsto\mathcal{F}(H\backslash P))
.
It then follows from Remark
A.2.7
that this will give an equivalence of triangulated categories.
∎

A.4.
Twisted Equivariant Sheaves

Let
H
be a complex algebraic group of finite type.
Let
X
be an algebraic
H
-stack over
\mathbb{C}
of finite type.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\ic}(H\backslash X,\mathbbm{k})
and let
\pi:X\to H\backslash X
be the quotient map.
We can then consider the action map
a:H\times X\to X
.
A basic property of equivariant sheaves states that there is an isomorphism

a^{*}\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}\cong\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{H}\boxtimes\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}

One can think of this condition as a weak equivariance condition. Informally, equivariant sheaves are eigen-objects for
a^{*}
with eigenvalue
\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{H}
.
The basic premise with twisted equivariant sheaves is that one could replace the eigenvalue with any multiplicative sheaf on
H
.

The theory of twisted equivariant sheaves is well-developed in the triangulated category setting in
[
LY20
]
and
[
Gou21
]
. In the
\infty
-category setting for

D
-modules this has been detailed in
[
Gai20
]
, and in the
\infty
-category setting for étale constructible sheaves the theory is contained in
[
Ete23
]
.
We follow Gaitsgory and Eteve’s formulation to define twisted equivariant sheaves in our setting. The main benefit in their approach is that it allows for additional
flexibility in the types of multiplicative local systems allowed.
Unlike constructible sheaves on schemes and algebraic stacks, twisted equivariant
sheaves will not satisfy a 6-functor formalism in the usual sense.
The main obstacle encountered is that our category of correspondences in this setting lacks a terminal object.

The motivation of the definition comes from our reformulation in Proposition
A.3.10
.
Recall that for an algebraic
H
-stack
X\in\Stk_{\mathbb{C}}
, we had a
D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})
-action on
D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
denoted by
\star
which made
D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
into a

D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})
-module.

Definition A.4.1
.

A rank one local system
\mathcal{L}\in D_{c}(H,\mathbbm{k})
is
multiplicative
if it is equipped with the following data:

(1)

A trivialization at
1\in H
, i.e., an isomorphism
1^{*}\mathcal{L}\cong\mathbbm{k}
,

(2)

An isomorphism
m^{*}\mathcal{L}\cong\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}
where
m:H\times H\to H
is the multiplication map such that the restriction at
(1,1)\in H\times H
gives a compatible isomorphism with (1).

We denote the set of all multiplicative local systems by
\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k})
.

Lemma A.4.2
.

Let
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k})
. Let
\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}\to\mathbbm{k}
be a ring homomorphism. Then
\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(\mathcal{L})\in\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Note that the trivialization condition ensures that
\mathcal{L}
is locally free. As a result,
\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(\mathcal{L})
is a local system.
The result then follows from extension of scalars commuting with sheaf functors.
∎

A.4.3.
Six-Functors

Fix a multiplicative local system
\mathcal{L}\in D_{\cons}(H,\mathbbm{k})
.
We define a new action of
D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})
-action on
D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
denoted by
\star^{\mathcal{L}}
obtained by twisting by
\mathcal{L}
. Precisely, we define

\mathcal{F}\star^{\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{G}=(\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}^{-1})\star\mathcal{G}

where
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})
and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
. This action makes
D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
into a
D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})
-module.
We will write
D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}}}{{\curvearrowright}}D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
to indicate this action.

Definition A.4.4
.

The category of
ind-constructible
(H,\mathcal{L})
-equivariant sheaves on
X
is defined to be

D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\coloneq D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})^{(H,\mathcal{L})}

i.e., the
H
-invariants of sheaves on
X
with respect to the
\mathcal{L}
-twisted action.

As in the equivariant setting, we get a pair of adjoint functors,

\Av_{H,\mathcal{L}!}:D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})\leftrightarrows D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k}):\For_{H,\mathcal{L}}

We define the subcategory of
constructible
(H,\mathcal{L})
-equivariant sheaves on
X
as the full subcategory of
D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of
sheaves
\mathcal{F}
such that
\For_{H,\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})\in D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})
.

Definition A.4.5
.

A
twisted stack
is a triple
(X,H,\mathcal{L})
where
H
is an algebraic group,
X\in\Stk_{\mathbb{C}}
is an
H
-stack, and
\mathcal{L}
is a multiplicative rank one local
system on
H
.
A morphism of twisted stacks
\tilde{f}:(X^{\prime},H^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})\to(X,H,\mathcal{L})
consists of a triple
(f,\varphi,\theta)
where
f:X^{\prime}\to X
is a representable morphism of
stacks,
\varphi:H^{\prime}\to H
is a morphism of algebraic groups, and
\theta:\varphi^{*}\mathcal{L}\to\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is a morphism of local systems such that

(1)

f
is
\varphi
-equivariant, i.e., the following diagram commutes
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\prime}\times X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi\times f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where
a:H\times X\to X
and
a^{\prime}:H^{\prime}\times X^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}
are the action maps.

(2)

\theta:\varphi^{*}\mathcal{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is an isomorphism of local systems.

We will frequently denote a twisted stack by
H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X
. If
\tilde{f}=(f,\varphi,\theta)
is a morphism of twisted stacks, we will often abuse notation and
write
f
to refer to the whole triple.
We denote the category of twisted stacks by
\TwStk_{\mathbb{C}}
.

Lemma A.4.6
.

The category of twisted stacks
\TwStk_{\mathbb{C}}
admits all pullbacks.

Proof.

Consider the diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{2}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}X_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{1}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{1}}X_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{f}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{f}_{1}}

Our goal is to construct the pullback of
\tilde{f}_{1}
and
\tilde{f}_{2}
.
Consider the cartesian squares
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{1}\times_{H}H_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}\times_{X}X_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}

Note that
H_{1}\times_{H}H_{2}
is an algebraic group. We then can construct an action of
H_{1}\times_{H}H_{2}
on
X_{1}\times_{X}X_{2}
via the commutative diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{1}\times_{H}H_{2}\times X_{1}\times_{X}X_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{2}\times X_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}\times_{X}X_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{1}\times X_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}

where
a_{1}:H_{1}\times X_{1}\to X_{1}
,
a_{2}:H_{2}\times X_{2}\to X_{2}
, and
a:H\times X\to X
denote the action maps. The existence of
a^{\prime}
follows from the
universal property for pullbacks.
It is easy to check that
a^{\prime}:H_{1}\times_{H}H_{2}\times X_{1}\times_{X}X_{2}\to X_{1}\times_{X}X_{2}
defines an
H_{1}\times_{H}H_{2}
-action on
X_{1}\times_{X}X_{2}
.
Let
\mathcal{L}=\pr_{1}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1}\cong\pr_{2}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{2}
where
\pr_{i}:H_{1}\times_{H}H_{2}\to H_{i}
are the projection maps for
i=1,2
.
Therefore,
(X_{1}\times_{X}X_{2},H_{1}\times_{H}H_{2},\mathcal{L})
is a twisted stack. Since
X_{1}\times_{X}X_{2}
and
H_{1}\times_{H}H_{2}
are pullbacks, it is easy to check that

(X_{1}\times_{X}X_{2},H_{1}\times_{H}H_{2},\mathcal{L})
is a pullback of
\tilde{f}_{1}
and
\tilde{f}_{2}
.
∎

Let
E
be the class of morphisms in
\TwStk_{\mathbb{C}}
consisting of morphisms
\tilde{f}=(f,\varphi,\theta):H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X^{\prime}\to H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X
such
that
\varphi
is an isomorphism of algebraic groups.
Note that the category of twisted stacks
\TwStk_{\mathbb{C}}
is not finitely complete.
In particular, it does not have a terminal object.
Nonetheless, the category of correspondences
\Corr(\TwStk_{\mathbb{C}},E)
is still well-defined. Moreover, the Cartesian product equips it with the structure of a monoidal
category (without unit).

Lemma A.4.7
.

Let
f:H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X^{\prime}\to H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X
be a morphism of twisted stacks.
The functor
\varphi^{*}:D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\ic}(H^{\prime},\mathbbm{k})
gives rise to a
D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})
action on
D_{\ic}(X^{\prime},\mathbbm{k})
.
The functor
f^{*}:D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\ic}(X^{\prime},\mathbbm{k})
is
D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})
-equivariant with respect to the
\mathcal{L}
-twisted
D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})
-action.

Consider the category
\LMod_{\BiAlg}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})
of left modules over bialgebras in
\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L}
.
The objects of
\LMod_{\BiAlg}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})
consists of pairs
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C})
where
\mathcal{A}\in\BiAlg(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})
is a bialgebra and
\mathcal{C}\in\LMod_{\mathcal{A}}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})
is a left module over
\mathcal{A}
(with respect to just the algebra structure).
The external tensor product of modules gives rise to functors

(A.4.1)

\LMod_{\mathcal{A}}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})\otimes\LMod_{\mathcal{B}}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})\to\LMod_{\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})

which makes
\LMod_{\BiAlg}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})
into a monoidal category.

Let
\mathcal{A}\in\BiAlg(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})
. The counit for
\mathcal{A}
gives an action of
\mathcal{A}
on
D(\mathbbm{k})
.
There is a
coinvariants
functor

\Coinv_{\mathcal{A}}:\LMod_{\mathcal{A}}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})\to\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L}

informally given on objects by
\Coinv_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{C}=D(\mathbbm{k})\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{C}
.
If
\Phi:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is a bialgebra morphism, then there is an induced functor

\For^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{B}}:\Coinv_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{C})\to\Coinv_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{C})

This allows us to define a functor

\Coinv:\LMod_{\BiAlg}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})\to\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L}

defined by
\Coinv(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C})=\Coinv_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{C})
and on morphisms by

\Coinv(\mathcal{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\to}}\mathcal{D},\mathcal{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Phi}}{{\to}}\mathcal{B})=\For_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathcal{B}}\Coinv_{\mathcal{A}}(F).

In fact, since (
A.4.1
) is an equivalence of categories
[
GR17
, Proposition 8.5.4]
, the coinvariants functor is monoidal.

Proposition A.4.8
.

There exists a six-functor formalism

D_{\ic}(-,\mathbbm{k}):\Corr(\TwStk_{\mathbb{C}},E)\to\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L}

satisfying

D_{\ic}(-,\mathbbm{k})(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X)=D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k}).

Proof.

We can equip
D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})
with the structure of a coalgebra by the pullback maps

e^{*}:D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\ic}(\pt,\mathbbm{k})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptm^{*}:D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\ic}(H\times H,\mathbbm{k})

where
e
is the unit map and
m
is the multiplication map for
H
. This coalgebra structure along with
!
-convolution make
D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})
into a bialgebra.

By Lemma
A.4.7
, there is a lax-monoidal
13
13

13

This should be interpreted as a lax-monoidal functor without any unit constraint.
functor

\Corr(\TwStk_{\mathbb{C}},E)\to\LMod_{\BiAlg}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})\hskip 28.45274pt(H,X)\mapsto(D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}}}{{\curvearrowright}}D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k}))

making the following diagram commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Corr(\TwStk_{\mathbb{C}},E)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\LMod_{\BiAlg}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Corr(\Stk)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L}}

where the vertical maps are the obvious projections. We can then define
D_{\ic}(-,\mathbbm{k}):\Corr(\TwStk_{\mathbb{C}},E)\to\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L}
as the composition

\Corr(\TwStk_{\mathbb{C}},E)\to\LMod_{\BiAlg}(\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Coinv}}{{\to}}\textnormal{Pr}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{L}.

∎

One aspect lacking from Proposition
A.4.8
is the proper pushforward of morphisms of twisted stacks of the form
f:H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X\to H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X
when
H\not\cong H^{\prime}
.
Nonetheless, it is often the case that the pullback functor
f^{*}
admits a left adjoint. For example, if the morphism
H^{\prime}\to H
is smooth, then
f^{*}
admits a left adjoint.
We will often abuse notation and write
f_{!}
for this left adjoint.

The categories
D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
are not monoidal unless
\mathcal{L}=\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{H}
– a facet of the category of twisted stacks not admitting a terminal object.
Nonetheless, we can define a tensor product of sheaves.
There are morphisms of twisted stacks

\Delta_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}:H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X\to(H\times H)\backslash_{\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X\times X.

We then define the
tensor product
of twisted equivariant sheaves as the bifunctor given by the composition

\displaystyle\otimes^{L}:D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\otimes D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X,\mathbbm{k})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}D_{\ic}((H\times H)\backslash_{\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X\times X,\mathbbm{k})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{*}}}{{\to}}D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X,\mathbbm{k}).

These bifunctors satisfy variants of the associativity and unit axioms.

The tensor product of sheaves admits a right adjoint,

\textbf{R}\mathcal{H}om:D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})^{\op}\otimes D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{-1}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X,\mathbbm{k}),

called the
sheaf-Hom functor
for twisted equivariant sheaves. Let
\omega_{H\backslash X}
denote the dualizing complex on
H\backslash X
.
We can then define a
Verdier duality
functor

\mathbb{D}:D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})^{\op}\to D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{-1}}X,\mathbbm{k})

by
\mathbb{D}=\textbf{R}\mathcal{H}om(-,\omega_{H\backslash X})
.

Let
\mathbbm{k}\to\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}
be a ring homomorphism.
The extension of scalars functor
\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(-):D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\ic}(H,\mathbbm{k}^{\prime})
is an algebra morphism. As a result, after taking coinvariants there are induced functors

\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(-):D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}(\mathcal{L})}X,\mathbbm{k}^{\prime})

which we also call
extension of scalars
.
Since extension of scalars commutes with the six-functor formalism of algebraic stacks, it is easy to deduce that it will commute with all six sheaf operations and
Verdier duality for twisted equivariant sheaves as well.

Definition A.4.9
.

Let
H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X
be a twisted stack.
A sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
is said to be
constructible
if
\For_{H,\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})
.
We denote by
D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
the full subcategory of
D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of constructible sheaves.

Remark A.4.10
.

(1)

There are forgetful functors
\For_{H,\mathcal{L}}:D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
given by evaluation on 0-simplices.
By construction, these forgetful functors commute with the six sheaf operations and extension of scalars.

(2)

By (1), there is a six-functor formalism

\Corr(\TwStk_{\mathbb{C}},E)\to\infty\textnormal{-}\Cat_{\mathbbm{k}}\hskip 28.45274ptH\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X\mapsto D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})

given by restricting the six-functor formalism of Proposition
A.4.8
to constructible sheaves.
Moreover, extension of scalars preserves constructibility.

A.4.11.
Averaging and Forgetful Functors

Let
\varphi:H^{\prime}\to H
be a morphism of algebraic groups. Let
X
be an algebraic
H
-stack of finite type and
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k})
.
The map
\overline{\varphi}:H^{\prime}\backslash_{\varphi^{*}\mathcal{L}}X\to H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X
given by
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\id}}{{\to}}X
and
\varphi:H^{\prime}\to H
gives rise to sheaf functors

\overline{\varphi}^{*}:D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\leftrightarrows D_{\ic}(H^{\prime}\backslash_{\varphi^{*}\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k}):\overline{\varphi}_{*}.

We think of these functors as variants of equivariance forgetful and averaging functors.
It will be useful to give an explicit description of these functors and applying
\For_{H,\mathcal{L}}
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
.
It can be easily checked from the definitions that
\For_{H,\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{F}
and
\For_{H^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\overline{\varphi}^{*}\mathcal{F}
are naturally isomorphic.

Let
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\ic}(H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X,\mathbbm{k})
.
The sheaf
\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{G}
can be regarded as an
(H\times H\times H^{\prime},\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{-1}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
-equivariant sheaf on
H\times X
with
respect to the action of
H\times H\times H^{\prime}
on
H\times X
via
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\cdot(g,x)=(h_{1}gh_{2}^{-1},h_{3}x)
.
Let
\tau:H\times H^{\prime}\to H\times H\times H^{\prime}
be given by
\tau(h_{1},h_{2})=(h_{1},\varphi(h_{2}),h_{2})
.
This gives rise to a morphism of twisted algebraic stacks

\overline{\tau}:(H\times H^{\prime})\backslash_{\mathcal{L}\times\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{H^{\prime}}}H\times X\to(H\times H\times H^{\prime})\backslash_{\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{-1}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}H\times X

where
H\times H^{\prime}
acts on
H\times X
via
(h_{1},h_{2})\cdot(g,x)=(h_{1}gh_{2}^{-1},h_{2}x)
.
Via the quotient equivalence, the sheaf
\overline{\tau}^{*}(\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{G})\in D_{\ic}\left((H\times H^{\prime})\backslash_{\mathcal{L}\times\underline{\mathbbm{k}}_{H^{\prime}}}H\times X,\mathbbm{k}\right)
can be regarded as a sheaf, denoted
\mathcal{L}\tilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{G}
, in
D_{\ic}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}H\times^{H^{\prime}}X,\mathbbm{k})
.
Let
a:H\times^{H^{\prime}}X\to X
denote the action map.
It follows from the Čech complex description of categorical coinvariants that
\overline{\varphi}_{*}\mathcal{G}\cong a_{*}(\mathcal{L}\tilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{G})
.
In particular, the underlying sheaf of
\For_{H,\mathcal{L}}(\overline{\varphi}_{*}\mathcal{G})
is given by
a_{*}\For_{H,\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{L}\tilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{G})
.

When
\overline{\varphi}:H^{\prime}\hookrightarrow H
is an embedding, we will occasionally write
\Av_{(H^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})*}^{(H,\mathcal{L})}\coloneq\overline{\varphi}_{*}
and

\For_{(H^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})}^{(H,\mathcal{L})}\coloneq\overline{\varphi}^{*}
.

A.5.
Twisted Equivariant Sheaves on Ind-Algebraic Stacks

We extend the theory of twisted equivariant sheaves to ind-algebraic stacks of ind-finite type with an action of an algebraic group of pro-finite type.

A.5.1.
Ind-Algebraic Stacks

We introduce the theory of ind-algebraic stacks. The geometry reviewed in this section is essentially well-known (c.f.
[
Eme20
]
).

A stack
X
is called a
(strict) ind-algebraic stack
if there exists a directed system
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of algebraic stacks and an isomorphism of stacks
X\cong\underrightarrow{\lim}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{i}}}X_{i}
where the transition maps
X_{i}\to X_{i^{\prime}}
for
i\leq i^{\prime}
are closed embeddings.
We further say that
X
is of
ind-finite type
if the
X_{i}
’s can be chosen such that
X_{i}
is of finite type for all
i\in I
.

A morphism
f:X\cong\underrightarrow{\lim}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{i\in I}}}X_{i}\to\underrightarrow{\lim}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{j\in J}}}Y_{j}\cong Y
is said to be
bounded
if
for all
j\in J
, the pre-image
f^{-1}(Y_{j})
is contained in
X_{i}
for some
i\in I
, and the restriction of
f
to
Y_{i}
is representable of finite type.
We will abuse notation and write
\IndStk_{\mathbb{C}}
for the subcategory of stacks consisting of ind-algebraic stacks of ind-finite type with bounded morphisms.

Given
\underrightarrow{\lim}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{i\in I}}}X_{i}\cong X\in\IndStk_{\mathbb{C}}
and
i\leq i^{\prime}
, write
\kappa_{i,i^{\prime}}:X_{i}\to X_{i^{\prime}}
for the transition map.
We can then define the derived category of constructible sheaves on
X
as the colimit of categories

D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})\coloneq\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{i\in I}}}D_{\cons}(X_{i},\mathbbm{k})

where the transition maps are given by pushforward maps
\kappa_{i,i^{\prime}!}
for
i\leq i^{\prime}
.
Likewise, the category of ind-constructible sheaves on
X
, denoted
D_{\ic}(X,\mathbbm{k})
, can be obtained by replacing
D_{\cons}(X_{i},\mathbbm{k})
with
D_{\ic}(X_{i},\mathbbm{k})
in the
above definition.
It follows from a standard argument that
D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})
does not depend on the choice of directed system
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in I}
.

Let
f:X\cong\underrightarrow{\lim}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{i\in I}}}X_{i}\to\underrightarrow{\lim}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{j\in J}}}Y_{j}\cong Y
be a bounded morphism of ind-algebraic
stacks of ind-finite type.
We can define sheaf functors

f_{!}:D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}(Y,\mathbbm{k})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptf^{*}:D_{\cons}(Y,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k}).

The boundedness constraint ensures that there are representable morphisms
f_{i,j}:X_{i}\to Y_{j}
for all
j\in J
and
i\in I
satisfying
X_{i}\subseteq f^{-1}(Y_{j})

given by the restriction to
f
.
We can then define
f_{!}
and
f^{*}
component-wise. Explicitly, if
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(X_{i},\mathbbm{k})
, we can find some
j\in J
such that
f(X_{i})\subset Y_{j}
. Then
f_{!}\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(Y_{j},\mathbbm{k})
can be viewed as an object in
D_{\cons}(Y,\mathbbm{k})
.
Similarly, if
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\cons}(Y_{j},\mathbbm{k})
, the
i
-th component of
f^{*}\mathcal{G}
is given by
f_{i,j}^{*}\mathcal{G}
for all
i\in I
satisfying
X_{i}\subseteq f^{-1}(Y_{j})
.

A.5.2.
Algebraic Groups of Pro-Finite Type

An algebraic group
H
is said to be of
pro-finite type
if there exists a projective system
\{H_{j}\}_{j\in J}
of algebraic groups of finite type whose
transition maps
H_{j}\to H_{j^{\prime}}
for
j\geq j^{\prime}
are smooth surjections, along with an isomorphism of algebraic groups
H\cong\underleftarrow{\lim}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{j}}}H_{j}
.
We further say that
H
is
predominantly pro-unipotent
if it contains a subgroup of finite codimension which is pro-unipotent, i.e., for sufficiently large
j
,

\ker(H\to H_{j})
is an inverse limit of unipotent algebraic groups of finite type.

Let
H\cong\underleftarrow{\lim}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{j\in J}}}H_{j}
be an algebraic group of pro-finite type.
We can define the derived category of
∗
-constructible sheaves on
H
as the inductive limit,

D_{\cons}^{*}(H,\mathbbm{k})=\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{j\in J}}}D_{\cons}(H_{j},\mathbbm{k})

where the transitions maps are given by pullbacks along the transition maps
\pi_{j,j^{\prime}}:H_{j}\to H_{j^{\prime}}
where
j\geq j^{\prime}
.

A
multiplicative local system

\mathcal{L}\in D_{\cons}^{*}(H,\mathbbm{k})
consists of a collection of multiplicative local systems
\mathcal{L}_{j}\in\Loc(H_{j},\mathbbm{k})
such that for

j^{\prime}
sufficiently large and
j\geq j^{\prime}
, there are compatible isomorphisms
\pi_{j,j^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{j^{\prime}}\cong\mathcal{L}_{j}
.
We denote the set of multiplicative local systems on
H
by
\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k})
.

Let
f:H=\underleftarrow{\lim}_{j\in J}H_{j}\to G=\underleftarrow{\lim}_{i\in I}G_{i}
be a morphism of algebraic groups of pro-finite type.
For all
i\in I
, there exists some
j\in J
, such that
f
factors through a group homomorphism
f_{i,j}:H_{j}\to G_{i}
.
We can then define the pullback functor

f^{*}:D_{\cons}^{*}(G,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}^{*}(H,\mathbbm{k})

by
f^{*}\mathcal{F}_{i}\coloneq f_{i,j}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{j}
.
Similarly, given
∗
-sheaves
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
on
H
, we can define a
∗
-sheaf
\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{G}
by
(\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{G})_{i}\coloneq\mathcal{F}_{i}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{G}_{i}
.
This gives rise to functor
\otimes^{L}:D_{\cons}^{*}(H,\mathbbm{k})\otimes D_{\cons}^{*}(H,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}^{*}(H,\mathbbm{k})
.
Similarly, we can define the external tensor product,

\boxtimes:D_{\cons}^{*}(H,\mathbbm{k})\times D_{\cons}^{*}(G,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}^{*}(H\times G,\mathbbm{k})\hskip 28.45274pt\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{G}\coloneq\pr_{H}^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\pr_{G}^{*}\mathcal{G},

where
\pr_{H}:H\times G\to H
and
\pr_{G}:H\times G\to G
are the obvious projection maps.

It is easy to check from definitions that if
m:H\times H\to H
is the multiplication map for an algebraic group
H
of pro-finite type and
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k})
,
then
m^{*}\mathcal{L}\cong\mathcal{L}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}
.

A.5.3.
Twisted Equivariant Sheaves

If
H
is a predominantly pro-unipotent algebraic group, then we can write
H\cong\underleftarrow{\lim}_{j\in J}H_{j}
where
H_{j}=H/K_{j}
with
K_{j}\trianglelefteq K_{j^{\prime}}
for
j\leq j^{\prime}
,
K_{j}
unipotent for large
j
, and such that
K_{j}
has finite codimension in
H
.
If
H
acts on an ind-algebraic stack
X\cong\underrightarrow{\lim}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{i\in I}}}X_{i}
, then we say that this action is
compatible
if for all
i
, there
exists some
i^{\prime}\geq i
and
j\in J
such that the algebraic stack
X_{i^{\prime}}
is
H
-stable and such that
K_{j}
acts on
X_{i^{\prime}}
trivially.
If
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k})
, we will call the triple
H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X\coloneq(X,H,\mathcal{L})
a
twisted ind-algebraic stack
.

Fix
i\in I
. We can then find some
j\in J
such that
K_{j}
acts trivially on
X_{i}
. We define

D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X_{i},\mathbbm{k})\coloneq D_{\cons}(H_{j}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{j}}X_{i},\mathbbm{k}).

If
j^{\prime}\geq j
, then the inflation functor
D_{\cons}(H_{j}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{j}}X_{i},\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}(H_{j^{\prime}}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{j^{\prime}}}X_{i},\mathbbm{k})
is an equivalence of categories.
As a result,
D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X_{i},\mathbbm{k})
is independent of the choice of
j\in J
.

We can now define the derived category of
(H,\mathcal{L})
-equivariant sheaves on
X
as the direct limit,

D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})=\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{i\in I}}}D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X_{i},\mathbbm{k}),

where the transitions maps are given by pushforwards along the maps
H_{j}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{j}}X_{i}\to H_{j}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{j}}X_{i^{\prime}}
where
i\leq i^{\prime}
and
j\in J
is
taken sufficiently large.

There are variations of the sheaf functors for sheaves on twisted ind-algebraic stacks. Namely, if
f:H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X\to H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X^{\prime}
is a
bounded morphism of twisted ind-algebraic stacks, there are sheaf functors
f_{*}
and
f^{*}
.
Moreover, when
H=H^{\prime}
and the component map
\varphi:H\to H^{\prime}
is the identity map, we can also have sheaf functors
f_{!}
and
f^{!}
.
We will not explicitly detail the construction of the six-functors nor assemble them into an abstract six-functor formalism.
The key observation is that just as in the untwisted setting, sheaves on ind-algebraic stacks are entirely determined by their behavior on twisted algebraic stacks of finite type.
As a result, all the properties of constructible sheaves on twisted algebraic stacks transfer without issue.

Let
X\cong\underrightarrow{\lim}_{i\in I}X_{i}
be an ind-algebraic stack of ind-finite type with a compatible action of an algebraic group of pro-finite type
H=\underleftarrow{\lim}_{j\in J}H_{j}
.
Let
H^{\prime}=\underleftarrow{\lim}_{k\in K}H_{k}^{\prime}
be another algebraic group of pro-finite type along with a morphism
\varphi:H^{\prime}\to H
of algebraic groups.
We can define the
induction space
as the ind-algebraic stack
H^{\prime}\times^{H}X\coloneq\underrightarrow{\lim}H_{k}^{\prime}\times^{H_{j}}X_{i}
where
i\in I,j\in K
, and

k\in K
are taken to satisfy that
X_{i}
is
H_{j}
-stable and
\varphi(H_{k})\subseteq H_{i}
.
Suppose that
H
and
H^{\prime}
are predominantly unipotent. Let
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k})
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=\varphi^{*}\mathcal{L}
. For each
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(H\backslash X,\mathbbm{k})
,
the twisted external product construction of §
A.4
gives rise to a sheaf
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\tilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}H^{\prime}\times^{H}X,\mathbbm{k})
.
Via
*
-pushforward along the action map
a:H^{\prime}\times^{H}X\to X
, we recover the classical description of the
*
-averaging functor
D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}(H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X,\mathbbm{k})
.

A.6.
Perverse Sheaves

In this section, we will construct various abelian categories of perverse sheaves for constructible on the various geometric settings we have considered thus far.

Let
S\in\Sch_{\mathbb{C}}
. We can define the
perverse
t
-structure
on
D_{\cons}(S,\mathbbm{k})
by

{}^{p}D_{\cons}(S,\mathbbm{k})^{\leq 0}=\{\mathcal{F}\in D_{c}(S,\mathbbm{k})\mid\text{for all }i,\text{ we have }\dim\supp H^{i}(\mathcal{F})\leq-i\},

The nonnegative portion of the perverse
t
-structure
{}^{p}D_{\cons}(S,\mathbbm{k})^{\geq 0}
is characterized by
\Hom(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}[-1])=0
for all
\mathcal{F}\in{}^{p}D_{\cons}(S,\mathbbm{k})^{\leq 0}
and
\mathcal{G}\in{}^{p}D_{\cons}(S,\mathbbm{k})^{\geq 0}
.
The fact that this defines a
t
-structure is a standard argument (see
[
Ach21
, §3.1]
).
We can then define the
perverse sheaves
on
S
, denoted
\Perv(S,\mathbbm{k})
, as the heart of this
t
-structure.

Now let
X\in\Stk_{\mathbb{C}}
and let
\pi:U\to X
be a smooth atlas in
\Sch_{/X}
of relative dimension
d
.
The
perverse
t
-structure
on
D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})
is given by

{}^{p}D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})^{\leq 0}=\left\{\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})\mid\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}[d]\in{}^{p}D_{\cons}(S,\mathbbm{k})^{\leq 0}\right\},

{}^{p}D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})^{\geq 0}=\left\{\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})\mid\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}[d]\in{}^{p}D_{\cons}(S,\mathbbm{k})^{\geq 0}\right\},

It is routine to then check that this is a
t
-structure and the resulting category of perverse sheaves on
X
, denoted
\Perv(X,\mathbbm{k})
, has the following description,

\Perv(X,\mathbbm{k})=\left\{\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})\mid\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}[d]\in\Perv(S,\mathbbm{k})\right\}.

Moreover, a routine argument using functorality of pullbacks shows that the perverse
t
-structure on
D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})
does not depend on the choice of smooth atlas.
If
X\cong\underleftarrow{\lim}_{i\in I}X_{i}
is instead an ind-algebraic stack, we define the perverse
t
-structure on
D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})
as the inductive limit of

t
-structures on the
D_{\cons}(X_{i},\mathbbm{k})
’s.

Let
H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X
be a twisted stack.
Recall, that there is a forgetful functor
\For_{H,\mathcal{L}}:D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\to D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})
.
The
perverse
t
-structure
on
D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
is given by

{}^{p}D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})^{\leq 0}=\left\{\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\mid\For_{H,\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})[\dim H]\in{}^{p}D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})^{\leq 0}\right\},

{}^{p}D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})^{\geq 0}=\left\{\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\mid\For_{H,\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})[\dim H]\in{}^{p}D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})^{\geq 0}\right\},

As before, we obtain an abelian category
(H,\mathcal{L})
-equivariant perverse sheaves on
X
, denoted
\Perv(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
, which has the following description,

\Perv(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})=\left\{\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\mid\For_{H,\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{F})[\dim H]\in\Perv(X,\mathbbm{k})\right\}.

If
H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X
is instead a twisted ind-algebraic stack, we define the perverse
t
-structure on
D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
as the inductive
limit of
t
-structures on the
D_{\cons}(H_{j}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}_{j}}X_{i},\mathbbm{k})
’s.

It is easy to check that when
\mathbbm{k}
is a field, the Verdier duality functor
\mathbb{D}
restricts to a functor

\mathbb{D}:\Perv(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})^{\op}\to\Perv(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{-1}}X,\mathbbm{k}).

A.7.
Fixed Stratifications

It will frequently be helpful to consider constructible sheaves with respect to a fixed stratification. We will always assume that our stratifications of ind-algebraic
stack are by algebraic stacks of finite type.

Definition A.7.1
.

Let
X=\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}X_{\lambda}
be a stratification of an (ind)-algebraic stack
X
.
We denote by
D_{\Lambda}(X,\mathbbm{k})
the full subcategory of
D_{\cons}(X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of sheaves which are constructible with respect to
\{X_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
.

Further, suppose that
X
is an (ind-)algebraic
H
-stack and that
\{X_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
is an
H
-stable stratification.
Let
\mathcal{L}\in\Ch(H,\mathbbm{k})
. We denote by
D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
the full subcategory of
D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of
sheaves
\mathcal{F}
such that
\For_{H,\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{F}\in D_{\Lambda}(X,\mathbbm{k})
.

Definition A.7.2
.

Let
f:H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X\to H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X^{\prime}
be a morphism of twisted stacks.
Let
X=\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}X_{\lambda}
be an
H
-stable stratification of
X
. Let
X^{\prime}=\bigsqcup_{\mu\in\Lambda^{\prime}}X_{\mu}
be an
H^{\prime}
-stable
stratification of
X^{\prime}
.
Suppose that
f
further satisfies the following two conditions:

(1)

for all
\lambda\in\Lambda
,
f^{-1}(X_{\lambda})=\bigcup_{\mu\in\Lambda^{\prime}_{\lambda}}X_{\mu}^{\prime}
where
\Lambda_{\lambda}^{\prime}\subset\Lambda^{\prime}
;

(2)

for each
\mu\in\Lambda_{\lambda}^{\prime}
,
f:X_{\mu}^{\prime}\to X_{\lambda}
is smooth.

In this case, we will call
f
is
stratified
.

Definition A.7.3
.

Let
H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X
be a twisted (ind-)algebraic stack.
We define a full subcategory
\Loc_{\textnormal{f}}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
of
D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
consisting of sheaves
\mathcal{F}
such that

\For_{H,\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{F}\in\Loc_{\textnormal{f}}(X,\mathbbm{k})
.
The objects in
\Loc_{\textnormal{f}}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
are called the
(H,\mathcal{L})
-equivariant local systems on
X
.

Suppose
X
is of finite type.
The category
D_{(X)}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
of
(H,\mathcal{L})
-equivariant constructible sheaves with respect to the trivial stratification consists of sheaves

\mathcal{F}\in D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
such that
{}^{p}H^{i}(\mathcal{F})[-\dim X_{\lambda}+\dim H]\in\Loc_{\textnormal{f}}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
for all

i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
It is convenient to define a functor

{}^{p}H^{i}:D_{(X)}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})\to\Loc(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k}),

\mathcal{F}\mapsto{}^{p}H^{i}(\mathcal{F})[-\dim X_{\lambda}+\dim H].

The following lemma follows from recollement.

Lemma A.7.4
.

Let
X
be an algebraic
H
-stack with finitely many
H
-orbits.
Let
X=\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}X_{\lambda}
be the stratification of
X
by the
H
-orbits. We denote by
j_{\lambda}:X_{\lambda}\to X
the obvious inclusion maps.
The category
D_{\Lambda}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
is the smallest stable subcategory of
D_{\cons}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X,\mathbbm{k})
generated by
j_{\lambda!}\mathcal{K}
(alternatively,
j_{\lambda*}\mathcal{K}
) for all
\mathcal{K}\in\Loc_{\textnormal{f}}(H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X_{\lambda},\mathbbm{k})
and
\lambda\in\Lambda
.

Remark A.7.5
.

Let
f:H\backslash_{\mathcal{L}}X\to H^{\prime}\backslash_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}X^{\prime}
be a morphism of twisted stacks.
Assume that there are finitely many
H
-orbits on
X
and
H^{\prime}
-orbits on
X^{\prime}
. We take the stratifications on
X
and
X^{\prime}
with respect to the orbits.
In this case, if
f
is stratified, then
f_{!}
,
f_{*}
,
f^{!}
, and
f^{*}
preserve the fixed stratifications.
